# CLAUDE.md — claude-plugins-validation (CPV)

**Authoritative structure + ops reference for this project. KEEP IT CURRENT:**
whenever you add/remove a command, agent, skill, or script, or change the
menu/publish/validate flow, update the matching line here in the SAME change.
The counts below are load-bearing — README, the menu doc, and
`test_*_preflight` tests assert against reality, so a stale count here means a
stale count everywhere.

## What CPV is

A Claude Code **plugin validator**: it validates plugins / skills / agents /
marketplaces for structural correctness (severity-counted, binary
VALID/INVALID) and security (a skillaudit engine + RC rules + external
scanners). It is itself a plugin, published to the `emasoft-plugins`
marketplace. Repo: `github.com/Emasoft/claude-plugins-validation`.

`cpv_ship_canon.py` the three ship-only-binary integrity detectors (#185 §5/§6/§7) — extracted-repo rot with an OPT-IN network probe that reports UNVERIFIED rather than clean when it cannot run, licence/NOTICE gating for shipped binaries, and a build-graph ROLE classifier that catches the `dist/`-bundle-from-`.ts`-source case the extension-keyed rule is structurally blind to; **Single-visible-command invariant:** only `/cpv-main-menu` is meant to be the
user's entry point; the specialist skills load via `cpv-the-skills-menu`
(itself `user-invocable: true` as the agent-facing router). Adding a new
top-level visible command breaks this — don't, unless explicitly asked.

## Menu architecture (the thing that surprised me — now documented)

`/cpv-main-menu` (`commands/cpv-main-menu.md`) renders an interactive numbered
menu of every CPV capability. It runs **INLINE in the main session** — no
subagent fork (the old `context: fork` + `agent: cpv-main-menu-agent` was a
stalled-migration leftover the CA-07 rule flags: it cold-re-primed the prompt
cache for zero benefit, since the Stop hook already renders the menu — removed
when the command was de-forked). It does NOT print menus inline — it routes
through the **external `claude-menu-system` plugin's Stop hook**, which emits the
menu post-turn via `systemMessage` (zero context cost). The menu TREE + per-leaf
execution recipes live in `skills/cpv-main-menu-skill/references/menu-tree.md`
(read this to know what every menu leaf dispatches to). Main Claude queues menu
specs + parses integer/letter choices inline; heavy work is dispatched to the
specialised work agents (cpv-plugin-fixer-agent/cpv-plugin-creator-agent/
cpv-plugin-diagnoser-agent/cpv-marketplace-fixer-agent/
cpv-cache-optimizer-agent/cpv-plugin-devitalizer-agent/
cpv-plugin-leaks-preventer-agent/etc.).

**Dependency:** the menu needs `claude-menu-system` (separate plugin,
`github.com/Emasoft/claude-menu-system`, dev checkout at
`~/Code/claude-menu-system`, has its own `scripts/publish.py`). CPV's menu is
only as bug-free as that hook.

## Key scripts

- **Validators** (`scripts/validate_*.py`, 25): `validate_plugin.py` (the
  user-facing plugin gate), `validate_skill.py` /
  `validate_skill_comprehensive.py`, `validate_marketplace.py`,
  `validate_security.py` (RC security rules), `validate_agent.py`,
  `validate_command.py`, `validate_hook*.py`, `validate_mcp.py`, …
- **Security engine:** `cpv_skillaudit_native.py` (the in-process skillaudit
  scanner + per-language context classifiers `_skillaudit_*_context.py`),
  `scripts/rules/skillaudit_patterns.json` (the rule catalog),
  `cpv_taint_engine.py`, `cpv_binary_scanner.py`.
- **Launcher:** `scripts/remote_validation.py` — the ONLY correct way to run a
  validator (running `validate_*.py` directly errors). See "Canonical
  commands".
- **Publisher:** `scripts/publish.py` — the 12-gate canonical pipeline (clean
  tree → tests → validate → bump → manifest → commit → tag → push → release).
  The ONLY thing allowed to push (a pre-push hook blocks all other pushes).
- **Self-hash manifest:** `scripts/_plugin_compute_hashes.py` regenerates
  `.plugin-self-hashes.json` / `.cpv-self-hashes.json` (the SHA list CPV's
  self-scan skips so it doesn't flag its OWN rule-pattern strings).

## Canonical commands (memorise these)

```bash
# Validate a plugin (NEVER run validate_plugin.py directly):
PLUGIN_SKIP_GITHUB_INTEGRITY=1 CLAUDE_PRIVATE_USERNAMES="$(whoami)" \
  uv run --with pyyaml python scripts/remote_validation.py plugin <path> --strict -o /tmp/out.txt
#   modes: plugin | security | skill | marketplace
#   CPV_SCAN_CACHE=0 → bypass the skillaudit result cache (MANDATORY when
#   testing a classifier change at the same version — the cache is keyed on
#   (content, catalog, __version__, ext), NOT classifier code).

# Self-validate CPV (after editing CPV's own files, regen the manifest FIRST):
uv run python scripts/_plugin_compute_hashes.py
PLUGIN_SKIP_GITHUB_INTEGRITY=1 CLAUDE_PRIVATE_USERNAMES="$(whoami)" \
  uv run --with pyyaml python scripts/remote_validation.py plugin . --strict -o /tmp/selfval.txt

# Test suite — run it SERIALLY before publishing. CI and release both shard it
# into a pytest-split MATRIX (count-based round-robin); each shard is serial +
# no-re2, so serial-pollution and no-re2 ReDoS stay catchable WITHIN a shard,
# but a polluter in one shard whose victim lands in another is invisible to all
# of them (TRDD-K7P2XR4Q).
# This local run is a CONVENTION, not a gate: publish.py's Gate 2 runs
# `pytest -n auto --dist=worksteal --maxfail=1` and the Gate 3c fork-parity
# probe runs `pytest -n auto --dist=worksteal`, so NOTHING in the publish path
# enforces a serial run. The enforced cross-shard ordering gate is
# .github/workflows/serial-suite.yml (weekly + workflow_dispatch).
uv run pytest -p no:cacheprovider -o addopts="" -q tests/

# Publish (bumps version, runs every gate, pushes, releases):
uv run python scripts/publish.py --patch   # | --minor | --major
```

## Load-bearing invariants / gotchas (the hard-won ones)

1. **Regen the manifest after editing CPV's own files**, before trusting a
   self-validate — else CPV flags its own changed files (their SHA no longer
   matches the skip list). New/changed files → `_plugin_compute_hashes.py`.
2. **`CPV_SCAN_CACHE=0` for same-version classifier testing** (see above).
3. **Serial suite before publish** — CI and release both shard into a
   pytest-split serial matrix (count-based round-robin; each shard serial +
   no-re2), which leaves cross-shard ordering pollution uncovered
   (TRDD-K7P2XR4Q). Running the suite serially locally is a CONVENTION and NOT
   a gate — every pytest invocation in the publish path is xdist (Gate 2:
   `-n auto --dist=worksteal --maxfail=1`; Gate 3c fork-parity: same). The
   enforced cross-shard ordering gate is `.github/workflows/serial-suite.yml`
   (weekly + dispatch). This entry used to call the local run "the
   authoritative cross-shard ordering gate", which was false in the direction
   that matters: it described a guarantee nobody was providing, so nobody went
   looking for the gap.
4. **Never suppress a security rule / never relax `--strict`** — the only
   auto-clear is provably-inert data (a pattern string in a rule-table never
   reaching a sink) or a non-instruction-loadable / comment / doc context;
   any possible execution path BLOCKS. Every FP fix is two-sided tested (the
   FP clears AND a real-threat sibling still fires).
5. **GitHub comments self-id:** begin every issue/PR/release comment with
   `This is the Claude responsible for the claude-plugins-validation project.`
6. **GitHub push-protection** rejects AWS-key-shaped test fixtures (`AKIA…`) —
   use a generic uppercase blob.
7. **re2 compatibility:** `skillaudit_patterns.json` regexes must be
   re2-safe (no lookbehind/lookahead) — CI runs without google-re2.
8. **Reports** go under `reports/` and `reports_dev/` (BOTH gitignored).

## Open issues snapshot (update as they close)

**v5.14.1 — v5.14.0's own timeout mark leaked across a cache hit, found by attacking the release rather than the code.** `_SCAN_INCOMPLETE` is keyed by scanner NAME, not by tree, and `_run_scanner_with_cache` returns early on a HIT **without ever calling `check_trufflehog`** — where the clear lived. The marketplace path calls `validate_security()` serially in ONE process, which is exactly where a warm cache is likeliest: plugin A times out (mark set, cache write correctly skipped), plugin B hits its cache, and B's row prints `[!!] TIMEOUT — scan INCOMPLETE` for a clean cached result. Direction is false-POSITIVE, so it does not reopen #218 — but it is the same *stale state read as a current fact* shape the whole 5.14.0 release is about, and it makes the brand-new TIMEOUT row untrustworthy exactly where it would first be seen. Cross-process is safe (the set starts empty), so a single fresh scan never shows it and the suite stayed green. The clear now lives in the wrapper that DECIDES whether the scanner runs, ahead of the hit-return, and covers the `shutil.which` early return too; the in-scanner clear stays for direct callers (`remote_validation.py security`, tests) and is idempotent. **Three of 5.14.0's own new tests were the vacuous-source-pin shape it had just criticised in three older ones** — `assert "if scan_was_incomplete(...):" in SRC` passes with the branch body replaced by `pass`, and a magic 1200-char window straddles unrelated code the moment the stub grows. Both now read the AST: the guard must be an `if` that RETURNS, positioned before `cache.put`, and the `scanner_argv` literal is parsed rather than sliced, with an `assert stub` anti-vacuity control. 13177 pass, 3 skip.

**v5.14.1 — v5.14.0's own new TIMEOUT mark leaked across a cache hit.** Found by an adversarial review of the RELEASE's claims rather than its code, which is why the green suite had missed it: `_SCAN_INCOMPLETE` is keyed by scanner NAME, not by tree, and `_run_scanner_with_cache` returns early on a HIT without ever calling `check_trufflehog` — where the clear lived. So in one process: plugin A times out (mark set, cache write correctly skipped), plugin B hits its cache, and B's step table reports a `TIMEOUT` that never happened. The marketplace path calls `validate_security()` serially in one process, which is exactly where a warm cache is likeliest — cross-process is unaffected, so no single fresh scan can show it. Direction is a false POSITIVE, so #218 does not reopen; the shape is identical though — stale state read as a current fact, the very thing v5.14.0 was about. Clear now lives in the wrapper that decides whether the scanner runs at all (the one in `check_trufflehog` stays, idempotent, for direct callers like `remote_validation.py security`). Two of the release's own new tests were the failure they criticised: `test_incomplete_scan_is_not_cached` pinned a SPELLING (passes with the branch body replaced by `pass`) and now pins the ORDERING guard→return→`cache.put`; the `scanner_argv` assertion sliced a magic 1200-char window and now reads the list out of the AST. The workflow-guard test retyped the shell snippet instead of lifting it from the emitted YAML — a proxy in templates full of `{{{{ }}}}` brace-doubling — and now runs the emitted line itself.

**v5.14.0 — four ways a run reported something it had not done (issues #213 / #214 / #215 / #216, plus a regression in v5.13.0's own fix; assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **v5.13.0's #219 fix was silently defeatable, and the code said so out loud.** `_run_scanner_with_cache` keys on a *curated copy* of trufflehog's flags whose own comment reads "a flag drift inside `check_trufflehog` should manually update this list". v5.13.0 added `--results=…filtered_unverified` and `--exclude-paths` to the real argv and did not touch the copy — and `scanner_version` tracks the trufflehog BINARY, not CPV — so any host holding a pre-fix `0 findings` entry for an unchanged tree kept replaying it. The fix shipped, the release was green, and on a warm cache nothing changed. Fixed by making the flag a single `TRUFFLEHOG_RESULTS_FLAG` constant both sites consume; a test pins that the literal has exactly ONE spelling in the module, so the two cannot drift again.
— **#218 defect 3 — the coverage table printed a timed-out scan as `[OK] RAN`.** Defect 2 taught `check_trufflehog` to return a MAJOR instead of 0, but the step row's status was a hardcoded `"RAN"` string, so the human-facing table still showed the abort with the clean-run glyph. New `TIMEOUT` status (`[!!]`, never `[OK]`), and the incomplete run is no longer WRITTEN TO THE CACHE — freezing "we never finished looking" into an entry every later warm run replays is the same false negative, made permanent.
— **#215 — a `tests/unit/` layout read as "no tests" and blocked the push mid-way.** `glob("test_*.py")` is top-level-only; pytest collects nested suites fine, so a green 205-test repo was refused at G4. Now `rglob`, in the emitted canon AND in CPV's own `publish.py` copy. The two workflow templates guarded on `ls tests/test_*.py`, which is both non-recursive and a lying probe in its own right (an unmatched glob is passed through literally) — now `find … -print -quit`. The zero-tests block is unweakened: pytest's exit 5 still catches an empty collection.
— **#213 — the fail-closed pre-push hook allowed a push it had scanned 0 bytes of.** `git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/some-name` feeds a remote branch name with no local ref; trufflehog logs "unable to resolve ref", scans nothing, and exits 0 — and `--fail` only fires on a secret FOUND, so *not looking* and *looking and finding nothing* were the same answer. The hook now resolves the ref itself and blocks. Test-fixture note: the existing suite's `_init_repo` never created the `feat` branch it fed the hook, so the "clean feature push is allowed" test had been exercising this exact unsafe shape all along; the fixture now creates the ref and a new negative control asserts the unresolvable shape still blocks.
— **#214 — a false provenance header stopped two sessions from grepping for the write site.** The emitted hook claimed publish.py rewrites it; in a consumer repo only `cpv-standardize` does. A header that answers convincingly is worse than none.
— **#216 — the retry pushed the previous attempt's tag, and the DEPENDENCY tag was the unguarded one.** The release tag already had a fail-closed drift check; `{name}--vX.Y.Z` — the tag Claude Code actually resolves dependencies against — was a bare "already exists locally — skipping". So after a publish that died between tagging and push, the fix commit that made the retry pass landed on the branch but outside the tag dependents install. Both now route through one `_ensure_tag_at_head`, which moves a tag ONLY on the remote's positive "unpushed", refuses a published one, and refuses an unreachable remote (an unanswered `ls-remote` is not consent).
— 15 new two-sided tests + 3 repaired source-pins; 13175 pass, 3 skip. The three pre-existing tests that went red were all source-TEXT pins asserting a spelling rather than a behaviour — one of them (`" -n " not in cmd`) matched POSIX test's string-is-non-empty operator in the new `find` guard, which is [[lesson-greedy-match-truncates-right-context]] in a different costume: judge the pytest INVOCATION line, not the whole shell block.

**v5.13.0 — a leak now BLOCKS the publish, in CPV's own pipeline AND in the canon it emits (issues #217 / #218 / #219; assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **The canonical pipeline documented a pre-push secret scan it never implemented.** `grep -icE "trufflehog|gitleaks|secret[_ -]scan" scripts/publish.py` returned **0**; a `tskey-auth-…` literal consequently reached a plugin's `main`, GitHub's own secret scanning flagged it, and the alert sat open for **85 days** while every local gate passed. New **Gate 3d** (`stage_secret_scan`) runs in the READ-ONLY parallel block, strictly BEFORE the bump/commit/tag/push, so a detected credential aborts with the tree untouched — a gate that fired after the push could not un-publish anything. It reports and BLOCKS; **redaction is deliberately not its job** and it names `cpv-plugin-leaks-preventer-agent` instead, because a publish gate that edited source to make itself pass would be rewriting security-relevant code with nobody reviewing the change, and a gate that can silence itself is not a gate.
— **The gate could not pass on CPV's own tree until it ARMED the self-scan exemption, and the handoff's diagnosis of why was WRONG.** Three hypotheses were recorded and all three are disproven: findings do carry a file path (the field is `.file`), the survivors were not cache/`.git` artifacts, and it was not an absolute-vs-relative path bug. The recorded conclusion — that `_set_cpv_self_scan` *refuses* to trust the local manifest on an unpublished tree, making Gate 3d structurally unable to pass before a push — is also **FALSE**: measured, it arms cleanly (`is_running_cpv=True` → branch 1, 1163 manifest entries) and `cpv_self_scan_skip` returns **True** for all three files it was reported to reject. The real defect was mundane and entirely mine: **Gate 3d never called `_set_cpv_self_scan` at all**, so the exemption was inert and CPV's own detector regexes read as credentials ("scanning the scanner"). The earlier False readings came from a **stale manifest** — the recorded *regen hashes LAST* invariant, hit again by editing `validate_security.py` mid-session. The arming suppresses nothing the plugin gate does not already suppress (every skip still requires a per-file SHA match; `is_cpv_self_scan` is False for every other plugin) and the **DISARM is a mandatory `finally`**, because the flag is a module GLOBAL and a left-armed flag would let a later scan read stale state.
— **#218a — the gitignore set now reaches the SCANNER, not just the results.** The post-filter dropped gitignored findings only AFTER trufflehog had walked every gitignored byte (the reporter measured 3.4 GB across 434 ignored zips scanned and then discarded, which is also what drove the timeout). `_trufflehog_exclude_args` writes an `--exclude-paths` file up-front. **The anchoring is the part that was wrong first:** trufflehog matches those regexes against **ABSOLUTE** paths, so a plugin-relative anchor (`^downloads_dev`) matches nothing while looking correct — caught only by running the real binary both ways, not by reading the code. The same ordering defect was fixed in the skillaudit walker: `_file_passes_scan_filters` now rejects on path/stat BEFORE `_file_is_scannable` (which reads content) and `_is_self_artifact_copy` (which hashes), so a 5 MB gitignored file is neither scanned NOR opened.
— **#218b — a timed-out scan is no longer a clean one.** It returned 0, making "we never finished looking" and "we looked and found nothing" the same answer. Now MAJOR, not WARNING, because WARNING never blocks `--strict`.
— **#219 — CPV was blind to every secret trufflehog cannot verify over the network.** The default result set omits `filtered_unverified`, the bucket an expired, revoked, entropy-filtered or unreachable-service credential lands in. Reproduced live: the default flags report **0 findings** on a file where the widened set reports the key. A committed credential is a leak whether or not a runner can reach its API.
— **An evasion route in my own fix, found by testing the case nobody asks for.** With the exclude-args correct, a **TRACKED**+gitignored secret still went unreported — `.gitignore` does not untrack an already-tracked file, so such a file still ships in `git archive`, and `git add payload` + `.gitignore payload` made it invisible to every external scanner. The exclude-args were git-accurate (they correctly produced an empty set); the drop was one layer down in the pattern-based `_external_finding_is_gitignored`, whose own docstring named this as accepted-and-backstopped follow-up. The backstop is real and was verified firing (`check_tracked_gitignored_files` → blocking MAJOR), but relying on a *different* gate to cover a hole inside the secret gate is how a hole survives a refactor, so it is now closed at the source: a **tracked path is never suppressed**, whatever `.gitignore` says. Fail-open by construction — outside a git repo the tracked set is empty, so the pattern verdict stands unchanged and the issue-#67 noise suppression is preserved. Mutation-proven: removing the check fails exactly the evasion control while all 15 siblings pass, which is why the untracked-suppression sibling is not enough on its own.
— **trufflehog is a DEPENDENCY, not a precondition (owner directive).** `check_trufflehog` reports a missing binary as a non-blocking WARNING and returns 0 — right for a general validate run, and wrong for a release gate, where it meant publishing having scanned nothing: the exact silent pass #217 was filed about, sitting inside the fix for #217. Gate 3d now INSTALLS it via the existing `cpv_install_scanners.ensure_trufflehog` (brew → `go install`, already in CPV's standard scanner set) and blocks only on a FAILED install — including when the installer itself raises.
— **The emitted canon gets the same gate, and it is the higher-impact instance.** The pipeline CPV scaffolds secret-scans **FEATURE branches only** — a default-branch/tag push is gated on `publish.py` ancestry instead — so the ONE path that reaches users was the one path never scanned. New emitted **`[G3s]`** runs between validation and tests, installs trufflehog the same way, asks for every result bucket, excludes only untracked-ignored paths via `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard --directory` (so a tracked+gitignored file stays scanned there too), and treats both a findings exit (183) and any other non-zero as BLOCKING. The standard body carries a byte-identity guarantee, so its pinning tests were updated deliberately; the parallel-preflight invariant test caught the new gate as unstubbed, which is precisely what it exists to do.
— **v5.13.1 — v5.13.0 shipped with RED CI, and both failures were the same shape: a test that depended on the DEV BOX having trufflehog.** Gate 3d probes for the binary and installs-or-blocks BEFORE it reaches the scan, so on a bare runner (no trufflehog, no brew, no go) it returned 1. Two populations hit it. (1) `test_publish_parallel_preflight.py` has FOUR tests that call `run_preflight_parallel`, and only ONE carried the "is every gate stubbed?" assertion — the other three stubbed 4 of 6 gates and had been running the REAL `stage_ci_preflight` all along, harmlessly, until a gate with an environment dependency joined the pool. **A guard written inside one test protects only the test that remembers it**, so the fix is structural: one `_GATE_SYMBOLS` map plus a `_patch_all_gates` helper every test routes through, with the invariant asserted on each call — mutation-proven, dropping `secret_scan` from the map now fails all four. (2) My own new gate tests assumed the binary was present; they now take an explicit `trufflehog_present` fixture, so they measure the gate instead of the runner. Verified by SIMULATING the bare runner (hiding trufflehog/brew/go from `shutil.which`) — 37/37 pass, where the pre-fix code reproduces CI's exact `rc=1` and message. The local suite was green all three times it ran; only CI could see this, which is the [[ci-vs-local-google-re2-serial]] class again.
— 27 two-sided tests, every suppression assertion paired with a positive control. Verified end-to-end through the REAL binary on real git fixtures rather than from the code: CPV's own tree passes, a planted Slack token blocks, an untracked+gitignored secret is skipped, a tracked+gitignored one blocks. version 5.13.0, test files 491. [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.13.0 (part 2) — `/cpv-validate-plugin-folder`: one read-only full-coverage scan of a folder OR a remote repo (assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **A composition, not a new validator.** `plugin` mode already runs the CA-01..CA-07 cache audit and an execution-class security SUBSET, but NOT the full `security` mode where secret/leak detection lives — so "full scan" is `plugin --strict` + `security`, and the new `scripts/cpv_validate_plugin_folder.py` orchestrates existing modes rather than re-implementing a rule. It runs them as SUBPROCESSES through `remote_validation.py` on purpose: that launcher's whole job is stopping the TARGET's `pyproject.toml` / a stale `cpv_validation_common.py` from hijacking CPV's imports, and importing the validators directly here would silently drop that guarantee on exactly the foreign trees this is pointed at.
— **`cache` mode is deliberately NEVER run, and the reason is that BOTH branches are wrong.** It is plugin-scoped — `validate_cache.py:1023` raises CRITICAL on any folder without `plugin.json` — so on a skill or unknown folder it reports an INAPPLICABILITY as a finding (measured: an empty directory scored `cache: CRITICAL` in the first draft). Where a `plugin.json` does exist, `plugin` mode already runs the same audit in-process, so a separate pass is a duplicate. A test pins its absence for all four shapes with that reason attached, because the reflex on a later read is to "finish the coverage" and add it back.
— **The remote gate is asymmetric on purpose.** A GitHub/GitLab URL or `owner/repo` slug is cloned to a temp sandbox, scanned, and deleted on EVERY exit path (a `finally`, not a trailing call — the early-return error paths are exactly where a trailing cleanup is skipped). A CLONED repo MUST be a plugin or a skill or it errors; a LOCAL folder still scans whatever shape it has. Scanning an arbitrary repository would report security findings about code that was never a Claude Code component, which reads as a verdict on something it is not — but a local work-in-progress tree is a legitimate target, so the gate binds only the remote half.
— **The GitLab fix landed in the SHARED fetch path, and one regex could not do both forges.** `cpv_pre_install_scan._fetch_target` already normalised GitHub URLs; a `gitlab.com` URL fell through to `_curl_download` and was fetched as a single FILE rather than cloned — so `/cpv-pre-install-scan` had the same defect. Fixed there rather than duplicated here. GitHub's repo is always path segment 2 (anything after is a `/tree/…` web view); GitLab namespaces nest arbitrarily deep and its web views are marked `/-/`, so the repo is the LAST segment before `/-/`. A first attempt used one regex for both and silently turned `gitlab.com/group/sub/repo` into `…/group/sub.git` — **caught by my own expectation table, not by the code review**, which is why the 9-case table is now a regression test.
— **The severity aggregation cannot use `max()`.** The exit vocabulary is 0..4 with **1** the MOST severe, so ranking on the raw codes inverts the verdict; `_SEVERITY_RANK` maps them explicitly and a test pins the ordering.
— **The skill is `user-invocable: true` as a deliberate exception.** The convention is `false` (the specialist skills load via `cpv-the-skills-menu`); the 14 already-`true` skills ARE the exceptions, not evidence the convention lapsed — a reading I got backwards mid-session and corrected. Body is minimal: it runs one script and relays the output.
— **`PLUGIN_SKIP_GITHUB_INTEGRITY` is NOT set anywhere in the shipped skill.** The integrity check compares against the manifest published on GitHub, so brand-new uncommitted files legitimately mismatch until released (its own documented scenario 2) — a dev-time condition, never a reason to ship a disabled tamper check.
— Verified by MUTATION rather than a green run: re-adding `cache` to `modes_for` fails 3 tests, disabling the remote gate fails its own test. 32 new tests; the 71 tests touching `cpv_pre_install_scan` still pass, so the GitLab change regresses nothing.

**v5.12.0 — CC spec sync v2.1.248, and a docstring that cited a doc line for a claim the doc does not make (assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **The window really was one release, and establishing that is most of the work.** v5.11.0 covered v2.1.247, so v2.1.248 was the whole new surface. Four constants set-diffed against the WHOLE live raw-docs tables (never the changelog window — that method lags by releases, the v5.11.0 `SendFeedback` lesson): `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS` **0 gaps** (180 keys vs 157 doc top-level headings), `VALID_TOOLS`/`CANONICAL_TOOLS` **0 gaps**, `BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS` **0 gaps** (`/usage-credits` already present). Two real additions, each confirmed absent at HEAD first (`git show HEAD:<file> | grep -c` → 0): agent frontmatter `experimental.cacheTtl`, and the `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTRICTED` env var. That one was challenged as a possible **INVERTED** permission — a host launch-mode var admitted to a plugin allowlist, i.e. permitting what CPV ought to flag. Settled by reading the constant's own docstring: `VALID_PLUGIN_ENV_VARS` is an **FP-suppression** list covering "Claude Code-configuration env vars that plugin authors may legitimately document or reference", so a host var is exactly what belongs on it. A good hypothesis, resolved the other way by the source.
— **`desktopSessionCleanupPeriodDays` is in the changelog and NOT in the live docs, so it was not added.** Recording the refusal matters as much as the additions: the changelog is an announcement, the docs are the spec CPV validates against, and adding a key on changelog authority alone would put CPV ahead of the surface plugin authors can actually read.
— **The reverse of v5.11.0: here the CHANGELOG leads the reference table.** v2.1.248 adds `experimental.cacheTtl` to "agent frontmatter", but plugins-reference.md's plugin-shipped field sentence still does not carry it. So `experimental` went into `KNOWN_FRONTMATTER_FIELDS` (project/user agents) and deliberately NOT into `PLUGIN_SHIPPED_AGENT_ALLOWED_FIELDS` — a plugin agent declaring it gets the MINOR drift nudge rather than silence. A guardrail test pins that choice, because the reflex on the next sync is to "finish the job" and add it. Value set is CLOSED to `5m`/`1h` (MAJOR otherwise), matching the isolation/effort enum severity; the accept test ships with a reject sibling, since an acceptance assertion passes vacuously without one.
— **The finding worth keeping is a FALSE CITATION that had been load-bearing.** `validate_plugin_shipped_allowed_fields`'s docstring said *"Per plugins-reference.md:70, plugin-shipped agents accept exactly these 15 fields"*. The doc says **11**, at line **68** — it omits `system-prompt`, `context`, `initialPrompt`, `agent`. Measured against the live raw docs: `system-prompt` and `context` appear in NEITHER plugins-reference.md nor sub-agents.md; `initialPrompt` only in sub-agents.md. **The gap was recorded, not closed** — those four entered in the v2.1.79–86 alignment and nothing shows CC stopped loading them, so narrowing to 11 would emit a MINOR on agents that load fine: a false positive, which costs more than the silence it buys. The citation was removed rather than re-pointed, with a "do not restore it" note. **A docstring that cites a file:line for a count nobody re-derives is a claim that decays silently** — it survived every prior sync because each one read the changelog window, not the sentence the docstring pointed at.
— **Three adversarial passes over the agent-frontmatter diff, and the finding is the METHOD, not the answer.** The answer never moved (0 gaps; `color` confirmed `in KNOWN_FRONTMATTER_FIELDS`, not in `PLUGIN_SHIPPED_AGENT_ALLOWED_FIELDS`, so the `experimental` split has precedent). What moved three times was the evidence, and each defect was one stage downstream of the last — **the proxy relocates, it does not vanish when you fix where you last found it**:
  1. **Extraction keyed on expectation.** The first "whole-table" grep was half a hand-written alternation of field names ALREADY KNOWN; a field CPV has never heard of cannot match it. Same blind spot as the changelog-keyed diff it was meant to replace.
  2. **CPV side counted substrings, not members.** `git show HEAD:… | grep -c '"field",'` — its own output disproves it (`tools`=4, `model`=3). Fixed with imported constants and real `in` tests.
  3. **Classification keyed on expectation.** With the extraction open-ended, the 17 unknown tokens were then sorted into "noise" BY NAME — and a real field called `dontAsk` or `local` is exactly what a reader expecting `name`/`model`/`tools` discards.
  The revert itself rests on a fact the compression dropped and this restores: **`cacheTtl` appears in NEITHER plugins-reference.md NOR sub-agents.md — zero hits** — so no doc sanctions it anywhere, and the plugin-shipped list cannot carry a field no doc names, whichever way the partial-sentence question falls. Doc-side extraction yielded **n=33** tokens. Settled two independent ways: every one of the 17 attributed to its section (permission modes L500-505, memory scopes L562-564, subagent hook events L681-683, project hook events L713-714, fork keybindings L1080-1082), AND positionally — the frontmatter table is **L288-305, 16 rows**, the 17 tokens are all L500+, disjoint by ~190 lines, so no heading inference is needed. `/tmp/sa.md` byte-compared to a live re-fetch (104943=104943) first, since a stale cached doc reads identically to a current one.
— **REACH, stated then MEASURED — the humble version was also wrong.** Recorded first as "tables only; prose/YAML fields invisible", which sounded careful and was false: sub-agents.md's canonical field list IS a table. The extraction covers the authoritative surface in full; the real residual is narrower — **a future field added OUTSIDE that table**. Under-claiming reach mis-states it exactly as an over-claim does.
— **`.cpv-self-hashes.json` carries a `computed_at` timestamp, so `git diff` on it is NOT a staleness check.** Every recompute dirties the file whether or not a single hash changed; measured here — the only delta across a regen was `computed_at`, every other key identical. Consequences, both real: a reader who treats git-dirty as "stale manifest" gets a **false positive 100% of the time** (an adversarial reviewer proposed exactly that check and would have mis-fired on it), and a stray regen leaves the tree permanently dirty until `git checkout`, which `publish.py` Gate 1 then REFUSES on — verified by reading it, not inferred from a log line: it collects `git status --porcelain`, and on anything dirty prints "Uncommitted changes detected" and `return 1`. The sole carve-out is a lone added/modified `uv.lock`, which it auto-commits; two dirty hash files are not that case. **This sentence was first written from an inferred log line** — the `plugins-reference.md:70` false-citation defect reappearing in the very bullet list that documents removing it, which is how cheap it is to cite a mechanism by name without opening it. To actually compare, diff the parsed JSON with `computed_at` excluded.
— **Drop the uniqueness guard exactly when the blast radius grows, and that is the edit that bites.** Every CLAUDE.md edit this session used `assert s.count(old) == 1` before replacing — except the compression, which used bare `s.index()` twice and overwrote the span between. `.index()` returns the FIRST match and raises only when a string is wholly absent, so in a long accreted document of near-identical version sections, mis-anchoring silently deletes whatever lies between. It did not fire (the commit removed exactly the two intended lines, checked with `git diff … | grep '^-[^-]'`), but **neither downstream green could have caught it if it had**: `_plugin_compute_hashes.py` hashes a damaged file just as happily, and `remote_validation --strict` validates plugin STRUCTURE — a CLAUDE.md that lost four version sections still returns 0/0/0/0. Appending cannot destroy; a span-replace can, and it is a BLIND write — it cannot report what it removed. Guards, in order: keep the count assert, and token-set diff the old span against the new (that is what caught the genuinely dropped `VALID_PLUGIN_ENV_VARS` / `INVERTED` content). Word count is not a control — 1120→906 fits a clean compression and an over-delete partly refilled equally well.
— **A worker's edit was reverted after checking its source.** The fan-out added `experimental` to the plugin-shipped set and bumped the docstring's count 15→16. Curling the doc showed line 68 lists 11 without it. A subagent's report is not evidence — the same rule that governs its findings governs its edits.

**v5.11.0 — CC spec sync v2.1.247, and a tool the DOCS had carried for nine releases before the changelog announced it (assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **Two additions, each confirmed absent at HEAD before being called a gap** (`git show HEAD:<file> | grep -c` → 0): the `SendFeedback` tool (`VALID_TOOLS` + `CANONICAL_TOOLS`) and the `feedbackDrafts` settings key.
— **The finding worth keeping is that `SendFeedback` is ALSO a miss of the two PRIOR windows.** The changelog announces it at v2.1.247; the tools-reference row says *"Requires Claude Code v2.1.238 or later"* — so it had been a documented tool through the v2.1.236–240 and v2.1.241–246 syncs, both of which recorded `VALID_TOOLS` as having "no doc-side additions". Both were right about their own window's changelog and wrong about the table. **A tool-name diff keyed on the CHANGELOG window lags the docs by releases**, which is exactly why the set-diff runs over the WHOLE live table each window rather than over the window's new rows — the method caught what a window-scoped read could not.
— **Most of the settings diff was the nested-key class again, and saying so is part of the result.** 216 `### <key>` headings against 175 known keys looks like a 41-key gap; every one but `feedbackDrafts` is a DOTTED nested key (`sandbox.*`, `permissions.*`, `worktree.*`, `policyHelper.*`) that `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS` models by design — a bare leaf entry there would excuse a genuine typo written at top level (the v5.1.0 `strictAllowlist` ruling, unchanged).
— **The `BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS` pass was VACUOUS on the first attempt, and an adversarial review caught it from the transcript rather than the result.** The extraction's `sed` errored (`unknown option to 's'`), so the TABLE-ROW half contributed zero lines and all 114 names came from inline `` `/name` `` code spans in `commands.md` ALONE — `skills.md`, which v5.10.0's own note records as holding half the folded-in `slash-commands.md` content, never contributed. Against CPV's 143 names a 114-name SUBSET yields `docs − cpv = []` by construction: **an empty diff from a half-broken anchor is indistinguishable from a clean pass**, which is the same vacuous-anchor shape as v5.8.0's moved settings table and v5.10.0's colliding basenames — three windows running, in three different places. Re-extracted as the UNION of both readings over BOTH files — every backticked `/token` on a table ROW (so the Aliases column is covered) plus every inline `` `/name` `` span, since neither extraction is a superset of the other and a built-in documented only in `skills.md` PROSE is exactly what a rows-only read would miss: and with the closing backtick DROPPED, because requiring it makes every argument form invisible — `` `/claude-api cost-optimize` `` yields nothing under a `` `/name` `` anchor, and that is this very window's changelog item: **148 doc names against CPV's 143, and the diff is NON-EMPTY — seventeen names, every one triaged to its own line and every one a user-authored EXAMPLE, an argument-form illustration (`/fix-issue 123`, `/migrate-component SearchBar …`, `/write-tests`), or a URL-path artifact**. The claim that actually matters settles in one line and is recorded rather than re-eyeballed: every command in the `commands.md` TABLE column (130 names) is already in CPV's set, so the prose-only residue is examples by construction (`/name` and `/skill-name` are literal placeholders in the `disable-model-invocation` / `user-invocable` frontmatter rows; `/commit`, `/deploy`, `/send-slack-message`, `/summarize-changes`, `/legacy-system-context` are the docs' own "a skill you might write" examples; `/apps`, `/my-plugin`, `/fancy`, `/deploy-staging` come from nested-dir and plugin-namespacing illustrations). The conclusion survived; the measurement did not, and only the non-empty result is evidence of either. **A `sed` error printed above a plausible number is the whole failure: the number was read, the error was not.** The 12 CPV-only names stay retained per the MultiEdit/SlashCommand precedent — a name leaving the doc table is not proof the runtime dropped it, and the error is asymmetric.
— **Verified as needing NO change rather than assumed:** `BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS` (per the re-run above — `/claude-api cost-optimize` is a SUBCOMMAND of a bundled skill, not a new slash name), the `^###` settings anchor (challenged rather than re-asserted: 21 `####` headings, **zero** of them a bare `` #### `key` ``, and `llms.txt` describes the page as carrying "an index of every key" — the docs' own completeness claim, which is the affirmative evidence an eyeball is not), and no markdown consumer of the settings-key set to drift — **and that sweep had to be redone, because the first one could not have found what it was looking for**: it grepped four named dirs for three keys added LAST window, so a doc enumerating the LONG-STANDING keys would miss every needle and read as clean, `2>/dev/null` hid a mistyped dir, and `$?` after a pipe measured `head` rather than `grep` (three proxy reads in one line — the same shape as the `sed`, one layer down). Re-run whole-repo on long-standing needles it exits 0 with ten paths — a live anchor — but every live hit was `disableAllHooks`, a **hooks.json** field rather than a settings key, which is no evidence either way: a doc enumerating the keys authors actually touch contains none of five hand-picked needles and never appears at all. Settled by making the needle THE SET ITSELF — all 176 known keys, generic English collisions (`env`, `model`, `hooks`, `permissions`) removed — over every `.md` in the repo: **126 files carry ≥5 distinct keys, so the check can fail**, and the maximum outside `docs_dev/` is EIGHT (`plugin-structure-fixes.md`), all plugin-topical (`mcpServers`, `pluginConfigs`, `extraKnownMarketplaces`, `lspServers`). Nothing enumerates the set; the ≥28-key files are all dated `docs_dev/` audit records, gitignored and unshipped. **It is the WHOLE-REPO census that earns that conclusion, not a directory list** — the shipping population is *tracked and not gitignored* (the v5.4.0 rule), which is wider than `skills/ agents/ commands/ references/` and includes `hooks/`, `scripts/`, `README.md`, this file, and every tracked `design/` doc. Also `VALID_PLUGIN_ENV_VARS` (`CLAUDE_CODE_SEND_FEEDBACK` is a per-session user toggle; that set is a curated plugin-authoring allowlist, not an env-vars.md catalog — the same call as the v5.8.0 ruling).
— 6 two-sided tests (`tests/test_cc_spec_sync_2_1_247.py`), each acceptance paired with a positive control (a bogus tool still flagged through the REAL `validate_tools_field`; three near-miss settings spellings still unknown). The `VALID_TOOLS − {Task} ⊆ CANONICAL_TOOLS` structural test already pinned by v5.7.0 means a one-sided tool add fails the suite. version 5.11.0, scripts 141, test files 489. [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.10.0 — CC spec sync v2.1.241–246, and the fetch that lost 9 pages to basename collisions (assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **Three real gaps, each confirmed absent at HEAD before being called a gap** (`git show HEAD:<file>` → 0 hits): five `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS` (`modelPicker`, `promptCacheTtl`, `subagentPromptCacheTtl` from v2.1.243, plus `autoContinueAtUsageLimit` and `disableDesktopLocalSessions` accumulated); skill `license`/`compatibility`; and twenty `BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS` (one new name, `rate-limit-options`, plus nineteen documented aliases).
— **`license`/`compatibility` were recorded LAST window as "already handled" — and that was true of only ONE of two validators.** They live in `OPENSPEC_ALLOWED_FIELDS`, which `validate_skill_comprehensive` reads; `validate_skill.py` warns off `SKILL_FRONTMATTER_FIELDS` alone, and that set lacked both. So which validator you ran decided whether a spec-correct skill was clean. Probed both ways: `license: MIT` + `compatibility: needs bash` now clean, while `licence:` still WARNs — the typo detector is intact. **"Handled" is not a finding until it names WHICH code path.**
— **The measurement defect is again the finding worth keeping, and it is the same SHAPE as v5.8.0's.** The corpus is enumerated from `llms.txt` (191 English pages), but the fetch loop named each file `basename $url` — and nine URLs share a basename with a differently-scoped page (`hooks.md`, `skills.md`, `plugins.md`, `mcp.md`, `overview.md`, `permissions.md`, `quickstart.md`, `sessions.md`, `troubleshooting.md`). Each pair silently collapsed: **191 URLs → 182 files**, second fetch wins, and an overwritten page is indistinguishable from a page with nothing new. Flattening the path into the filename (`/` → `__`) restored all 191; a byte-identical-pair check over the result found **zero** collisions, which is the positive evidence that nothing fell back. Last window it was the anchor that had moved, this window the filenames that collided — **the measurement, not the diff, is what needs re-verifying each window.**
— **Most of the apparent diff was extraction noise, and saying so is part of the result.** Table-header cells (`Field`, `Result`, `Valid`), prose headings (`Disclaimer`, `Limitations`), and permission-mode/scope enum VALUES (`acceptEdits`, `plan`, `project`) all match a field-name regex. `VALID_TOOLS`, `VALID_HOOK_EVENTS`, agent frontmatter and marketplace fields had **no** doc-side additions; `archive` (v2.1.224) and `command` (v2.1.229) marketplace sources and the `DirectoryAdded` hook were already present, and are now pinned by tests so a later edit cannot quietly drop them.
— **`slash-commands.md` no longer exists** (folded into `commands.md` + `skills.md`). A hand-listed page set would have read that as a clean pass; enumerating from `llms.txt` is what caught it.

**v5.8.0 — CC spec sync v2.1.236–240, and the set-diff that had gone vacuous without saying so (assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **The window is narrow; establishing that it IS narrow is the work.** Five releases, nearly all of it runtime behaviour CPV cannot see. Checked against the RAW docs by mechanical set-diff (the recorded method — a WebFetch summary has false-negatived twice), then every candidate PROBED against the real validator before being called a gap. Seven candidates, three real, four verified-correct-as-is.
— **Both halves of the method decayed silently, and each rendered as a clean pass.** The first pass fetched **13 hand-listed doc pages**; `llms.txt` — step 1 of this repo's own recorded method, which I skipped — lists **140**. Nineteen of the unfetched pages carry v2.1.236+ text, so "the complete doc-visible delta" was a claim about a corpus never enumerated. Re-run across all 141 pages, the extra surfaces are env vars (`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL`, `MCP_DISCOVERY_CACHE*`, `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG`), the built-in `Concise` output style, and two built-in command names — **none a CPV surface** (CPV keeps no built-in-command or output-style list, and `VALID_PLUGIN_ENV_VARS` is the `${VAR}`-interpolation allowlist, not a user-env catalog). The conclusion survived; it just hadn't been earned. **Corollary now recorded: a fallback page would have shown up as MANY byte-identical fetches — there is exactly ONE pair (`slash-commands.md` == `skills.md`, a real redirect), which is what rules out silently-wrong fetches rather than assuming them away.**
— **The measurement defect is the finding worth keeping: the settings diff had silently become vacuous.** The authoritative "Available settings" table MOVED to `settings-reference.md`; `settings.md` — which the v5.7.0 pass diffed — no longer carries a single key row. So that diff was comparing CPV's 153 keys against an EMPTY set and reporting nothing new, which is indistinguishable from being in sync. Re-anchored, it surfaced **11 missing keys**: `keybindingFlavor` (v2.1.238, the one genuinely new in-window) plus ten accumulated (`diffTool`, `enableWorkflows`, `externalEditorContext`, `permissionExplainerEnabled`, `skipAutoPermissionPrompt`, `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`, `sshHostAllowlist`, `syncClaudeAiSkills`, `teammateDefaultModel`, `terminalTitleFromRename`). `teammateDefaultModel` is listed deliberately although v2.1.234 removed its `/config` row: the set is a **typo detector**, and a real-if-deprecated name is not a typo — omitting it would flag a settings file that works. **A table that matches nothing reads exactly like a table with nothing new** — the anchor, not the diff, is what needs re-verifying each window.
— **`classifierContext` (v2.1.236)** is a `PostToolUse` `hookSpecificOutput` field carrying a note about this call's result to the auto-mode classifier instead of to Claude (hooks.md:1939, example :1973). CPV emitted a NIT — which blocks `--strict` — on a spec-correct hook. Scoped to PostToolUse only: the same key on PreToolUse still draws the NIT, control-tested, because the doc puts it on one event.
— **The marketplace `headersHelper` (v2.1.238) is the one with a real cost, and the one that required a judgement.** A catalog entry may name a command Claude Code runs on install/update to mint HTTP headers (a short-lived token) for catalog and same-origin archive fetches. CPV emitted a publish-blocking **MAJOR** whose text told the author the field *"will be ignored at install time"* — false in the dangerous direction: it is honoured, and it RUNS A COMMAND. CPV was instructing authors to delete a working auth mechanism. It is **changelog-only** — plugin-marketplaces.md documents only `headers` on a settings-level url source — so this deliberately breaks the rule applied to changelog-only SETTINGS keys, and **the asymmetry is the point**: omitting a settings key leaves a typo detector merely conservative, while omitting this one actively broke valid plugins. Recognised is not unexamined (the v5.5.0 `command`-source ruling), so it is shape-checked — **reusing the `command` source's readability predicates rather than re-deriving them**, because the hazard is identical and stated by the changelog itself (the command is SHOWN, then accepted at `[y/N]`): a control character or a four-space run renders as something shorter than it is, so what the user accepts is not what runs. **Tiered one notch below `command`, on purpose:** that function MAJORs because the doc states each constraint as one Claude Code REFUSES at install; here no doc says so, so readability findings are WARNING — visible, never blocking — and only shapes that cannot be a command under any reading (non-string, empty) stay MAJOR. Asserting MAJOR without a spec would invent a gate (the v2.154.1 ruling).
— **And the comment that had been lying about the allowlist for longer than the feature has existed.** `_KNOWN_MARKETPLACE_ENTRY_FIELDS`' own comment claimed the set carried *"CPV extensions (`alwaysLoad`, `headersHelper`)"*. It never did — and neither name is a marketplace field at all: both belong to `validate_mcp.py::KNOWN_SERVER_FIELDS` (`alwaysLoad` v2.1.121, MCP `headersHelper` v2.1.85). Two allowlists cross-wired in prose, so a reader checking whether a marketplace entry already accepted `headersHelper` would have concluded yes. **A comment that asserts behaviour the code lacks is worse than no comment: it stops the next reader from checking** — the same shape as v5.4.0's drifted reserved-name copy and v5.1.3's documented-but-nonexistent parity test. Corrected to describe what the code does; `alwaysLoad` stays rejected at marketplace level.
— **Verified as needing NO change rather than assumed**, each probed: tools-reference col-1 vs `VALID_TOOLS` in sync; skill `license`/`compatibility` already in `OPENSPEC_ALLOWED_FIELDS`, **and CPV rejecting a mapping for `compatibility` is CORRECT** — skills.md:341 specifies a *string* of ≤500 chars, so the obvious "fix" would have been a regression (pinned by a test, because "environment requirements" reads like an object); `metadata.pluginRoot` (v2.1.239) handled since GAP-34, bare-name sources included; the v2.1.239 UTF-8 BOM fix already covered by `parse_frontmatter`'s BOM strip; `DirectoryAdded` and `updatedInput` already present; `extraKnownMarketplaces` entries enforce no strict key allowlist, so the documented `headers` draws no FP. **Deliberate omission, pinned with its reason:** the `<name>@synced` identity (v2.1.239) is NOT added to any reserved-marketplace-name set — it is a load-identity suffix, no doc calls it reserved, and inventing a gate the spec lacks is the failure this project already ruled against.
— **The corpus sweep's real prize was a false NEGATIVE, and it surfaced only by disproving my own grep-miss.** Having fetched all 141 pages, I checked whether `commands.md`'s v2.1.236+ rows were a CPV surface, grepped `BUILTIN_COMMAND|RESERVED_COMMAND|builtin_commands`, got nothing, and wrote off built-in command names as something CPV does not model. The constant is named **`BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS`** and `validate_command.py:706` has checked against it since v2.1.121. **A grep that misses is not an absence** — the same shape as the vacuous anchor, one layer down, and this time I was the one who committed it. Diffed properly: **53 entries against the doc's 108**, so a plugin shipping `commands/plan.md`, `run.md`, `diff.md`, `export.md`, `hooks.md`, `vim.md` … drew NO collision WARNING. That is the FN direction and the costly one: the bare `/name` resolves to the built-in, so **the plugin author's command silently never runs** — precisely what the check exists to prevent. 62 names backfilled (bundled skills exposed as commands included — they collide identically), now 108/108. The six CPV-only names are **retained, not pruned**: a name leaving the doc table is not proof the runtime dropped it, and the error is asymmetric — a stale name over-warns on a collision, a dropped live one under-warns (the MultiEdit/SlashCommand precedent). Verified end-to-end through `validate_command`, two-sided: `plan` warns, a non-built-in name is silent.
— **The top-level filter was challenged and POSITIVE-CONTROLLED rather than re-asserted.** The 11 keys were isolated with `'.' not in key` on the DISPLAYED name — an assumed format, so an adversarial review flagged it as the likely repeat of the vacuous-anchor shape: if the doc rendered a nested key as a bare leaf, a sub-key would have been promoted to top-level and would then EXCUSE a genuine typo, the exact invariant `sandbox.network.strictAllowlist` is pinned for. Settled by measurement, not argument: the anchor matches **151 bare-name rows** (not zero — the control that makes an empty result meaningful), every one anchored to its own lowercase, and all 59 dotted rows carry `parent-child` anchors. The doc never renders a nested key bare, so the filter is sound — and the v5.7.0 59-key backfill, which used the same filter, is retroactively covered.
— 22 two-sided tests, non-vacuity proven by mutation (reverting each constant fails exactly the accept-test while every control still passes). `ruff format` also normalised **four pre-existing hunks** in `validate_marketplace.py` unrelated to this change — verified pre-existing by format-checking the committed HEAD, semantically inert (quote style + line joining), kept so the file is format-clean. version 5.8.0, test files 483. **v5.7.1 — MCP discouraged-but-never-cut-off policy (owner directive 2026-08-19)**
— `validate_mcp` now emits a non-blocking INFO advisory noting MCP servers are discouraged (prefer CLI tools/skills for token economy) but NEVER a blocking finding — plugins with MCP servers remain fully valid; policy note added to `cpv-register-mcp`; rationale recorded in wikimem page `mcp-discouraged-but-never-cut-off`. No severity model change, no gate change.

**v5.7.0 — CC spec sync for the v2.1.233–235 window (raw-doc mechanical set-diff, per the recorded drift method)**
— **Tools:** `ListAgents` added to `VALID_TOOLS` (a tools-reference row since v2.1.224, surfaced by this window's set-diff); `SlashCommand`/`MCPSearch` demoted to the legacy-emits-WARNING bucket (dropped from the tools-reference table; kept in the set per the MultiEdit precedent — runtime removal unproven); `CANONICAL_TOOLS` backfilled with the 5 current rows it lacked (`Artifact`, `ListAgents`, `ReportFindings`, `SendUserFile`, `Workflow`) plus the retained-legacy names its docstring promises, and a structural test now pins `VALID_TOOLS − {Task} ⊆ CANONICAL_TOOLS`.
— **v2.1.233 todo/task-tool model gating:** `TodoWrite`+`TaskCreate/Get/List/Update` aren't provided on Opus 4.8 / Sonnet 5 / Fable 5 / Mythos 5+ without opt-in (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1`). They stay VALID (availability is a runtime/model property); `validate_agent` now emits WARNING for `TodoWrite` (disabled by default everywhere in favor of the Task tools) and INFO for the four Task tools. `TaskStop`/`TaskOutput` are NOT gated and draw no note (control-tested).
— **Settings:** `spellcheck` (v2.1.235) plus a 59-key backfill of `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS` — every key a verified row of settings.md's Available-settings table (`theme`, `verbose`, `fastMode`, `autoCompactEnabled`, `fallbackModel`, …); nested sub-keys stay excluded (control-tested). GitLab tokens, GitLab marketplace URLs, and the `additionalMarketplaces`/`allowedMarketplaces` aliases were already shipped by the v2.1.225–232 sync — re-verified, not re-implemented. Agent frontmatter (`background`/`isolation`/`initialPrompt`/`memory`) verified current; no env-var catalog work owed (CPV tracks env-vars.md only for secret names and effortLevel).

**v5.6.0 — the fleet Phase-2 hub dispatch (TRDD-BRRJK57P): four canonical cards, every finding re-verified first-hand before a line was written**
— **TRDD-QOZXF6A6 (P1, fleet-blocking):** `setup_branch_rules.py` still emitted the PRE-2026-08-13-ruling baseline shapes and its :807→UPDATE→apply chain PUT them back onto every repo touched — actively REGRESSING already-correct repos. Rebuilt to the janitor code SSOT (`branch_protection_lib.baseline_ruleset_payloads` — NEVER the machine-global prose, which still states the pre-ruling shape): history-protect carries the admin bypass (`[]` was a lock with no key on solo-owner repos; non-admins stay fully bound), pr-and-checks emits the `pull_request` rule CONDITIONALLY (solo-owner repos omit it; when present, approvals is 0 not 1 — GitHub forbids self-approval, so 1 was unsatisfiable, not strict), `required_status_checks` is OMITTED when no CI contexts are detectable (GitHub 422s an empty list and the 422 fails the WHOLE write), and `baseline-tag-protect` (tag target, deletion+update, no bypass) joins the set — pair→TRIO. Sequenced FIRST deliberately: the janitor's gate fix (TRDD-DD0M4QL7) waits on it, because a working applier aimed at the wrong payload would rewrite the fleet wrong.
— **TRDD-WC2GEDOC (P1, canonical):** the atomic release push passed `capture_output=False`, so `result.stderr` was None and the transient classifier's `if not stderr: return False` made EVERY failure permanent — the retry wrapper never retried the one call it most exists to protect (12/22 fleet copies). Fixed in CPV's own publish.py AND the emitted canon; captured stderr is echoed on success and re-raised failure so nothing git says is swallowed. Per the integrator's fleet warning (a review that refuted its own fix because four artifacts shared ONE unverified premise), the classifier fixtures are REAL captured producer output — an actual push to an unresolvable host (transient) and an actual rejected non-fast-forward (permanent) — never strings synthesized from the pattern list they test.
— **TRDD-1YNY73NT (P2, canonical):** golden rule G1.1 mandates self-identification on every GitHub write, and the version-bump commit the tool ITSELF creates carried none — a tool that cannot obey, not discipline drift. All three commit sites in publish.py and the canon's bump commit now append `Agent: <derived-slug>` as a second `-m` paragraph (a proper git trailer; subject untouched so the `%s` recovery comparison holds); the slug is DERIVED from the manifest, and any `@` is STRIPPED — GitHub linkifies `@word` in commit messages and a handle-shaped name would page a real account. Proven against a real git repo via `%(trailers:key=Agent,valueonly)`, plus an AST sweep that no commit argv in publish.py lacks the trailer.
— **TRDD-6UW0KZVY (P2, hub's 4th card — the amvcp TRDD-YY5ISKCJ shape):** `_remote_tag_exists` collapsed "ls-remote FAILED" into "tag absent", and two DESTRUCTIVE pre-push consumers acted on that guess — the interrupted-publish recovery (`git reset --soft` + local tag delete) and the stale-tag move — either of which, on a network failure while the tag WAS published, mangles local state against the public source of truth. Now three-valued: None (could not read) is DISTINCT from False (read succeeded, zero tags — the first-publish path, unaffected); both destructive sites REFUSE on None; a confirmed-published tag takes a plain follow-up commit (the pre-#151 resume flow, kept intact); the post-push verify keeps its positive-only wrapper so None and False alike report UNVERIFIED — never green, never newly blocking. Tested on REAL git remotes (missing path → None; bare → False; pushed tag → True), for CPV's copy AND the helpers exec'd out of the emitted canon. version 5.6.0, test files 481. **v5.5.0 (part 2) — the reported-issue batch: twelve fixes, and central verification caught three defects the delegated work did not**
— **Sixteen open issues, filed by nine ecosystem plugin Claudes, triaged first-hand before a line was written.** Four closed with evidence rather than code: **#196** (`background` accepted since v5.4.0 — probed, incl. the widened `no`/`off`/`0` spellings), **#197** (the ratified `baseline-*` trio is live and to spec on the repo; the reporter's `required_linear_history` claim is **stale** — it was removed by owner ruling and must not return), **#204** (`--unreleased` gone since v5.3.0; the reporter's `--prepend` proposal declined **in writing**, because it preserves history but is NOT idempotent and a retried publish would duplicate a section), **#206** (`git add -A` gone since v4.3.0).
— **The twelve real ones, each with the reason that does not survive in a diff.** `SSRF_ADVANCED` had no static-literal carve-out although `SSRF_PATTERN` did, so a module-level https constant drew a publish-blocking MAJOR (#198). `AGENT_MEMORY_MOD`'s verb had no position anchor, so any identifier ending `-write`/`-edit` became the verb of a memory-modification instruction — RE2 has no lookbehind, hence `(^|[^\w-])` (#208). The canon emitted a `nosec` comment whose **last word completed the SSRF needle on the same line** — the generator shipping code its own validator blocked; the word moved, the marker stayed on the call line because bandit only honours it there (#199). The emitted `stage_gh_release` passed the **whole full-history changelog** as one release's body, harmless only while the truncation bug kept the file to one section — so fixing v5.3.0 exposed it (#205). A bare `@word` in a commit subject survived git-cliff into the release body and **pages a real account**; the postprocessor backticks what GitHub actually linkifies and leaves `@types/node` / `@lru_cache` / `user@host` / `actions/checkout@v4` alone, measured against the real engine (#202). Gate 15 uninstalled and never asserted its own cleanup (#209) — **READ-ONLY**, because the registry is Claude Code's state and a publish pipeline that edits another tool's state file to tidy up after itself is worse than the orphan; cannot-read reports UNVERIFIED, never clean. pyright ran without `cwd=repo_root`, so a validator launched from a foreign cwd — the normal case for agents and CI wrappers — mass-reported false unresolved-import MINORs (#200). `pyrightconfig.json` **is JSONC by its owning tool's definition**, and a strict parse turned a valid config into a publish blocker; routed through the JSONC loader CPV already had, because a second parser drifts and a drifted copy is how a validator accepts on one path what it rejects on another (#210). A command whose advertised purpose was baseline compliance created a parallel ruleset and printed a `DELETE` aimed at the ratified one — the root was conflating *"a ruleset I can learn bypass actors from"* with *"a ruleset that should be removed"* (#203). `RC-TEST-COVERAGE` credited a component whose name merely appeared in a test of a different KIND and gave nothing to a table-driven test covering 23 by globbing — wrong in both directions, and it **penalised the stronger test shape** (#207). And "(demoted, needs review)" asked for a verdict while naming nowhere to record one: the mechanism existed, but it is a repo-root FILE and not a flag, so a `--help` census could never find it — one INFO line per run now names it WITH its constraints, so it cannot read as a mute button (#201).
— **#211 is the one whose fix paid for itself immediately.** Phase durations were stamped at SUBMIT time and emitted in SUBMIT order, so 27 concurrent phases all reported the batch wall time and the instrumentation could not answer the only question it exists for. Workers now measure their own span and return it; emission stays on the dispatching thread, because a worker touching stderr is what deadlocked every Linux CI run in v3.23.0. **Measured before/after on this tree: 27 phases at an identical ~601.2s → every duration distinct**, carrying its worker slot — and the result named the bottleneck on the first run: `skillaudit_native` **509.0s cold / 380.1s warm, SERIAL**, against a whole parallel batch whose slowest member is 119s. Parallelising 27 validators that finish in 0.0s buys nothing while one phase runs alone for six minutes.
— **Three defects central verification caught that the delegated work did not**, and each is a shape this repo has met before: a test that grepped for a token **its own fix had put in a COMMENT** (a comment naming an anchor is indistinguishable from the anchor); a catch-all `@` regex that **contradicted its own four assertions** by flagging `@types/node`, which pages nobody — a guard that fires on correct output is a guard that gets deleted; and a rule-catalog edit shipped **without regenerating the self-hash manifest**, which silently stopped CPV recognising a copy of its own catalog (2 failures in the full suite, and the recorded invariant is *hashes last, always*). The new SSRF fixture is **DEVITALIZED, not suppressed**: the link-local metadata address is assembled at import time so no source line carries the needle, while the string handed to the scanner is byte-identical — proven two-sided (0 occurrences in source, present at runtime), so the fixture still proves the carve-out refuses it.
— **Verified centrally rather than accepted from five agents' reports:** full suite **12897 passed / 3 skipped**; cache-cold `--strict` self-validate **0/0/0/0/0, exit 0, 47/47 phases**; ruff + mypy + pyright clean on all 16 changed files — and the near-miss worth keeping is that a stale nine-hour-old report file briefly read as a clean gate, caught only because 4 completed phases cannot produce a 47-phase verdict. **A check that measured nothing looks exactly like a check that passed.** test files 478. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[a-regression-test-must-be-verified-to-fail]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.5.0 — CC spec sync v2.1.225–232: a source type that RUNS a command, the marketplace check that had quietly become a GitHub-only gate, and a pinned omission whose reason expired**
— **The window's real surface is narrow, and finding that out is the work.** Eight releases, most of it runtime behaviour CPV cannot see. Checked against the RAW docs rather than the changelog (the method this repo already records as the only reliable one, after a WebFetch summary false-negatived twice): seven candidate surfaces, six real gaps, one deliberate non-change.
— **`command` sources (v2.1.229) are the security-relevant one.** A marketplace entry may now name a LOCAL COMMAND whose stdout is the plugin directory — so a marketplace can cause arbitrary code to run on the user's machine, gated only by an acceptance prompt showing the command string. CPV rejected the whole type as invalid, which reads as safe and is not: an author who cannot validate the entry ships it unvalidated. The type is now recognised AND shape-checked, and the shape checks are the security half rather than schema pedantry — the doc's own constraints (printable ASCII, ≤500 chars, no run of four or more spaces) exist so the user can READ the whole command they are accepting. A control character or a long space run renders as something shorter than it is, so what the user accepts is not what runs. `timeout` is bounded 1–600 and **excludes `bool`**, because `isinstance(True, int)` is True and `"timeout": true` would otherwise pass as one second. Its managed kill switch `disableCommandPluginSources` joins `MANAGED_ONLY_KEYS` — an org control over arbitrary execution that a project file can override is not a control.
— **The FP was the one nobody had filed, and v2.1.232 is what made it bite.** `validate_github_source_required` emitted a **MINOR** — which BLOCKS `--strict` — on any `repository` that was not github.com. The spec has never said that (plugins-reference.md:491 and plugin-marketplaces.md:214 both call it a "source code repository URL"), and v2.1.232 made bare gitlab.com marketplace URLs clone exactly like github.com ones — so CPV was hard-blocking the publish of a plugin hosted on a host Claude Code had just made first-class. Fixed by DELEGATING to the host-agnostic `validate_repository_url` rather than re-deriving a second copy, because two copies of a URL rule drift and a drifted copy is how a validator accepts on one path what it rejects on another. **The delegation then regressed something my own probe caught and the test suite would not have:** `git@host:owner/repo` has no URL scheme, so the shared rule sent every scp-style SSH URL — including the `git@github.com:` form the old check explicitly accepted — to the invalid branch. A widening that quietly narrowed. New `SCP_LIKE_SSH_PATTERN`, host-agnostic on purpose. Two-sided: gitlab/bitbucket/self-hosted/nested-subgroup/ssh all clear, while `ftp://`, `a/b/c/d`, a bare word and prose still fire.
— **Nine GitLab token families the secret scanner could not see.** `glrt-`, `gloas-`, `glptt-`, `glagent-`, `glimt-`, `glsoat-`, `glcbt-`, `glft-`, `glffct-`, `gldt-` are separate PREFIXES, not variants of `glpat-`, so the single existing pattern matched none of them — a committed runner or OAuth token was invisible. One alternation, not nine near-copies: nine copies is nine chances for one to drift.
— **A pinned omission retired for the right reason, which is the part worth keeping.** v5.4.0 deliberately held `crossSessionInbound` out of `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS` and pinned the omission WITH its reason: changelog-only, so its level was unverifiable, and a wrong-level entry in a typo detector excuses a genuine typo. settings.md now carries it in the Available-settings table and documents a stricter project value being honored, so the reason EXPIRED — the judgement was never wrong, the evidence changed. That is exactly what pinning a reason buys: the next sync checks the reason instead of re-litigating the decision from memory. **`sandbox.ripgrep` (v2.1.232) is the new deliberate omission**, pinned the same way — it is nested under the already-known `sandbox`, so no top-level entry is owed and a bare `ripgrep` entry would excuse a typo written there.
— **Verified as needing no change rather than assumed:** `command` is absent from `SOURCE_REQUIRED_FIELDS` on purpose, matching the `archive` precedent — the required field is checked by the per-type shape function, which reports the specific rule id a fixer routes on, and listing it in both places would emit two findings for one defect. +56 two-sided tests; **non-vacuity proven by reverting all three sources — 36 fail, and the 20 that pass are precisely the FN-safety controls** (unknown source type still rejected, near-miss typos still unknown, `glpat-` still fires, malformed URLs still fire, the ripgrep omission), which must hold in BOTH states. version 5.5.0, scripts 139, test files 469. [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] [[claim-verification]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.4.0 — the release body I shipped one version earlier was the WHOLE changelog; the emitted canon had none of what v5.3.0 fixed; and a CC spec sync for v2.1.220–224 (assumes a `--minor` bump)**
— **The regression was mine, and it was live.** v5.3.0 fixed the changelog GENERATOR and shipped a `release.yml` whose body step was still a bare copy of the entire file: measured on the released tag, **110,166 characters against GitHub's 125,000-char release-body limit**, i.e. a couple of releases from `gh release create` failing outright *after* the tag is already public. The awk section extractor now takes this release's section only — measured **277 chars** — and, the half that matters, **every branch is BOUNDED**: the no-section-match fallback is the git log since the previous tag, never the whole file, because falling back to the full CHANGELOG re-introduces the exact defect the moment the header format drifts. The header class accepts em-dash and hyphen: CPV's own CHANGELOG uses the hyphen form, so matching only the em-dash would have taken the fallback on every release while looking correct.
— **The generator carried none of it.** Per the fix-what-the-tool-EMITS rule, the generator copy is the higher-impact instance — and the emitted `publish.py` had **zero** of the install smoke and **zero** of the `ls-remote` tag verification v5.3.0 added to CPV's own pipeline, so every plugin scaffolded since inherited a pipeline that could not prove its own release. Both ported. **A defect the port created and only inspecting the GENERATED file caught:** the emitted template does not import `tempfile`, so the ported stage would have `NameError`d in every scaffolded plugin at publish time — invisible to `ast.parse`, and invisible to any check that reads the generator instead of its output. Locked by a test that resolves every free name in the emitted stage against the emitted file's own imports.
— **Then the byte-identity test earned its keep, again.** The migrator lifts the CI-verify call block VERBATIM out of freshly-rendered canon and ended that lift at "the next `cprint`" — a silent assumption that the CI-verify call was the LAST post-release call. The moment canon gained a second one, the lift swallowed it and the migrator emitted the install smoke **TWICE**, into a file that already had it. The span now ends at the call this migrator owns. Worth recording precisely because the first repair was WORSE: `_slice_between` EXCLUDES its end anchor, so ending on the call dropped the call itself — the stage defined and never invoked, which is the dead-code half `_inject_ci_verify` explicitly refuses to ship. **Three separate times this session an assertion failed because my own explanatory prose contained the literal token the assertion greps for** — a comment naming an anchor is indistinguishable from the anchor.
— **Eight more review findings, all fixed and all two-sided.** Two migrator "refusals" that could not refuse (a block-insert `.replace` that was a silent no-op when `def main() -> int:` was absent shipped a flag and a call with no helper; an `args`-before-`parse_args()` ordering that bricked every invocation of the migrated file); a changelog migrator that pointed an already-correct oddly-spelled call at `--force-templates`; `print_canon_version` claiming a canon was stale when it merely could not compare; a lag note whose `not in` test fired on every run because install stdout carries no semver; an install smoke that left what it installed behind (now `--scope local --keep-data -y`, both flags safety — the author usually has the plugin installed at USER scope); an unregistered marketplace reported SKIPPED rather than failed; and a test that passed only on the new warning prose instead of reading the git-cliff argv.
— **The spec sync (v2.1.220–224), verified against the RAW docs rather than a summary.** `archive` joins the per-plugin source types (a zip over HTTPS, v2.1.224+) — and because every constraint the doc states is a case Claude Code REFUSES at install, the entry is shape-checked too: `url` required and HTTPS-only, loopback / link-local / cloud-metadata hosts rejected, `sha256` 64 hex either case. `skills` now accepts the bare `"."` (the plugin root holding a SKILL.md — the doc's own example), scoped to that field and that exact string, so `"."` elsewhere and `..` anywhere still MAJOR; a plugin using the documented form was drawing a publish-blocking MAJOR. `dialogExpiry` becomes a known settings key — **and got its own scope set rather than reusing `PROJECT_REJECTED_KEYS`**, because that set's remediation offers `settings.local.json`, which for this key is not a valid home: a fix that leaves the key just as ignored while looking addressed. Three env vars added. **`crossSessionInbound` is deliberately NOT added** and the omission is pinned WITH its reason: it appears only in the changelog, so whether it is top-level is unverifiable, and `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS` is a typo detector — an entry at the wrong level would excuse a genuine typo written there.
— **RC-TEST-COVERAGE was lying about CPV's own tree, and the byte budget was the liar.** `_coverage_test_blobs` concatenated test files until a 5 MB cap and then **broke out of the loop** — so on any suite bigger than that, the tail was never read and every component tested only there was reported as having "no discoverable test". CPV ships **7.7 MB** of tests, so **35% of its own suite was never scanned**: the advisory named **21** components, of which **10 were artifacts of where the budget happened to fall**. A finding that changes with filesystem iteration order is not a finding. The scan now matches per-token and **drops each token the moment it is seen, stopping as soon as none remain**, which turns the budget back into what it was meant to be — a runtime guard, not a source of findings — and when the budget genuinely IS exhausted with components unaccounted for, the check says **it could not determine coverage** rather than naming them. That asymmetry is the whole point and it is the mirror of this project's own rule: *cannot check is never a pass* — and it is not a FAILURE either.
