An advisory that guesses in the accusing direction is one its reader learns to discount, which costs it the credibility it needs to be read at all (the v5.1.1 lesson, one layer down). 21 → **11 genuinely untested**, and those 11 then got REAL tests (31 of them, two parallel agents, one file each) rather than the name-drop that would clear the check while testing nothing — CPV's own tree now reports **zero** untested components. **And the POPULATION half, from a peer's retraction rather than my own testing.** A fleet session published a fork-skill count for this repo, retracted it, and the corrected figure was still wrong — the "1 unpinned skill" it named was `.trashcan/…/skills/cpv-format-menu/SKILL.md`, a skill DELETED in May sitting in the janitor's safe-delete staging inside the working tree. It carries genuine line-1 frontmatter, so a strict parser matches it too; **only a population rule removes it.** That sent me to CPV's own enumeration: components were clean (bounded globs), but TEST discovery was `rglob`-based and counted **8** dead files — archived copies under gitignored `*_dev/` trees plus two trashcan ghosts. Zero credited a component **today**, so nothing was wrong with the reported list; but a dead test crediting a live component is the **FN direction** — it mutes the advisory silently — and the printed suite size was overstated by 8 regardless. Fixed by reusing the rule CPV already owns (`gitignored_unshipped_paths`, v2.126.26) rather than the tempting name-list: **naming conventions belong to one author and CPV validates everyone's plugins**, and the name-list would also have dropped the case the git rule keeps — a **TRACKED**-then-gitignored file, which still ships in `git archive`. That anti-evasion control passes in BOTH states by design, which is exactly why it is the one worth having. The finding now PRINTS what it excluded, because a count whose scope is invisible is the shape that misleads: a capped walk, an unfiltered tree and a wrong root each yield a plausible number and none announces itself. Non-vacuity, three ways: reverting the detector fails **4 of 5** truncation tests (the 5th is the control that must pass in BOTH states — without it, a "fix" that reported nothing at all would satisfy every other assertion while destroying the check); and the delegated tests were verified by MUTATION rather than by their authors' say-so — pinning a `model:` on `cpv-spark-agent` and zeroing `BenchResult.recall` each failed exactly the test that claims to cover it, and both files were restored clean. **Writing the tests then surfaced four latent defects in the newly-covered scripts, and checking them first is why only three were fixed.** REAL: `parse_corpus` labelled an exemplar by its enclosing SECTION, so a `### FP-1:` misfiled under `## TP exemplars` was counted as a TP **and retitled `TP-n`, erasing the evidence** — a bench whose precision/recall ledger can be silently wrong is worse than no bench; the block's own prefix now wins. REAL: a missing `notify-marketplace.yml` template fell through with **no output at all**, so the run printed "Setup complete!" while the file the next step tells you to copy was never created — now a loud WARNING, deliberately not a failure, since it is a reference copy and failing would newly break setups that work today. REAL: `get_submodule_paths` seeded `path` but not `url`, so a stanza omitting `url` returned a differently-shaped dict that `KeyError`s on someone else's repo. **NOT REAL: the reported `ZeroDivisionError` in `_compose_report` does not exist** — all three quotients are guarded and `rows` is built from a hard-coded 3-phase list, so `rows[1]`/`rows[-1]` always exist; hardening a division that cannot divide by zero would have added a branch no test can reach. Reported defects get verified before they get fixed.
— **The v2.1.221 Desktop-sync names — and the drift found under them.** A marketplace named `org`, `org-provisioned` or `unknown` **in any casing** is accepted by Claude Code and makes Claude Desktop's managed sync reject the WHOLE marketplace (plugin-marketplaces.md:1153); `claude plugin validate` has checked this since v2.1.221 and CPV did not. It is a **WARNING**, deliberately, and NOT an entry in `RESERVED_MARKETPLACE_NAMES` — that set is CRITICAL, and these names work perfectly in Claude Code, so blocking a publish on one would invent a gate the spec does not have (the v2.154.1 ruling, applied again). A marketplace never distributed through Desktop org sync is unaffected and the file cannot say which it is. **Then the drift:** `standardize_marketplace` carried a SECOND copy of the reserved list whose own comment claimed it was *"aligned with validate_marketplace.py"* — it had drifted by **NINE names**, every addition since it was written, `healthcare` and `first-party-plugins` among them, so `standardize` was silently accepting names the validator rejects. **A copy that ASSERTS it is in sync is worse than an obvious fork: it stops the next reader from checking** — the v5.1.3 #191 shape, where a documented-but-nonexistent parity test hid a live bypass. It now imports the spec set and unions its own single-word scaffolding advice (`test`, `demo`, `example`, `official` — advice, never spec-reserved), with a test that fails the moment anyone re-forks it. **Verified as NEEDING NO CHANGE, rather than assumed:** Desktop's own name rule (≤128 chars of letters/digits/`.`/`_`/`-`) is strictly LOOSER than CPV's kebab-case-and-64 rule, so every name CPV accepts Desktop accepts too — adding that check would be dead code.
— **A v2.1.218 gap the sync surfaced, and the failure mode is silence.** A `context: fork` skill runs in the BACKGROUND by default (skills.md:271), so the turn that invoked it receives an agent handle instead of the skill's output — no error, no warning — and the fork runs with the narrower background-subagent tool set. skills.md:573 states it plainly: *"Before v2.1.218, forked skills always blocked the turn until they finished"*, so **every forked skill written against the old behaviour changed meaning without its file changing**. CPV knew `background` was a valid key and checked nothing about it. Now a fork with no explicit `background` draws a WARNING — the only tier that never blocks, deliberately, because backgrounding is legal and Claude Code still waits in cases CPV cannot see from the file (non-interactive `-p` / the Agent SDK, background tasks disabled, a re-entrant invocation, a scheduled task). An EXPLICIT `background` either way is silent: the author decided. The two canon references that TEACH `context: fork` gained the default too — a doc that omits it re-introduces the surprise on the next upgrade, the v2.137.1 shape. +101 two-sided tests; **non-vacuity proven by reverting all five spec-sync sources — 21 fail, and the 11 that pass are exactly the controls.** version 5.4.0, scripts 139, test files 467. [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.3.0 — the canon was DESTROYING every plugin's changelog history, and the canon's own repo was the worst instance (ai-maestro#62)**
— **The defect, reproduced against our own tree first.** Gate 9 ran `git-cliff --bump --unreleased --tag <v> -o CHANGELOG.md`. `-o` OVERWRITES, so restricting content to the unreleased window and then writing leaves a changelog holding ONLY the release just generated. Reported by the ai-maestro server Claude against canon 5.0.0; I went to reproduce rather than agree, and the first measurement was **our own CHANGELOG.md: 1 section, against 381 shipped releases**. Dropping `--unreleased` rebuilds **381 sections back to 1.3.1**, and running it **twice is byte-identical** — which is the whole argument against the obvious fix: **`--prepend` preserves history but is NOT idempotent**, so a publish retried after a failed downstream gate duplicates a section. Both assertions are pinned, and the old command is run in the same test to prove the test can fail. **Blast radius, measured across every plugin repo on this host because nobody had run it:** CPV 1/381, visual-comunicator 1/46, claude-menu-system 1/9, WEBDESIGN 1/7, assistant-role 1/3, web-scenario-tester 1/3 — and rechecker-plugin **114/114**, the anomaly that validates the two-assertion insistence: it survived because it uses `--prepend`, i.e. the almost-fix, exposed to duplication instead of loss. Nothing is unrecoverable — the history is rendered from the commit log and returns on the next publish. **The comment is the other half of the fix:** the old one cited "the pattern recommended by the git-cliff docs", so a reviewer comparing the step to its documentation found agreement and moved on. *A wrong step that documents itself as right survives review* — the reporter's phrasing, now written into the canon as the reason `--unreleased` must NOT be there.
— **`--canon-version` (the fleet asked for it through the user).** A downstream plugin carries a COPY of the canon, so "which canon am I on?" is unanswerable from the copy. The generator now bakes `CANON_VERSION` at scaffold/migration time and the emitted `publish.py --canon-version` prints installed-vs-latest (latest read from the canon repo's manifest). **Exit 0 ALWAYS** and it runs BEFORE any tree check: an info command that fails offline, or refuses to answer because the tree is dirty, is useless exactly when someone is debugging a stale canon. Offline prints `unknown (could not reach GitHub)` — never "up to date".
— **R3 prove-the-tag (server) + R2 prove-the-install (MANAGER).** After the atomic push each tag is checked against the REMOTE via `git ls-remote` — a stage that ran and silently failed its ref-update is otherwise indistinguishable from one that worked. New **Gate 15** installs the just-published plugin from its marketplace into a clean temp dir, because no static check catches an uninstallable release (the manifest is valid, the tags exist, the entry is correct — and eleven plugins still shipped uninstallable). It runs post-release by necessity, so by DEFAULT it reports loudly rather than failing the run (a non-zero could not un-ship anything); `CPV_PUBLISH_REQUIRE_INSTALL_SMOKE=1` gives the fleet the fail-the-release semantics they asked for. **Cannot-check is never a pass:** no `claude` CLI or an unresolvable marketplace reports SKIPPED with the reason, and the marketplace name is RESOLVED (Layout B manifest / Layout A notify-workflow → remote manifest), never guessed — installing from the wrong marketplace would prove nothing.
— **Delivery is the half that reaches anyone:** `standardize` never overwrites an existing `publish.py` on a plain `--fix`, and the plugins that need these most are the ones that cannot safely `--force-templates`. Two surgical idempotent migrators (changelog + canon-version) ship both on ANY `--fix`, lifting canonical text VERBATIM from freshly-rendered canon. **A real defect the tests caught in my own migrator:** the canon's `--canon-version` argparse call spans two lines, and lifting only the first left an unclosed `(` — a publish.py that will not parse, i.e. exactly the half-rewritten outcome these migrators exist to refuse. Fixed with a paren-balancing statement lift, pinned by a migrated-file-must-compile assertion. +23 two-sided tests; 116 green across the new suite and every sibling migrator suite. version 5.3.0, test files 462. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.2.0 — the audit-consent REGISTRY: a recordable review verdict for demoted findings that cannot host the #101 sentinel (closes #194; lands PR #195 after a measured 3-hole review)**
— **The gap (#194, filed by the ai-maestro-plugin Claude):** content that is BOTH instruction-loadable AND a DESCRIPTION of the attacks it forbids — a governance rulebook's rule-table rows — had no honest path through `--strict`: the #101 sentinel is fence-anchored (a table row has no fence to host it), the doc-only suppression rightly excludes `references/`, and the demoted-NIT "needs review" state had nowhere to record a false-positive verdict, so it blocked forever. New `.cpv-audit-consent.json` at plugin root maps (file, ruleId, sha256 of the flagged line) → reason; a MATCHING **demoted** finding emits as a visible non-blocking WARNING "(demoted, consented)". **The review was done with the real scanner, not the PR prose, and measured a genuine hole in the submitted branch:** a demoted `INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT` with a matching entry consented to WARNING — a weaker parallel gate an author shops to when the sentinel says no. Three fixes landed on the branch: (1) `_CONSENT_PROTECTED_RULES` (= `_INTENT_HARD_SIGNAL_RULES`, the "prose IS the attack" family: prompt-inject, exfil, secrets, decode-threats) can NEVER consent, demoted or not — A2A/agent-manipulation, the #194 use case, is provably outside the set (pinned by test); (2) the hash input moved from the finding's `lineContent` (= `line.strip()[:200]`) to the FULL stripped line RE-READ FROM DISK at report time — governance rows routinely exceed 200 chars, so an edit BEYOND char 200 would have silently inherited a consent recorded for the original text (proven by a two-sided test whose precondition asserts the tamper is invisible to the truncated recipe); (3) the `reason` is whitespace-collapsed + capped at 200 chars at load time, because it is interpolated into the report where an embedded newline could fabricate report lines. Everything else fail-closed: live/keep findings are untouchable by any registry entry, absent/malformed registry consents to nothing, a missing warning bucket falls through to blocking, a missing flagged file declines. 16 two-sided tests. **Process fact worth keeping: the PR path is structurally impossible in this repo** — CI's Validate verifies the tree against the manifest PUBLISHED on GitHub, which only updates when a release lands on master, so a PR touching any manifest-covered file can never go green; the branch was reviewed, fixed, rebased, then landed via the canonical publish.py direct-to-master flow (the PR closed with a pointer). version 5.2.0, test files 461. [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[lesson-review-competing-fix-with-real-scanner]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.6 — the integrity-failure recovery hint fabricated "cached versions" that do not exist (found by hitting it live)**
— on any manifest mismatch, `_plugin_verify_hashes` printed EVERY sibling dir of `plugin_root.parent` as a runnable `python3 "<sib>/scripts/remote_validation.py"` recovery command. Correct in the plugin-cache layout (`<cache>/<marketplace>/<plugin>/<version>/`), garbage in a dev checkout: the parent is an arbitrary WORKSPACE, so `reports/`, `docs_dev/`, `thoughts/` — agent-writable dirs — were endorsed as launchers for files that do not exist (observed during the v5.1.5 session's own self-validate). Worse than wrong: a hint that recommends `python3` on an unverified path in a dir agents write to is a place a planted script could be endorsed. New `_discover_sibling_cached_versions` applies two gates — the sibling's NAME is a dotted numeric version AND `scripts/remote_validation.py` actually EXISTS (never suggest running a path not verified to exist) — sorts numerically (10.0.0 > 9.0.0, where the old lexicographic sort inverted), and an uniterable parent yields the honest "(no sibling cached versions found)" branch instead of a swallowed pass. +8 two-sided tests. version 5.1.6, test files 460. [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.5 — the CI-recovery hints dead-ended at their first step (code-review findings, fixed and shipped same-day)**
— Gate 14's RED hint printed `gh run view --log-failed --commit <sha>` — but `gh run view` has no `--commit` flag (verified against installed gh; `--commit` belongs to `gh run list`) — and the UNVERIFIED hint passed `sha[:8]` to `gh run list --commit`, which on a short sha silently matches nothing and exits 0 (this repo's own recorded lesson, lesson-gh-run-list-short-sha-silently-empty): both documented recovery paths failed exactly when a maintainer needed them, the v5.1.3-incident shape. Now full sha + a two-step (`gh run list --commit <full-sha>` → `gh run view --log-failed <run-id>`) with WHY comments citing both gh gotchas, in the live gate AND the canonical template every scaffolded plugin inherits (`generate_plugin_repo.py` — per the fix-what-the-tool-emits rule the generator copy is the higher-impact one; byte-identity test still green). Also: the #189 test module's header still asserted the falsified "SEM_VALUE_MAX 32767 on macOS and Linux" premise this same suite's platform fix disproved — it now states 32767-macOS / INT_MAX-Linux so the next reader cannot re-derive the mistake that shipped v5.1.3 with red CI; CLAUDE.md test-file count 453→459 (load-bearing inventory); 2 shebang scripts chmod +x. Found by `/code-review high --fix`, verified first-hand: gh flag surface, doc-parity 26/26, issue-189 suite green, publish gate-contract 20/20. version 5.1.5, test files 459. [[lesson-gh-run-list-short-sha-silently-empty]] [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.4 — v5.1.3 shipped with RED CI, because the deadlock's own regression tests assumed macOS's semaphore ceiling on a Linux runner**
— **v5.1.3's CI: Test Shard 2 FAILED and the release was already public** — Gate 14 reported `⚠ UNVERIFIED — still running after 900s`, and a direct watch of the released commit found CI `cancelled`. Two platform errors in the NEW #189 tests, same root: `SemLock.SEM_VALUE_MAX` is **32767 on macOS but INT_MAX on Linux**. (1) `test_the_ceiling_that_causes_this_is_real` asserted `SEM_MAX < 86222` unconditionally — false on Linux, instant FAIL. (2) The end-to-end test computed `SEM_MAX + 25` work items — **2,147,483,672 paths on Linux** — which silently consumed 18 minutes of the shard until the job's 20-minute `timeout-minutes` killed the runner; run-level verdict `cancelled`, real cause a resource bomb. **This also reframes #189 itself: the overflow is macOS-reachable, which is WHY the reporter (Darwin) hit it and Linux CI never did.** Fixes: the ceiling test asserts the universal premise (queue bound == semaphore cap) everywhere and the `< 86222` premise only on darwin; the blocking-control is skipif-not-darwin (its reason string carries the 2-billion-item story); the end-to-end caps items at `min(SEM_MAX, 32767) + 25` — over-the-cap proof on macOS, full-load feeder exercise on Linux. Also: **Gate 14's stdout is now flushed** — block-buffered stdout put the gate's banner ~70 lines AFTER its own stderr verdict in the captured log, making an honest gate read as one that silently printed nothing. And the **watchdog's exit code moved 3 → 124**: 3 collided with `EXIT_MINOR` and was misread within an hour of shipping (a one-MINOR self-validate taken for a budget abort); 124 is the `timeout(1)` convention, outside the 0..4 verdict vocabulary. version 5.1.4, test files 459. [[lesson-green-macos-suite-does-not-prove-linux-fork]] [[ci-vs-local-google-re2-serial]] [[lesson-bounded-per-item-unbounded-in-aggregate]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.3 — the #189 deadlock is `mp.Queue`'s slot semaphore; a hang could never exit; one path classifier existed twice, having quietly diverged into a bypass; `--gate` refused its own advertised mode; and the size gate hid its margin (#189, #190, #191, #192, #193)**
— **#189, REPRODUCED rather than reasoned about — and the third answer was the first correct one.** Running the reporter's exact command against their exact commit wedged `security_execclass_gate` for **1710s of a 1800s budget at 336s CPU** — asleep 81% of the time — and the abort's own stack dump named the line: main thread in `queues.py:89 in put` ← `cpv_scan_supervisor.py:498`, feeder thread in `connection.py:384 in _send` on a full pipe. **A default `mp.Queue` is NOT unbounded: its slot semaphore caps at `SemLock.SEM_VALUE_MAX` = 32767.** `supervised_scan` filled `task_q` with every work item BEFORE spawning a single worker, so past 32767 items the parent blocked in `put()` with no consumer in existence and nothing that could ever drain it. The reporter's tree holds **86222 files** (a Rust `target/`, a `.venv`, caches) against 1479 tracked — so it fired on **every** run, which is exactly why their bisect was clean. **That threshold is also why three attempts got this wrong, two of them mine:** every probe used a corpus under 32767 files, where the bug CANNOT occur, and each completing probe was read as evidence of innocence. I published "not the supervisor" off a throughput benchmark, then shipped v5.1.2 claiming a fix for a race the reporter had never hit, then argued it was a duration problem. **A probe that cannot fail the way the report describes proves nothing** — the same lesson, three times, until a real stack ended it. Fix is ordering plus concurrency: workers spawn FIRST so a consumer exists, and the feed moves to a daemon thread, because feeding on the main thread deadlocks even WITH consumers (a blocked `put()` means the loop that drains results never runs). Every `put` is now bounded and a test asserts that **structurally** — an unbounded one anywhere reopens this through another door. The feeder stops before the shutdown sentinels: a sentinel overtaking a real item would retire a worker with files still queued, converting a deadlock into **silently unscanned files**, which is worse because it reads as a completed scan. Measured on the corpus that hung: **1710s stuck → 53.7s DONE**, whole run 392s. [[lesson-throughput-cannot-disprove-a-deadlock]]
— **#190 — a hang could never exit, and that outlives any individual deadlock.** `_serial_phase` wraps every serial step in progress markers and, as its own docstring says, *"this only observes"*. Individual phases bound their own work; the RUN had no upper bound, so the interpreter waited forever, its parent died, and it was reparented to `launchd` — **two found on the reporter's host at 3h and 11h, at 0.0% CPU**, one per hung attempt, surfaced by nothing. New wall-clock budget over the whole run, armed in BOTH `remote_validation.main()` (the process actually found orphaned) and `validate_plugin.main()`. **It exits 124 (the `timeout(1)` convention), never 0** — a budget expiring into a pass converts a hang into a silent VALID, and "could not finish" is not "found no problems". **The first draft used 3, which collides with `EXIT_MINOR` — and was misread within the hour:** a clean-but-for-one-MINOR self-validate exited 3 and was taken for a budget abort. Reusing a verdict code recreates in the exit status the very conflation the watchdog exists to prevent; 124 is outside the 0..4 finding vocabulary and cannot be emitted by a verdict. **It calls `os._exit`, not an exception:** the firing thread is not the stuck thread, and a main thread in `pthread_cond_wait` can never observe a raised exception, so a polite abort would leave the hang exactly as it was. **The env may only LOWER it** (`CPV_VALIDATE_BUDGET_S=0` is ignored) — a guard an env var can switch off is a guard an attacker can switch off. The abort names the stuck phase AND dumps every thread's Python stack via `faulthandler`, which needs no root: that dump is what diagnosed #189 in one run after three rounds of guessing, and every future hang now carries it automatically. [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]]
— **#191 — the duplication was not a tidiness problem, it was a live bypass.** Ten separately-closed FP issues shared one root cause: the detector was right about the bytes and wrong about the FILE. `cpv_skillaudit_native` and `_skillaudit_markdown_context` each carried a copy of the doc-only classifier, documented as staying in sync *"via a parity test in `tests/test_skillaudit_doc_only_parity.py`"* — **that test does not exist**, and the copies had duly diverged: the native side was fixed (audit MED #8) to strip a literal `./`, the mirror kept `str.lstrip("./")`, a CHARACTER-SET strip that turns `.specs/evil.md` into `specs/evil.md`. **Measured: `.specs/`, `.docs/`, `.doc/`, `.guides/`, `.wiki/` were classified as inert documentation**, demoting execution-class matches and giving bash fences the doc-only treatment inside directories an attacker names freely. Data sets verified byte-identical; only the normalisation had drifted, so centralising on the native behaviour strictly **TIGHTENS** the mirror — the FN-safe direction. **Deliberately NOT what the issue proposed:** the reporter asked for an "authored instruction surface" tier at reduced severity, but `cpv_skillaudit_native:2258` already rules the opposite way for INTENT-class rules, and correctly — *prose IS the threat-delivery vector*, a malicious markdown file's only payload IS its prose. So this centralises WHICH FILE it is and changes **no severity at all**; which rule families apply stays with the detectors, where one misclassified path cannot mute all of them at once. Tests assert **identity (`is`), not equality** — equality passes for two copies that agree today, the exact state that shipped the bypass — plus positive controls, so it could not be "fixed" by classifying nothing as docs and reintroducing all ten original FPs. **The centralisation also tripped a real iron rule:** the full suite caught the new module-level import breaking `cpv_skillaudit_native`'s ZERO-third-party-imports invariant; `cpv_surface_class` is first-party and imports only `typing`, so the allowlist entry is legitimate — but an entry justified by "that module is stdlib-only" is only sound while that stays TRUE, so it ships with a test that fails the moment anything non-stdlib appears there, closing the laundering route the entry would otherwise open.
— **#192 — the emitted `--gate` advertised a no-push check mode, then refused to run it.** `run_gate` opened with an unconditional G0 ancestry check, so standalone invocation died in 0.0s with *"Direct push not allowed"* — an error about an action the user never took. G0's invariant is "no PUSH bypasses the pipeline", never "no checks may run", so enforcement now keys on whether a push exists: `_push_in_flight()` walks the same non-spoofable ancestry for git-push (or the pre-push hook's own path — partial ancestry must not open the guard). **Three-valued, and `None` is the security half:** ancestry-undeterminable FAILS CLOSED — collapsing `None` into `False` would turn a broken `ps` into the exact bypass G0 prevents. G1's version-not-bumped block gets the same treatment (pre-bump is the NORMAL standalone state; in push context equal versions mean a bypass and still block). Behavioural tests import the RENDERED canon and flip only the ancestry: the same bare directory is blocked at G0 under push ancestry and reaches G2 standalone. Non-vacuity: neutered (`push_ctx = True`), 2 fail / 8 controls pass.
— **#193 — the size-gate finding said "bpe estimate" and hid the x1.3.** Verified in BOTH directions before touching anything: the estimator's bytes/token swings 3.45→1.17 across text kinds, so the reporter's `len/3.0151` hypothesis is FALSE (it is a real vendored o200k tokenizer); but on their own file CPV's raw o200k is 3861 vs their cl100k 3841 — 0.5% apart — with the reported 5020 being exactly `ceil(3861 × 1.3)`, so their magnitude claim is TRUE: the label asserted a method the number is not, and cost them an hour of reverse-engineering a divisor that never existed. Fix is **DISCLOSURE, not recalibration**: `check_token_limit` (the single emitter) now prints the char count and the estimator's own `detail` line — raw o200k + factor — which it previously computed and threw away. **The factor and every cap are unchanged and a test PINS them**: shrinking a safety margin to quiet a complaint is gate-weakening wearing a reporting fix's clothes; if 1.3 is ever recalibrated that is its own decision with its own evidence. version 5.1.3, test files 459. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-empty-result-is-not-a-clean-result]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.2 — a supervised scan waited forever on a work item that no longer existed, and the first fix's tests proved nothing (#189)**
— **The reporter bisected the hang to v5.0.0, the release I had publicly exonerated.** v5.0.0 (#182) made `resolve_hard_kill_after_s` fill the time budget BY DEFAULT for any scan with `files >= 32` or a single 1–8 MiB file, and `parallel_scan` routes to the supervisor whenever that budget is set — so the supervisor stopped being opt-in and became the path almost every real plugin takes, while `cpv_scan_supervisor.py`'s own docstring still said **"OPT-IN … the default per-plugin scan path is unchanged"**. A sentence that was true when written and false since made the routing flip invisible to anyone reading the module to find out what runs where; it cost an hour of misdirected investigation before the flip was noticed at all. Corrected in its own commit, with the measurement inlined so the next reader inherits it instead of repeating it. — **The methodological failure is worth more than the fix.** I ruled the supervisor out on a THROUGHPUT benchmark (400 files / 0.62 MB — lean 19.84s vs supervised 14.35s, i.e. **0.72x, FASTER**) and said so in the issue. Throughput cannot disprove a deadlock: a probe that always completes cannot fail the way a non-termination report describes. **Before publishing "not the cause", ask whether the probe could have failed the way the report describes** — a wrong "ruled out" spends someone else's runs. [[lesson-throughput-cannot-disprove-a-deadlock]]
— **The identical error then repeated one layer down, inside the fix.** The first guard commit shipped with 5 tests that were **verified to pass with the guard disabled** (5/5 green in 0.85s): `heartbeat[wid] = idx` is written BEFORE `scan_func` runs, so a scanner raising `SystemExit` dies AFTER the heartbeat and takes the already-handled WorkerDied path. Green tests over a real fix, proving nothing. **The window is only reachable from INSIDE `task_q.get()`** — where the queue unpickles the item — so the reproduction is a work item whose `__reduce__` revives into a function calling `os._exit(1)`: the worker dies with the item already consumed off the queue and no heartbeat published, exactly the state that strands a file. `supervised_scan` documents `file_list` as "paths or work-units; forwarded verbatim", so this needs **no test hook in the production hot path** — the alternative, and a bad one in a security scanner. `os._exit` rather than `sys.exit` on purpose: the child must not unwind, flush or record anything, matching the abruptness of the SIGKILL it models. **Two-sided, measured: guard present → 7 passed in 3.35s; guard neutered → TIMED OUT, exit 124, no output at all.** That 124 is the reported bug reproduced in a test.
— **The guard: a quiescent state is proof of loss, not progress.** Nobody in flight, queue drained, files still missing — after `_LOST_ITEM_STALL_TICKS` consecutive such polls the missing files are re-dispatched **once** (a transient loss still gets scanned, and the guard costs nothing on a healthy run); lost again, they are recorded as **errored** ScanResults. **An unscannable file must be REPORTED, never silently returned clean** — a guard that terminated with `findings=[]` would pass the termination test while converting a hang into an invisible coverage hole, which is strictly worse, because a hang at least announces itself. Requiring several consecutive quiescent polls keeps a worker mid-dispatch (item pulled, heartbeat not yet published) from being mistaken for a lost one. **A note on the measurement itself:** the first neuter run was piped into `tail`, so `$?` was tail's status and reported a meaningless 0 — the pipeline exit-code trap already in this project's notes. Re-run with the exit captured directly it was 124. Worth stating because a green pipeline status is exactly how an unproven fix gets called proven. version 5.1.2, test files 454. [[lesson-throughput-cannot-disprove-a-deadlock]] [[lesson-bg-task-exit-code-is-not-pytest]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.1 — RC-TEST-COVERAGE called a tested module untested, because it could not see through one level of indirection**
— **The advisory was not lying, and that is exactly why it was worth fixing.** It matched a component to a test by filename stem or content mention only, and said "no **discoverable** test" — literally true. But CPV's own `_skillaudit_rust_context` is driven by **78** test files through `cpv_skillaudit_native.scan_content`, and its name appears in none of them, so CPV told its own author that a classifier they had just written 14 tests for was untested. **A correct-but-misleading advisory is worse than a silent one:** the author knows it is wrong, and the next finding it emits gets discounted too — it spends the credibility the check needs in order to be read at all. **I had first reported this to the owner as the detector "misreporting a real file", which overstated it; checking the test source showed my test never names the module, so the defect is under-discovery, not misreporting.** New `_coverage_indirect_python_tokens` credits a `scripts/*.py` module that a DIRECTLY-named module imports. **The import scan is source-level and allows leading whitespace on purpose, which is the whole point rather than a detail** — `cpv_skillaudit_native.py:2205` imports the rust classifier **lazily, inside a function body**, and a top-level-only scan would miss precisely the dispatch shape this exists for. **ONE hop, deliberately bounded:** that is the depth at which "the suite drives a facade over this module" is still a fair claim, while full transitive closure would credit nearly every module in a cohesive package and make the advisory vacuous — an advisory that can never fire is worth exactly what no advisory is worth. **Two gates keep the credit from becoming a blanket mute:** only a module the suite NAMES may lend coverage onward (otherwise two untested modules would launder coverage off each other), and only a SIBLING plugin module counts (a stdlib import lends nothing). Measured on CPV's own tree: **24 → 18** untested components — it tightened, it did not mute. **Two-sided, 10/10; non-vacuity proven by neutering the credit — 7 fail, and the 3 that still pass are precisely the FN-safety guards** (untested importer lends nothing · a name in a comment is not an import · stdlib credits nothing), which must hold in BOTH states. WARN-only throughout: `exit_code_strict()` never blocks on a WARNING, so this cannot change any plugin's verdict in either direction. **Also settled two adjacent reports as NOT-defects, by checking rather than assuming:** the background-task notification's `exit code 0` is the last command in the chain (technically correct, practically useless — recorded in memory, and the janitor is NOT exposed since its heartbeat dispatches agents fire-and-forget per `janitor-architecture.md:46`); and the release push's `Bypassed rule violations` is the `cpv-branch-rules` ruleset's own `bypass_actors` (`RepositoryRole`, `bypass_mode: always`) being used as configured, so publish.py's direct-to-master model works — a sanctioned bypass, not a circumvention. — **THE CANON HALF — the release was never actually gated on CI, in CPV's own pipeline AND in the one it emits to every plugin it scaffolds.** `publish.py` cut the GitHub release at Gate 13 and stopped: `grep gh.run scripts/publish.py` returned **nothing**. Meanwhile the release push targets the default branch directly and the `cpv-branch-rules` ruleset grants the maintainer role `bypass_mode: always`, so GitHub prints `Bypassed rule violations … 3 of 3 required status checks are expected` and lets it through. **The bypass is deliberate — it is what makes a scripted release possible at all — but it means the required checks never gate anything:** tag, GitHub release and marketplace notification are all public before CI has said a word. "CI must be green" existed only in the creator/fixer agent PROSE, and prose is skippable — **the identical defect v2.157.0 fixed one gate earlier** when `ci-preflight` turned out to be enforced only by a sentence in `cpv-plugin-fixer-agent.md`. New **Gate 14** (`stage_verify_ci_green`, emitted as `[post-release]`) polls the released commit's runs to conclusion. **It never returns non-zero, and that asymmetry is the design rather than a weak gate:** by the time it runs the release is already published, so a non-zero exit could not un-ship anything — it would only abort *after* the irreversible step and discard the report that is the entire point. A RED result is a loud notice naming the failing runs plus the exact `gh run view --log-failed` command, which is what feeds the documented fix→re-publish loop. `skipped`/`neutral` are not failures (CPV's own dormant PyPI workflow reports `skipped` on every release; treating that as RED would cry wolf every time), and **"cannot check" is never reported as green** — no `gh`, no runs, or an expired wait each report UNVERIFIED with the reason ([[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] applied at design time).
— **The half that makes it reach anyone: 20+ plugins depend on this canon, and fixing the generator alone would have reached NONE of them.** `standardize` never overwrites an existing `publish.py` on a plain `--fix`, and the plugins that need this most are exactly the ones carrying a hand-customized pipeline they cannot safely `--force-templates` — the v3.22.0 (#179) lesson verbatim. So `migrate_publish_py_ci_verify` mirrors its two proven siblings: surgical, idempotent, on ANY `--fix`, and **lifting every replacement VERBATIM out of freshly-rendered canon** rather than carrying a second copy. **ALL-OR-NOTHING**, because the stage, its call site and the top-level `import time` are one unit: the call without the function is a `NameError` at publish time in someone else's repo at the least recoverable moment, and the function without the call is dead code that silently keeps the gap open **while looking migrated** — worse than not migrating. An unrecognised shape is left **byte-identical** and reported.
— **Three defects the verification caught that review would not have.** (1) The emitted template **lacked `import time`** — every scaffolded plugin would have `NameError`d on `time.monotonic()` at publish; caught by asserting the *generated* file's imports, not the generator's. (2) The migrator's hard-coded call-site comment **drifted from canon on its first run** (ASCII hyphen vs canon's em-dash), caught by the byte-identity test — so the call block is now lifted from canon too and there is literally one source. (3) A naive `"import time" in text` check would have been satisfied by canon's own **function-local** `import time` and skipped the module-level one; the check is anchored `^import time$`, with a test pinning that exact case. **The load-bearing test is `test_migrated_output_is_byte_identical_to_canon`** — it is what stops migrator and generator diverging, and it earned its keep on day one. +22 two-sided tests; **non-vacuity proven by neutering both halves — 13 fail, 9 pass**, the 9 being the controls (canon still compiles, the refusal paths, idempotency, the `--fix` wiring). Two pre-existing gate-contract tests were updated to admit Gate 14 (`range(15)`, `labels[-1] == "Gate 14"`) — maintenance of a real contract, not a weakened assertion. Deliberately **not** renumbered into the `[N/11]` sequence: those steps run before anything is public and any of them can abort, so numbering this one as a 12th step would misrepresent it as another gate the publish is conditional on. Both copies of `pipeline-rules.md` updated (canonical-pipeline + standardize-plugin) — a stale duplicate re-introduces the defect on the next upgrade, the v3.22.1/v2.137.1 shape — plus the missing `RC-TEST-COVERAGE` row in `plugin-error-index.md`, which had **no entry anywhere the fixer reads**. version 5.1.1, test files 453. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.1.0 — CC spec-drift sync v2.1.219 (a missing hook event, a model family the full-ID regex never learned, a settings key that must NOT be added) + the Rust in-process-spawn FP that was gating a green release (#188)**
— **#188: `std::thread::spawn` scored as shell execution.** SHELL_EXEC catalog pattern index 2 is a bare `\bspawn\s*\(`, aimed at `subprocess.Popen` / `child_process.spawn` / `Command::spawn` and carrying no receiver or language guard — so it matched a Rust thread spawn, which starts an OS thread inside the SAME process: no shell, no `exec`, no child. **Reproduced before touching anything** (a prior report turned out to be already-shipped, so the check is not a formality). It is not cosmetic noise: the finding lands at `medium` → MINOR, and a MINOR blocks `--strict` (exit 3), so one FP gated a release whose CRITICAL and MAJOR counts were both **0**. **The reporter had no fix available on their side, and that is the load-bearing part** — the flagged line is a `#[cfg(test)]` proof that a write lock serializes two concurrent holders, so it REQUIRES a second thread; deleting it would delete the evidence the concurrency gate works, and rewriting correct code to dodge a regex is the obfuscation CPV's own devitalize guidance rules out. Same shape as #184: *there is no source fix — the scope is what's wrong.* Fixed in `_skillaudit_rust_context.py`, not the catalog (so no re2-audit regen is owed), with the family covered rather than the single reported token: `std::thread::spawn`, bare `thread::spawn`, `tokio::spawn`, `rayon::spawn`, `async_std`/`smol`/`glommio` task spawns. **The two shapes are held to DIFFERENT standards of proof, which is the whole design:** a PATH-QUALIFIED spawn clears on its own because the crate path itself names an in-process executor, but a SCOPED handle (`s.spawn(…)` inside `thread::scope(|s| …)`) proves nothing by itself — `cmd.spawn()` on a `Command` is byte-similar and is a real process spawn — so it clears ONLY when a bounded look-back finds a scope opener, and never when it finds a `Command` builder first. **Two-sided through the real scanner, 13/13:** all 8 in-process shapes clear while `Command::new("sh").arg("-c").spawn()`, its multi-line chain, `std::process::Command`, a bare `cmd.spawn()` whose head is a `Command`, `powershell -c`, and a line carrying BOTH a thread spawn and a real `Command` all still fire. **Non-vacuity proven by reverting the classifier: 7 fail, and all 6 must-still-fire controls pass in BOTH states** — which is the half that matters, since a discriminator that cleared every Rust `spawn(` would satisfy the FP tests while opening a security false negative. **One test is honestly labelled a control, not evidence:** `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` passed against the UNFIXED classifier too, because `\bspawn\s*\(` needs `spawn(` immediately and `spawn_blocking(` puts `_blocking` in between — it was never broken, and its docstring says so, so nobody later cites it as proof.
— **v5.1.0's CC spec-sync half:**
— The window v2.1.206→v2.1.221 carries plugin-spec surface in exactly ONE release: **v2.1.219**. Everything here is allowlist-widening or a stale user-facing string — no security gate touched, no `--strict` severity changed, nothing suppressed. **`DirectoryAdded`** (fires after `/add-dir` or the SDK `register_repo_root` registers a working directory mid-session) was absent from `VALID_HOOK_EVENTS`, so a plugin declaring that hook was flagged as using an unknown event. A mechanical set-diff of the doc's event table against CPV's constant then proved the fix complete and the sweep clean: **31 = 31, zero drift in BOTH directions** — nothing in the doc missing from CPV, nothing in CPV absent from the doc. **The near-miss worth recording is the matcher question.** Every other event added in recent sweeps was matcher-less, and I was about to add `DirectoryAdded` to `EVENTS_WITHOUT_MATCHERS` on that pattern alone — but the hooks-doc matcher table gives it `slash_command` / `register_repo_root`, so listing it there would have made CPV flag a CORRECT hook that scopes on how the directory was added. Fetching the raw doc caught it; the inference from the sibling pattern was wrong. A test now pins it OUT of that set, with a control asserting genuinely matcher-less events are still IN (without which the assertion would also pass against an empty set).
— **`claude-fable-5` was rejected while `fable` was accepted.** `VALID_MODELS` has carried the short alias since v2.142.0, but `_FULL_MODEL_ID_RE` enumerated only `opus|sonnet|haiku`, so only the FULL-ID spelling failed — the kind of split that reads as a validator bug to any author who writes the documented id. **The enumeration is deliberate and stays:** it is a hallucinated-family detector, so `claude-gpt-4` and `claude-bogus-5` must keep failing; the fix adds one real family, never a generic `\w+`. That is exactly what the positive controls pin — a regex widened to `claude-\w+-\d` would satisfy every "fable is accepted now" assertion while silently accepting all six bogus ids. Both invalid-model MESSAGES were stale in two ways at once: they enumerated every family EXCEPT `fable` (sending an author to fix something already correct) and cited `claude-opus-4-6`, two generations behind the current `claude-opus-5`.
— **The settings item is half a correction of my own change set.** v2.1.219 adds two settings keys, and I had scoped both for `KNOWN_SETTINGS_KEYS`. Reading the changelog line rather than the note showed the sandbox one is **`sandbox.network.strictAllowlist`** — NESTED. That set models TOP-LEVEL keys only (nested paths live in the `*_NESTED_KEYS` dotted-tuple sets), `sandbox` is already known and its sub-keys are tolerated, so no entry is owed — and a bare `strictAllowlist` entry would have **excused a genuine typo written at the top level**, quietly widening the very check it was meant to sync. Only `workflowSizeGuideline` is genuinely top-level ("settable from any settings file"). A test pins the omission WITH its reason, so the next sync does not helpfully add it back.
— **The error-string fix was incomplete until a repo-wide grep corrected it.** I had scoped TWO emitters of the invalid-model message from reading the change set; `grep -rn "Invalid 'model' value" scripts/` found **FOUR** — `validate_skill_comprehensive.py` carried the identical stale list (and is the one the fixer's own `skill-fixes.md` names as the source), and `validate_agent.py` cited the stale example id. So the test is keyed on an ENUMERATED emitter list plus a guard asserting no FIFTH script emits the message unchecked — otherwise a new validator could ship a stale list while every assertion still passed. The same staleness had reached the two skill reference docs the fixer agents READ (`frontmatter-schema.md` literally taught the pre-fix alternation `claude-(opus|sonnet|haiku)-<digit>`), which is the v2.137.1 shape where a stale canon doc re-introduces on the next upgrade exactly what the code just fixed; both now also list `best`, an alias `is_valid_model` has accepted since v2.160.0 and neither doc mentioned.
— **VERIFIED CLEAN by mechanical set-diff, no change owed:** the TOOLS table (CPV is a deliberate superset, 49 ⊇ 43, nothing in the doc missing); and **`ultracode` is deliberately NOT added to `VALID_EFFORT_VALUES`** — `claude agents --effort ultracode` appears in the window and looks like an enum gap, but cli-reference scopes it to a SESSION flag (v2.1.203+, "starts the session at `xhigh` with ultracode on") while BOTH frontmatter tables (skills.md, sub-agents.md) list exactly `low, medium, high, xhigh, max`, and skills.md states outright that "Ultracode is not a distinct level and reports as `xhigh`". Accepting it in frontmatter would validate a value the spec does not define there. **Reported, not fixed:** 13 documented `CLAUDE_*`/`ANTHROPIC_*` env vars are absent from `VALID_PLUGIN_ENV_VARS` (each a latent "unknown env var" FP for a plugin referencing one) — every one PREDATES v2.1.206, so they are pre-existing drift outside this window rather than something this sync introduced or silently skipped. — **Adding the event was an INCOMPLETE change, and CPV's own suite is what caught it.** `VALID_HOOK_EVENTS` and `validate_hook_output.HOOK_OUTPUT_EVENT_FIELDS` are two halves of one contract, pinned by `test_hook_output_event_fields_covers_all_events` — so registering `DirectoryAdded` without its output schema left the output validator with no row for an event it now considered valid. The full serial suite failed on exactly that, which is the whole reason that invariant test exists. The schema is `frozenset()` **on purpose**: the docs state "DirectoryAdded hooks have no decision control" (it cannot block an add that already completed) and its section documents INPUT fields only — its two siblings `CwdChanged`/`FileChanged` DO carry `watchPaths`, so the tempting move is to give it fields too; a test now pins the empty set with that reason, plus a control asserting the siblings keep theirs so the empty set reads as a fact about this event rather than a broken map.
— **The failure was nearly missed, and the near-miss is worth more than the fix.** The background task reported *"completed (exit code 0)"* while pytest had **failed** — the notification carries the exit code of the LAST command in the chain (`tail`), not pytest's. The real line was `PYTEST_EXIT=1 · 1 failed, 12261 passed`. A recalled memory note ([[lesson-bg-task-exit-code-is-not-pytest]]) is what prompted reading the captured file instead of trusting the notification; without it this release would have shipped a red suite behind a green-looking signal. **Always grep the output file for `failed` AND read the explicitly-captured exit code — never the task notification's.** +20 two-sided tests; **non-vacuity proven by reverting the scripts — 7 fail, and the 9 that still pass are precisely the positive controls and FN-safety guards**, which must pass in both states. version 5.1.0, test files 451, 12286 tests (12283 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed on the authoritative full serial run). [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-bg-task-exit-code-is-not-pytest]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v5.0.0 — BREAKING: the ship-only-binary canon becomes UNIVERSAL, and the fail-safe direction inverts with it**
— **The opt-in was enforcing almost nothing.** Through v4.3.0 the blocking escalation required `cpv.canon: ship-only-binary` in the manifest, so the plugins most in need of migrating were exactly the ones that never opted in — the gate was armed only by authors who had already complied. The owner's directive was that the whole fleet aligns on the canon, so `_canon_strict` is now TRUE by default: `RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY-STRICT` and `RC-ATTEST-MISSING` are publish-blocking MAJORs for **every** compiled plugin. **The ordering is the whole design decision and is why this did not ship in 4.3.0:** enforcing "ship only the binary" before `cpv.attest[]` existed would have forced the fleet to ship binaries nothing could tie to a source revision — CPV's five scanners cannot read native code, so the record IS the audit trail, and an opaque blob is a worse outcome than shipped source. 4.3.0 shipped the record; this release turns on the requirement that makes it checkable. **This DELIBERATELY retro-breaks plugins that are green today**, consciously overriding #170 rather than quietly bending it — which is why the exception is a first-class, greppable declaration rather than a silent tolerance. **`cpv.canon: none`** is the one escape hatch: it withholds the BLOCK, never the finding — every advisory still fires, plus an `RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY-OPTOUT` WARNING naming the exception. It exists because some authors cannot migrate on this release's schedule (a source repo they do not control, a build they cannot yet reproduce in CI), and without it their only recourse would be to stop upgrading CPV — forfeiting every OTHER security fix, which is strictly worse than a visible exception. **The security-relevant part is that the fail-safe direction INVERTS.** `ship_canon_opted_out()` returns False on a missing, malformed, or unreadable manifest — i.e. **ENFORCED**. Under an opt-in a broken manifest degraded to "advisory"; under a mandatory canon it must degrade to "enforced", or a corrupt manifest would buy an exemption. Verified across all five manifest shapes (absent / `none` / legacy opt-in / typo / unreadable). **A defect the flip created and the tests caught:** the escalated finding still read *"the plugin declares cpv.canon: ship-only-binary"* — false under a universal canon, and a finding that misstates its own trigger sends the author to fix the wrong thing; both STRICT messages now state the real trigger and name the escape hatch. A legacy opt-in declaration is reported as redundant (`RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY-DECLARED`, INFO) rather than treated as an error. **Two-sided end-to-end:** default → MAJOR 3→9, exit 2 (blocked); `cpv.canon: none` → back to MAJOR 3 with the attestation findings demoted to WARNING and the OPTOUT warning visible; a source-only plugin is untouched. +13 tests; non-vacuity proven by restoring v4 opt-in semantics (3 behavioural tests fail, 10 controls still pass).
— **#185 §1 — the canon optimised the wrong thing, and the number is the argument.** Committing every platform×arch binary means ~79% of a real install is native code the host can never execute, against the ~1% source-stripping saves. New `cpv_platform_delivery.py` groups shipped binaries into `<tool>-<os>-<arch>` families, computes the unusable fraction, and emits `RC-PLATFORM-BLOAT` carrying the real MB and % — **WARNING, never blocking**, because unlike every other rule in this release there is no defect: the plugin did exactly what the canon asked, so failing it would punish compliance. `RC-PLATFORM-DELIVERY-OK` (INFO) recognises the cheaper shape — a dispatcher plus a checksum-verified release-asset download — reusing `validate_plugin._has_release_asset_installer` rather than forking it. The new `per-platform-delivery.md` states the tradeoff honestly: fetch-on-install is preferred where a network exists at install time, and neither shape is a security downgrade **provided the checksum is verified** — an unverified download would be strictly worse than committing the binary. +27 tests.
— **#180 — FIXED at v3.22.0, plus the residual that triage found.** The reporter pins `@v3.5.0`, which predates the budget, the `tee`, and the phase markers, so nothing they observed is reproducible on their tag. Verified first-hand at HEAD: one deadline computed before the loop and passed to every file, an overrun reported SKIPPED and never cached, and WARNING never blocks. **But the deadline gated task START only** — `_check_one` consulted it once, at entry, so the retry ladder and the per-host semaphore queue ran unchecked behind it; on a fully-timing-out strict host that is ~55 s per URL (5 attempts × 8 s + backoff) with up to 16 started tasks draining 2-at-a-time, overshooting the 300 s ceiling by minutes. Now re-checked per attempt AND after acquiring the semaphore. The **first** attempt stays exempt on purpose: a task admitted under budget must get one real request, or a URL could be reported SKIPPED having never been tried at all. +4 tests, non-vacuity proven.
— **#185 §5/§6/§7 — the three remaining holes the canon itself opened**, in one standalone `cpv_ship_canon.py`. **§5 extracted-repo rot:** `cpv.strip.extract[]` records `{path,url,sha}` for source moved out of the tree, and nothing ever checked those still resolve — `RC-EXTRACT-MALFORMED` is offline and structural, `RC-EXTRACT-ROT` needs `network=True` and is **OPT-IN**, because an offline or pre-install scan must never fail on connectivity. But a check that could not run must not read as clean either, so an uncontacted record emits an explicit `RC-EXTRACT-UNVERIFIED` INFO rather than silence — the [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] shape, applied at design time instead of after an incident. **§6 licence gating:** shipping only a binary drops the source tree's LICENSE, so the artifact users install can carry no licence at all — `RC-BINARY-NO-LICENCE` fires only when compiled artifacts actually ship, reusing `iter_shipped_binaries` rather than re-deriving magic-byte detection. **§7 build-graph role, the verified defect:** the compiled-source rule keys on file EXTENSION (`compiled_languages` maps `.rs`/`.go`/…), so a `dist/` bundle whose source is `.ts` was never caught at all; `classify_build_role` classifies by ROLE — build OUTPUT signals (`dist/`, `target/release/`, `.min.js`, a sourcemap sibling) versus INPUT signals (a `package.json` entry point, `tsconfig` include, `Cargo.toml`) — and `RC-BUILD-OUTPUT-SHIPS-SOURCE` fires when both an output and its generating source ship together. +119 two-sided tests; non-vacuity proven in BOTH directions — three under-fire neuters (38/8/12 in-scope failures, 0 out-of-scope) and four over-fire guard removals that break the negative siblings, which is the half that usually goes unproven.
— **#182's open half — the per-file scan bound is now DEFAULT-ON.** v4.2.0 fixed the verdict (a file whose scan did not complete BLOCKS instead of passing) but left the wait unbounded, because bounding it correctly needed a measurement first: the supervisor costs a FIXED ~0.2–0.28 s process spawn independent of N, so supervising unconditionally taxes every small scan (serial 0.003 s → supervised 0.200 s at 3 files). Routing therefore engages when `files >= 32` **or** any single file is 1 MiB–8 MiB — the two shapes where a wedge is plausible and the fixed cost amortises. 32 mirrors `validate_security`'s existing `CPV_PARALLEL_SCAN_THRESHOLD`, and the 8 MiB ceiling mirrors `MAX_SCAN_BYTES`, so the bound engages exactly where the parallel path already does rather than inventing a third notion of "big". **The override is clamped, not merely validated:** `min(value, 32)` means `CPV_SCAN_SUPERVISE_MIN_FILES` can only LOWER the threshold (more supervision) — verified first-hand that 999999 clamps to 32 and that zero/negative/unparseable all fall back — so no environment setting can disable the bound, which is the property that distinguishes a guard from a suggestion. +53 tests.
— **#181 — PARTIAL, and it corrected two of CPV's own claims.** The reporter's "the hang is a pool with ZERO workers" inference is **unfounded**: a live `resource_tracker` with no workers beside it is the normal steady state after any pool drains, and their own stack frame (`_queue_SimpleQueue_get`) is what `ThreadPoolExecutor` workers block on, not a process pool. REPO LINT is thread-based (`cpv_lint_engine.py:2418`, zero `multiprocessing` references in the file), so the pool they sampled belonged to a different in-flight phase — the v4.1.0 note was right, though it cited `:2415`, the comment block. **Correcting v4.1.0 in the other direction:** it credited v3.24.0's spawn pin with satisfying #181's "switch to the API that raises", but `ProcessPoolExecutor` was **already** the API at the reporter's own pin (`git show v3.19.0:scripts/cpv_parallel_runner.py`) — that half was never broken. What genuinely remains is the unbounded per-file scan wait, which is #182's open half.

**v4.3.0 — a walker that could not see a directory told 11 shipped binaries to "compile before use" (#187), the release path stops sweeping untracked files into a public commit (#186), and the ship-only-binary canon gets the verifiability it was missing: `cpv.attest[]` (#185 §2/§3)**
— **#187: one bug, and the reporter's two findings were the same bug.** They measured a contradiction rather than diagnosing it — the validator said *"12 Rust source file(s) with build system but no pre-compiled binaries in bin/. Users will need to compile before use"* while `ls bin/` at the same commit listed 11 committed binaries covering all 5 targets, 154 MB, and explicitly said *"I did not read the detector's implementation, so this is reported as a verified output-vs-filesystem contradiction rather than a diagnosed root cause."* Their guess — a naming convention the `pss-<os>-<arch>` scheme does not match — was wrong, and the real cause is one layer down: **`GitignoreFilter.rglob` never yielded a directory.** It pushed a dir onto its descent stack but never tested it against the pattern, so `rglob("bin")` returned `[]` for a real, tracked, populated `bin/` — reproduced in three lines before anything was changed. Two consequences, and the second is the one nobody had noticed: `has_bin` was False for **every** plugin, and the entire platform-coverage section returned early on an empty `all_bin_dirs`, so **CPV had never once checked binary platform coverage** — dead code that now runs and reports (verified: `Found 5 compiled binary file(s)` / `cover recommended platforms: …`). The method's own docstring claimed `Path.rglob` semantics, which is exactly what made it invisible: an empty iterator is indistinguishable from "the tree really has none", so no call site could tell. **The fix is at the walker, not the caller** — the same defect was live at both `bin` sites, and patching only the reported one would have left the platform check dead. Matching pathlib means every caller now sees dirs too, so the seven that require files carry an explicit `is_file()` guard, making each caller's intent visible instead of relying on a silent quirk. **FN-safety is the property under test:** a gitignored `bin/` is still pruned (that pruning is why this class exists), and nothing green can go red — the newly-live section emits only WARNING/INFO/PASSED, and WARNING never blocks even under `--strict`. Two-sided end-to-end against a stashed baseline: before, the reporter's exact sentence; after, gone, with the binaries reported instead. **A near-miss worth recording: my first test harness read a `findings` key the validator does not emit** (it is `results`), so it returned `[]` for every run and the "this message must be ABSENT" test **passed while proving nothing** — the three sibling tests asserting presence failed loudly, which is the only reason it surfaced. The harness now carries a non-vacuity guard and that absence test carries a positive control. Non-vacuity proven by reverting the walker: 9 fail, the 4 FN-safety controls still pass. #187 also asked for a docs fix, and it is the more expensive half: `compiled-component-canon.md` promised G2e/G2f build gates in "the generated `publish.py`", so a maintainer naturally diffs against `<cpv>/scripts/publish.py` — which is **CPV's own pipeline, not a template**, contains none of them, and produced a 1805-vs-3236-line diff with 40+ functions apparently missing, reading as catastrophic drift and inviting a destructive wholesale port. Both the canon doc and the skill now name **`gen_publish_py()` as the canon**, give the command to generate the real comparand, and lead with *determine your PROFILE first — a diff against the wrong profile is meaningless*.
— **#186: `git add -A` in the release path.** Three sites, all verified verbatim before editing. A release commit is the worst possible place for an accidental inclusion — it is pushed to a public repo AND it is the artifact users install, so once it lands, forks and caches make it unrecoverable in practice; and a plugin tree at release time routinely holds `reports/` (CPV's own convention: "routinely contain private data — absolute paths, usernames, internal hostnames, proprietary source, tokens caught in logs"). New `stage_release_changes()` stages tracked modifications (`git add -u`) plus the pipeline's own generated files **by name**, then enumerates `git status --porcelain` and prints whatever is left untracked instead of absorbing it. That is safe **here specifically** because the clean-tree gate already ran: everything legitimate is committed by then, so anything still untracked is by construction not part of the release — and the failure mode is deliberately "your new file was not included, here it is by name" (loud, cheap) rather than "your scratch directory was published" (permanent). **The third site matters most: it is in the GENERATOR**, so every plugin CPV scaffolds inherited blanket staging in its own release path. Then the sweep nobody asked for but the rule demands — CPV was still **teaching** the antipattern in twelve places it prints or documents (`generate_plugin_repo`/`generate_marketplace_repo` scaffold instructions, the marketplace setup guide, the validator procedures checklist, the create-plugin skill, and — the one that would have re-introduced the bug on the next upgrade, exactly the v2.137.1 shape — the canon reference describing the release commit as `git add -A`). All now stage by name.
— **#185 §2/§3: the canon mandated an artifact CPV cannot read, and nothing tied it to any source.**
— **The canon mandated an artifact CPV cannot read, and nothing tied it to any source.** The perfect-skill-suggester Claude filed a seven-point design review of ship-only-binary, from the plugin that is its canonical test case. The load-bearing point: shipping only a binary makes the executable **unverifiable by construction** — `cpv.strip.extract[]` records `{path,url,sha}`, but nothing ever checked that the binary in `bin/` was built from that sha. **Not hypothetical: they hit it the same day.** Their `publish.py` staged only `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` inside the `rust/` submodule, and a rebuilt binary was about to ship while its `main.rs` change was never committed — the shipped binary would have contained a prompt-injection sanitizer that **existed in no repository**. They caught it only because the source was still in-tree and `git status` showed it; under ship-only-binary that discrepancy is structurally invisible. Compounding it (§3), CPV's five scanners are all blind to native code, so the canon reduced assurance exactly where risk is highest. **The ordering matters and is the whole design decision:** the user's directive was that all plugins align on the canon, and the defensible reading is that alignment REQUIRES attestation first — flipping the canon universal before it exists would have made every compiled plugin in the fleet ship an unverifiable blob, which is the opposite of what a security validator is for. So v4.3.0 ships the record; the universal flip is the next release. New `cpv_binary_attestation.py` + `cpv.attest[]`: per artifact a `sha256`, full `source_commit`, `toolchain` and `build_cmd`, verified against the bytes on disk. **What it proves is scoped honestly in the module docstring** — the manifest is COMPLETE and CURRENT (every shipped binary has a record; the hash still matches, which is exactly what catches the PSS shape), NOT that the bytes came from that commit, which only a rebuild can show and belongs in release CI. Detection is by **magic bytes, not the executable bit** (a checkout can lose the mode) with a compiled-suffix short-circuit, so a `launcher.sh` sitting beside the binaries is correctly NOT attested — it is scannable source. **WARNING, deliberately:** a plugin published before this format existed cannot retroactively carry it, so blocking on arrival would retro-break every compiled plugin (#170); `attest --emit` makes compliance mechanical. **The emitter cannot certify itself** — it fills `path`/`sha256`/`source_commit` but leaves `toolchain`/`build_cmd` as TODO placeholders that FAIL validation, because a fabricated build command is worse than an absent one: it looks like a verified fact. A malformed record is reported (`RC-ATTEST-MALFORMED`) and still does not satisfy its binary, so an incomplete entry cannot launder an artifact past `RC-ATTEST-MISSING`. **Verified in code, not assumed:** §7's claim that the rule keys on file EXTENSION is true (`compiled_languages` maps `.rs`/`.go`/…, so a `dist/` bundle whose source is `.ts` is never caught), and §4's cross-tool contradiction is verbatim real — `ai-maestro-janitor`'s `repo-trust-score.py` lists `bin` in `_SUSPICIOUS_PARENTS` (`:82`) and `.exe`/`.dll`/`.so`/`.dylib` in `_SUSPICIOUS_BIN_SUFFIXES` (`:72`), scoring +1 per blob and +1 more for its parent dir (`:142`), so CPV mandates the exact shape the janitor penalises. Scoped honestly on re-reading the collector: it keys on SUFFIX, so extensionless `pss-darwin-arm64` artifacts do not count and only the `.exe`/`.so`/`.dylib` ones do — 4 points of the threshold-8 budget for a 2-Windows-binary plugin, a real contribution rather than an automatic trip. +28 two-sided tests (attestation) +13 (#187) +14 (#186). version 4.3.0, scripts 135, test files 445. Still open from #185: §1 per-platform delivery (79% of a PSS install is native code the host can never run, vs the 1% source-stripping saves), §4 janitor reconciliation, §5 extracted-repo rot, §6 licence/NOTICE gating, §7 build-graph-role reframing. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[claim-verification]]

**v4.2.1 — archiving a finished TRDD no longer turns a green plugin red: all FOUR design/ lifecycle zones are one corpus (#184)**
— **Obeying the governance rules was failing the gate.** `ai-maestro-plugin` was exit 0 on `--strict`, then archived 12 terminal TRDDs — a pure `git mv` from `design/tasks/` → `design/archived/`, which `git show --stat` records as `1 file changed, 0 insertions(+), 0 deletions(-)` — and went to exit 4 with NIT=3. Archival is not an edge case: it is the **routine terminal transition of every finished card**, mandated by the TRDD folder lifecycle `ai-maestro-janitor` ships to `~/.claude/rules/` on every machine. And the reporter had no source fix available, because `trdd-design-tasks` §12 **freezes terminal TRDD bodies** — rewording an archived card to please a scanner would corrupt a frozen project record. That is CPV's own #176 case-2 shape one layer up: *there is no source fix — the scope is what's wrong.* They refused to add a suppression and left their gate honestly red, which was the right call. **REPRODUCED AT HEAD before a line was changed**, with a fixture carrying the reporter's three verbatim lines: `design/tasks/` → NIT=0, `design/archived/` → NIT=3 (`FS_WRITE`, `PRIVILEGE_ESC`, `CMD_INJECTION`), identical bytes, one variable. **My first diagnosis was WRONG and probing is what caught it.** Static reading found `design/tasks` keyed literally in four places and I concluded `_DEV_SCRATCH_DIR_PARTS` governed only EXTERNAL scanners (I had followed it to its `check_tirith_scanner` call site) while the other three sites were hash-anchored to CPV's own manifest and so could not touch a third-party plugin — leaving no candidate that explained the symptom. Probing `scan_content` directly showed it fires **identically in all five zones**, so the decision had to be downstream in a consumer; `validate_plugin._run_skillaudit_native._should_skip` (`:2471`) calls `_is_dev_scratch_path` on **IN-PROCESS skillaudit** findings too. One list, one missing zone. The reporter had guessed the mechanism exactly (*"if the exclusion is keyed on a literal `design/tasks` rather than the corpus, that is the whole gap"*) while explicitly declining to assert it — *"the symptom locates the bug, the diagnosis does not survive being assumed"* — and they were right where my reading was not. Fix: one `_TRDD_LIFECYCLE_DIR_PARTS` SSOT covering `proposals/tasks/archived/refused`, applied at every site including the ones that could not be the cause (each carried the identical latent bug for CPV's own archived cards) and mirrored in `_plugin_compute_hashes.py`, which must stay import-free of the validator so the manifest builds standalone — the duplication is deliberate and pinned by a parity test. **Grants nothing new:** the `trdd-` filename gate and the hash anchoring are retained, so this recognises the SAME documents in their other three folders. **Two-sided, and the control is the point:** all four zones → NIT=0 while a non-lifecycle sibling `design/notes/` still fires all three, so the fix can never decay into a blanket `design/` mute; non-vacuity proven by reverting the source (14 fail, 21 controls still pass). **The reusable form, which is the part worth keeping:** *when a scope exclusion names ONE member of a set the ecosystem defines as a set, the excluded member is a coincidence of which zone existed first and the others are a latent gate failure — armed the first time anyone follows the lifecycle to its end.* +35 two-sided tests. version 4.2.1, test files 442. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-verify-fp-scope-before-category-fix]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]]

**v4.2.0 — a file whose security scan did NOT complete now BLOCKS instead of passing (#182)**
— **The bug was live, needed no timeout to trigger, and I reproduced it with an existing env knob before writing a line of code.** `parallel_scan` returns `ScanResult(findings=[], error=…)` when a per-file worker dies or is killed — and `findings=[]` is indistinguishable downstream from "this file is clean". Both SECURITY sinks leaned on that: `validate_security` reported a **WARNING**, which `exit_code_strict` documents as never blocking *"even in strict mode"*; `cpv_skillaudit_native` reported `severity:"low"` → NIT, which blocks only under `--strict` — and the case with the most at stake, scanning an untrusted third-party plugin **before installing it**, runs in DEFAULT mode. So the one mode that mattered most was the one that passed. **PROOF, measured not argued:** a fixture plugin whose `curl … | sh` payload sat in a `SKILL.md` padded until its scan took 7.27 s went from `MAJOR:4 NIT:3 WARNING:0` to `MAJOR:4 NIT:2 **WARNING:1**` when that one scan was killed — the payload's blocking finding replaced by one that cannot block. A plugin author controls how slow their own files are to scan, so this is an **attacker-triggerable mute**, not merely an availability bug. After the fix the same kill yields `MAJOR:5 WARNING:0` — blocking. **The fix that the handoff told me to make was WRONG, and checking is what caught it.** The recorded next action was "bound the wait — `future.result(timeout=…)` at all 14 `parallel_scan` call sites". `ProcessPoolExecutor` cannot cancel a *started* task: after the timeout the loop moves on, but the `with` block's `shutdown(wait=True)` then blocks on the wedged worker anyway, so the hang is **relocated, not removed** — the module's own comment at `cpv_parallel_runner.py:191-197` already said so, which is why `cpv_scan_supervisor` exists. Worse, since v3.22.2 the release gate is fail-closed on a killed job, so today's hang **blocks**; a timeout whose outcome was a WARNING would have converted a blocking failure into a pass. **Doing only the "bound" half would have manufactured the exact bypass #182 asks not to be built.** So the verdict semantics are fixed first, and alone. **What makes fail-closed fair:** a new shared `retry_failed_serially` re-runs a **crashed** item once in-process, so a transient CI OOM-kill cannot fail an honest plugin and only a *reproducible* failure is reported — but a **timeout is never retried**, because re-running a wedge in this process would hang the main thread with nothing left to preempt it, and a timeout is already proof the work does not terminate. It must run INSIDE the existing `try`, since both workers read `_WORKER_ENV_*` that the `finally` restores. Also corrected a comment that had been wrong since it was written: skillaudit's `"low"` was annotated "emitted as WARNING-class" when `_SEVERITY_MAP` maps it to **nit**. **Measurements** (CPV's own 1083-file tree, cache off): worst single file 27.13 s with google-re2, **28.25 s without** — the no-re2 CI path is only ~20 % slower in aggregate and its max is within 5 %, so the catalog's re2-safety work holds and a future bound is well constrained. **Deliberately NOT bundled:** making a bound default-on (the right lever is the existing supervisor's `hard_kill_after_s`, already env-wired as `CPV_SCAN_SKIP_STUCK_AFTER`; it changes the default pool path so it needs its own overhead measurement plus a Linux fork-parity pass), and converting the REPO LINT / URL phase budgets to FAIL (those run style linters and dead-link checks, not the security scanners — skipping them hides no threat, and #162 showed a cold-runner storm would false-block). +14 two-sided tests; non-vacuity proven by neutering all three changes at once (4 fail, the 10 controls still pass). version 4.2.0, test files 441. [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-bounded-per-item-unbounded-in-aggregate]]

**v4.1.0 — `--force-templates` stops DELETING a compiled plugin's release machinery, and a blocking preflight gate stops hiding the errors (#183)**
— **THE DESTRUCTIVE CASE, MEASURED on a real perfect-skill-suggester clone before any code was written:** `standardize --fix --force-templates` replaced PSS's 1506-line hand-written `scripts/publish.py` with canon's 2392-line one and took every Rust release behavior to ZERO — `_ensure_submodule_pushed` 2→0, `CARGO_LOCK` 6→0, `CARGO_TOML` 4→0, `rustup` 3→0, the second crate 11→0. The worst single loss is `_ensure_submodule_pushed()`, which refuses to release while the parent references an UNPUSHED submodule commit; without it the plugin can tag a release whose submodule ref nobody can resolve, and **Claude Code recurses submodules on install**, so every user's install breaks. **The surprise is that CPV already thought it had handled this:** `gen_publish_py` IS profile-aware (v2.140.0 / TRDD-e9f13df1) and DOES emit a `submodule-build` variant — but only the gitlink / submodule-push mechanics, never the toolchain steps beside them, which I confirmed by generating all three variants and counting the markers (submodule-build: 52 `submodule` hits, **0** of the five Rust markers). The existing `--force-templates` guard has exactly two skip conditions — a manifest `intentional_divergence` entry and a `_classify_drift_direction` of `ahead`/`mixed` — and PSS declares neither while its publish.py classifies `behind`, so it was overwritten. `_classify_drift_direction` keys on HARDENING markers; a cargo/rustup step is not one, so it is structurally blind to this. **The fix declines the overwrite for the two files that carry RELEASE MACHINERY** (`scripts/publish.py`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`) when the plugin builds a compiled artifact — signalled EITHER by a declared profile canon cannot fully model (`submodule-build`, `binary-release`) OR, **intrinsically, by `plugin_ships_compiled`**, because a plugin that never declared a profile is exactly the one nobody warned. `remote-validation` is deliberately EXCLUDED (gen_publish_py documents that its gate shape already lives in the standard template, so canon genuinely models it) and `ci.yml`/`cliff.toml` are excluded (toolchain-agnostic, fully modelled; the two config files already MERGE rather than clobber). **Selector, not suppressor** — it only DECLINES a destructive write, relaxes no gate, and the validator's drift WARNING still fires; deleting the file and re-running remains the documented escape hatch, since the create path always writes.
