Fix (Phase 1 of the larger canon — the user then set the deeper rule that compiled components must build-and-ship-ONLY-the-binary, source in a SEPARATE repo; see the approved plan): added **self-detecting** gates to the generated `publish.py` `run_gate` template — `G2e` (Rust: globs `Cargo.toml`, top-level-manifest filter for workspaces, runs `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `cargo test`) and `G2f` (shell: globs `*.sh`/`*.bash`, runs `shellcheck`), both between G2d/mypy and G3/validate. They self-detect at the GENERATED plugin's runtime (no Cargo.toml/`*.sh` → clean skip; language present but tool absent → WARN+skip, the build pipeline/CI backstops; tool ran + found issues → BLOCK) — the exact degrade-if-absent idiom of the existing G2b(jscpd)/G2c(actionlint) gates, so a missing toolchain never false-blocks a push. Also added `cargo clippy -D warnings` + `cargo test` + `submodules: recursive` to `gen_release_binaries_yml`'s build workflow (part of #175's "CI/release workflows" ask; ready for the Phase-3 wiring). +8 two-sided tests (`TestPublishPyLanguageGatesIssue175`: gates present/ordered/self-detect-skip/degrade/block + the release-workflow gates). Generated publish.py compiles; ruff+self-validate clean. Shell CI was already gated by Mega-Linter `BASH_SHELLCHECK` + `cpv_ci_preflight._gate_shellcheck`; this adds the local pre-push shell check. #175 stays OPEN — Phases 2–6 (the source-exclusion WARN→migrate→BLOCK canon + PSS-compat proof) follow. version 3.6.0, test files 414. [[lesson-verify-issue-is-not-already-shipped]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v3.5.0 — `/cpv-agent` direct-entry slash command (user directive)**
— user: "make the cpv-agent callable from both user and agents. ensure i can do `/cpv-agent update the pipeline`." The `cpv-agent` AGENT already existed and was agent-dispatchable — that surface is IMPLICIT: any `agents/*.md` with a `description` is callable via the Agent tool as `subagent_type: claude-plugins-validation:cpv-agent`, no frontmatter flag. The MISSING surface was the USER slash: agents have NO slash surface at all; a `/name` the user types exists ONLY if there is a `commands/<name>.md`. Added `commands/cpv-agent.md` — frontmatter `user-invocable: true` + `argument-hint`; the BODY dispatches `$ARGUMENTS` to the `claude-plugins-validation:cpv-agent` subagent (body-dispatch, matching the 10 `cpv-batch-*` commands — NOT a legacy `agent:` frontmatter field, which CC does not reliably auto-forward and which v2.90.0's `AGENT_COMMANDS == {}` asserts unused). Now reachable THREE ways: user `/cpv-agent <req>`, model auto-invoke (no `disable-model-invocation`), and other agents via `subagent_type`. There is NO `agent-invocable` field — `user-invocable` is the only invocation flag. Extended the THREE command-allowlist gates that pin the command set (2 allowlists in `test_consolidation_v211.py` + `test_menu_unification_v290.py` + `test_menu_visibility.py`) with `cpv-agent.md`; +1 coverage test file (`test_cpv_agent_command.py`, 7 two-sided tests). A command and an agent may share the name — CPV's ONLY command-name collision check is vs built-in slash commands (`/clear`, `/loop`, …), never command↔agent. `validate_command` → 7 PASS / 0 findings; gate subset 74 green. commands 14, test files 414. Frontmatter/invocation mechanics captured in wikimem [[agent-vs-command-invocation-surfaces]]. version 3.5.0. [[maintain-project-claude-md]] [[menu-unification-v290]]

**v3.4.0 — formatter canon: CPV recommends LINTERS, never FORMATTERS (user directive)**
— user: "publish a version of the plugin that will use a canon where publish.py won't use formatters, only linters. `ruff check --fix`, not `ruff format`. markdown lint, without `--fix`." Root observation: publish.py / the fixer agents were reaching for markdown linters WITH `--fix` and markdown FORMATTERS, which reflow and BREAK the structured Markdown that skills / agents / TRDDs / wikimem / obsidian notes / YAML frontmatter depend on. Verified first (claim-verification): the PIPELINE itself is already report-only — `cpv_lint_engine` runs `ruff check` (no `--fix`, no format) + `markdownlint-cli2` (report), and generated CI is `APPLY_FIXES: none`. The formatter MANDATES lived in CPV's CANON (generated templates + pipeline standard + a JSON fixer step), so the fix is to purge them there and make the rule EXPLICIT for the fixer agents. THE CANON (v3.4.0): NEVER a formatter on ANY file (`ruff format` / `prettier` / `mdformat` / any markdown formatter); Markdown / mdz / docs / TRDD / wikimem → lint-only, fix findings BY HAND, never `markdownlint --fix`; Python / JS/TS → the LINTER autofix is fine (`ruff check --fix` / `eslint --fix`) but never the FORMATTER; JSON / YAML / config → lint/report only, no `prettier --write`. Changes (7 files): generated README + setup-plugin template (`ruff format` → `ruff check --fix` + a canon note), canonical-pipeline CI standard (dropped `ruff format --check` + `prettier --check` from the CI step lists), fix-validation JSON procedure (`prettier --write` → jsonlint/`json.tool` report + fix-by-hand), a prominent "Formatter canon" section in the shared `cpv-fix-validation` SKILL.md, and an explicit never-format guardrail in BOTH fixer agents (`cpv-plugin-fixer-agent`, `cpv-marketplace-fixer-agent` — the named culprits). +4 regression-lock tests (`TestFormatterCanon`: generated README lints-never-formats, CI standard has no formatter STEP, the skill carries the canon, both fixer agents forbid formatting). Dogfooded: the changed `.md` self-validate 0/0/0/0 (the canon applied to CPV's own Markdown); ruff + mypy CI-parity clean (132 files); focused slice 516 green. A pure canon/docs + guardrail change — no pipeline logic altered (it was already compliant). version 3.4.0, scripts 127, test files 413. [[feedback_linters_not_formatters_never_fix]] [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v3.3.0 — RC-165 dependency agent-context-writer detector (closes #174; autonomous go-on-yourself feature; user: security-first, zero suppressions)**
— a NEW security-DETECTION rule (FN-safe; ADD-ONLY — nothing suppressed, no gate relaxed). CPV already flags a plugin's OWN components that write files an agent later loads as instructions (RC-164); this closes the sibling gap raised by AgentlensPro: the same write-capability can arrive from a plugin's **dependency**. A plugin that declares `playwright` inherits the `playwright init-agents` CLI able to write `.claude/agents/*.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.github/agents/*.agent.md` — and, with no `--loop`, `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` (control falls through to the Copilot generator, so a dependency's CLI can introduce a GitHub Actions workflow). New `scripts/cpv_dependency_agent_writers.py`, wired as RC-165 in `validate_security.py` (new `check_dependency_agent_writers` + Phase-2f orchestration step). THE THREE-STATE RULE (issue #174 — score capability vs live SEPARATELY, never a flat critical that trains readers to discount the detector): state 1 a known-writer dep DECLARED → WARNING (capability present, opt-in command, not blocking — playwright is a hugely common legit test dep); state 2 an install-time trigger → CRITICAL, in two forms — 2a the plugin's OWN `package.json` lifecycle script (`preinstall`/`install`/`postinstall`/`prepare`/`prepublishOnly`) INVOKES the writer command, 2b the declared writer dep ITSELF auto-runs at install (recorded per named-list entry — playwright ships `scripts:{}` so `install_trigger=False` — plus a node_modules escalation when a NEWER installed version added an install hook); state 3 the writer's DISTINCTIVE consumer-footprint artifacts present in the tree → MAJOR. UNINSTALLED-SAFE ([[feedback-cpv-scans-uninstalled-plugins]]): the baseline reads only always-present files — declared manifests via the shared `cpv_sbom_writer.iter_dependencies` (which already skips `node_modules`), the root `package.json` scripts, and the on-disk tree; it NEVER gates on `node_modules` being present. TARGETED, not a node_modules scan: state 1/2b key on a curated `KNOWN_AGENT_CONTEXT_WRITERS` named list (playwright is entry one; the reporter's "one list plus one guard" primitive) matched against DECLARED dep names — the only `node_modules` read is one named package's `package.json` for the 2b escalation. LOW-FP state 3: the artifact globs are anchored to the writer's distinctive consumer paths a well-formed plugin never ships (a plugin uses `agents/`, not `.claude/agents/`); the plugin's legit root `.mcp.json` is DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED (named in the capability text, never a state-3 signal). Each state is emitted at its INTRINSIC tier — deliberately NOT through `effective_severity` (its `.md`→`is_doc_path` demotion would wrongly downgrade a `.claude/agents/planner.md` agent-instruction artifact "because it is markdown"; the anchored globs mean an artifact can never live in a sample subdir, so the demotion buys no FP reduction — keeping a bundled example's genuine install trigger at CRITICAL is the security-first call). CPV self-scan clean (declares no known writer; no distinctive artifacts). +17 two-sided tests (`test_cpv_dependency_agent_writers.py`) — every positive has a minimal-mutation negative sibling (non-writer dep, non-lifecycle script, `scripts:{}` dep, legit root `.mcp.json`, pypi-playwright ecosystem-mismatch); ruff clean; new + RC-164 sibling suite 60 green; cold-cache strict self-validate 0/0/0/0. **ALSO in 3.3.0 — dependency `version` semver-range FALSE-POSITIVE fix** (user directive "improve CPV's handling of dependencies … [expanded spec]"): the spec says `version` accepts "any expression supported by Node's `semver` package" (plugin-dependencies.md:44-52), but `cpv_dependency_schema.is_valid_semver_range`'s single-atom regex WRONGLY rejected (emitted a spurious MAJOR on `.version`) valid node-semver ranges a plugin author routinely writes — x-ranges (`1.x`/`1.2.x`/`1.*`/`x`), the `*` wildcard, **space-separated comparator SETS** (`>=1.2.3 <2.0.0`, the AND form), and a `v`-prefixed version. Rewrote the validator to node-semver's range grammar (`||`-separated range-sets → hyphen range OR whitespace-separated comparator set → operator + partial with `_XR` x-range components), re2-safe. Two-sided proven: all 26 valid npm ranges now accepted (0 FP), all 15 junk strings still rejected (0 FN — a bare/doubled operator, `1..2`, `1.2.3foo`, `1 . 2 . 3`, empty OR alternative, non-ASCII). Shared schema, so BOTH `validate_plugin` + `validate_marketplace` deps benefit; 438 dependency/plugin/marketplace tests green + 3 new regression-lock cases in `test_dependency_schema.py`. A pure FP reduction — no gate relaxed (junk still MAJOR). version 3.3.0, scripts 127, test files 413. [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[feedback-cpv-scans-uninstalled-plugins]] [[feedback-opus-for-security-analysis]] [[lesson-verify-issue-is-not-already-shipped]] [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v3.2.1 — skillaudit shell CMD_INJECTION false-negative: a benign co-located command substitution suppressed a DANGEROUS sibling (autonomous ensemble-scan finding under go-on-yourself; user: "keep security as first priority. never allow suppressions")**
— a security DETECTION fix (FN-safe; NO rule suppressed, NO gate relaxed — it strictly TIGHTENS an over-broad suppression). An ensemble scan of the per-language classifiers + a direct probe confirmed that `_skillaudit_shell_context._is_shell_literal_arg_cmdsub` and `_cmdsub_is_safe_data_command` returned True on ANY safe command substitution anywhere on the line (`$(uname -m)` / `$(ls)` / `$(cat "literal")`) WITHOUT verifying the CMD_INJECTION catalog match actually lay inside that safe cmdsub — the `_is_shell_literal_arg_cmdsub` loop even carried a self-incriminating comment ("Verify the match span overlaps … We accept any cmdsub on the line as a positive signal"). So a dangerous sibling on the SAME line — `$(uname -m) && $($CMD arg)` (variable-program cmdsub) or `x=$(uname -m); $INJECT` (bare variable execution) — was SUPPRESSED, a false-negative letting the threat through. The whole-line danger guards (`_SHELL_INTERPRETER_PIPE_RE` for `| bash`/`| sh`, `_CMDSUB_EXEC_WRAP_RE` for eval/bash-c/source, sensitive-path, exfil-sink, secret-to-URL) caught the OBVIOUS shapes — so the two clearest ensemble examples (`… | bash`, `eval …`) were false alarms, correctly ruled out by probe — but the residual co-located gap was REAL (reproduced at both the helper level and the `classify()` dispatch level). FIX: both helpers now SCOPE the suppression to the catalog match's own span — suppress ONLY when the matched token lies inside a safe cmdsub; if any occurrence of the match falls outside every safe span, keep the finding VISIBLE. The `_cmdsub_is_safe_data_command` rewrite (iterate ALL cmdsubs instead of returning True on the first safe one) also fixes a latent ordering bug where a safe cmdsub appearing BEFORE a secret-leaking net-capture cmdsub suppressed the line early. `_pipe_to_text_processor` deliberately LEFT as-is — its FN is blocked by the interpreter-pipe guard and was NOT reproducible (probe showed `pipe=False`); refactoring its fiddly pipe-segment span logic for a theoretical gap would add risk, not close a bug. Two-sided verified through the real `classify()` dispatch: the 3 dangerous co-located cases now fire (`!= safe_literal`) while `ARCH="$(uname -m)"`, `pid=$(cat "/p/f")`, and a benign net-capture `$(curl -s https://api.github.com/…)` all stay suppressed (ZERO FP regression). +6 regression-lock tests (`TestShellCmdsubMatchSpanScoping`); FULL SERIAL suite 11084 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed (no existing FP-suppression test regressed — the tightening reintroduced ZERO FPs); ruff clean; cold-cache strict self-validate 0/0/0/0. Two ensemble FALSE POSITIVES were verified + REJECTED: `_skillaudit_json_context._is_api_field_name_json` (deepseek said CLEAN; the substring-clear was already scoped) and the rust variable-program `Command::new` (mimo said CLEAN). NOT a `--strict` relaxation anywhere. version 3.2.1, scripts 126, test files 412. [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[feedback-opus-for-security-analysis]] [[claim-verification]] [[llm-ext-ensemble-too-slow-for-multifile-scans]] [[lesson-greedy-match-truncates-right-context]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v3.2.0 — autonomous production-readiness pass (user: "do as you think is better. i trust your judgement. keep security as first priority. never allow suppressions"): a taint-engine detection FALSE-NEGATIVE closed + dead-code cleanup + the previously-unshipped CC v2.1.217-218 spec-sync/robustness/experimental-generators**
— ships three unreleased commits, security-STRENGTHENING and suppression-free. **`fix(taint)` (55ffadbd):** `cpv_taint_engine._process_augassign` handled value types Name/Call/Subscript but NOT `ast.Attribute`, while the plain-assign path (`_process_assignment`, the `isinstance(value, ast.Attribute)` branch at L778) DOES recognize a bare attribute source (`x = sys.argv`, chain ∈ `TAINT_SOURCES`) — so `x = sys.argv` propagated taint but `x += sys.argv` silently DROPPED it, a taint FALSE-NEGATIVE letting a later `exec(x)` / `os.system(x)` go unreported on the augmented-assign path. Mirrored the plain-path Attribute branch into `_process_augassign` (reuses `_attribute_chain` + `TAINT_SOURCES`) so the two paths agree. **FN-safe by construction** — only ADDS recognition of chains ALREADY in `TAINT_SOURCES` (the same set the plain path uses); it can never create a false taint, only close the gap. **Surfaced by an LLM-ensemble scan (mimo-v2.5), then source-verified**; the scan's two CO-flagged candidates were REJECTED after concrete verification as FALSE POSITIVES: deepseek's CRITICAL "`bytes.translate(None, delete)` is Py2-only, raises TypeError" is WRONG (`b'aXbYc'.translate(None, b'XY')` → `b'abc'` in Py3 — delete-only, no translation; mimo correctly said CLEAN), and the "MAJOR `_plugin_is_clipboard_domain(None)` AttributeError" is UNREACHABLE (every call site coerces `plugin_meta` to a dict: production `Context.plugin_meta` is `default_factory=dict`, bench `_build_context` L223 does `... or {}`; deepseek correctly said CLEAN). +3 two-sided tests (`TestAugAssignAttributeSource` — positive `cmd += sys.argv → os.system` fires; plain==aug consistency; benign `config.value` attribute no-FP); taint suite 49 + 91 taint-consumers green. **`refactor(deadcode)` (ec1f0355):** removed 5 legacy/back-compat dead funcs + 1 dead alias const + 1 orphaned import across `_skillaudit_python_context.py` (`_container_is_all_literals`, `_is_inside_string_literal`, `_container_is_all_safe`) and `cpv_skillaudit_native.py` (`_normalize_unicode_for_test`, `_should_suppress`, the `_INTENT_CLASS_RULES` "Backwards-compat alias" const, `import unicodedata`) — all self-describing as legacy/back-compat with ZERO real refs (full dead-symbol closure sweep + bare-name cross-check across `scripts/`+`tests/`; the no-ghosts rule). Rigor KEPT 2 false-deads: `abs_path_const_is_inert_py_data` (live via the `_inert_check=` alias in `cpv_validation_common.py`) and `_INTENT_HARD/SOFT_SIGNAL_RULES` (used independently). **`feat(spec+robustness)` (9e3aa50a, prior window):** CC v2.1.217-218 spec-sync (`emojiCompletionEnabled` setting; `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS` + `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH` env vars; `background` skill frontmatter key; frontmatter-bool widening) + 8 ruff-bugbear robustness fixes (B904/B905/B034/B033/B023) + 2 experimental agent generators (`create_mono_agent.py` / `create_micro_agents_workflow.py` + skills). NOT a security / `--strict` change EXCEPT the taint fix, which strictly STRENGTHENS detection (FN-safe, no gate relaxed, nothing suppressed). Full suite 11081+ passed; cold-cache strict self-validate 0/0/0/0; CI+Release+Marketplace all green. version 3.2.0, scripts 126, test files 412. **v3.1.0 — issue batch: cross-platform + cspell-JSON FP reductions, a branch-aware pre-push, and the injection-guardrail FP ruled by-design (user: "check your own repo for new issues", "fix all before publishing (except 7)", "plugins must be cross platform and not all platforms have the same temp folders")**
— ships the previously-committed-but-unshipped v3.0.1 re2 stderr fix (`d44c8b47`, documented below) together with four issue fixes; a MINOR because #169 adds new pre-push capability. **#172 (cross-platform temp paths):** `# nosec B108` on the three temp-path allowlist DATA constants (`/tmp/`, `/var/tmp/`; bandit flags the string literals, the code never creates a temp file), plus the real cross-platform fix — `ALLOWED_DOC_PATH_PREFIXES` gained `/var/folders/` (macOS `$TMPDIR`), the ONE temp form that genuinely FP'd. VERIFIED the pre-compaction hypothesis was WRONG: Windows temp (`C:\Windows\Temp`, `%TEMP%`), `/private/*`, and Linux `/tmp` are NOT matched by `ABSOLUTE_PATH_PATTERNS` at all (pattern-3 roots = `usr|opt|etc|var|bin|sbin|lib|root`; pattern-2 = `C:\Users` home-only), so they never FP and need no entry — only macOS `/var/folders/…` (via the `/var` root) fired MAJOR in a doc while the Linux equivalents were silent. Two-sided (`tests/test_issue_172_macos_temp_dir.py`, 6): the macOS temp path clears while a non-temp `/var` system path and a `/Users/` home leak still fire, and hardcoding `/var/folders/` in CODE still fires (the allowlist is doc-only). **#171 (cspell.json `words` → TOOL_SHADOW):** `standardize --fix` writes a `.cspell.json` whose `words` array holds real tokens (`monkeypatch`), which `agent_manipulation TOOL_SHADOW` — the fixer breaking its own gate — flagged MAJOR. New `_skillaudit_json_context.is_cspell_json_words_entry` clears the `_BINARY_INAPPLICABLE` family (TOOL_SHADOW + the injection/manipulation/intent rules) ONLY on a string ELEMENT of a cSpell word-list array (`words`/`ignoreWords`/`flagWords`/`userWords`, incl. `overrides[].<>`) of a recognised cSpell config basename — the structured-JSON sibling of the `.txt`/`.dict` `_is_cspell_dictionary` carve-out. FN-safe: basename-gated + array-scoped, and `_BINARY_INAPPLICABLE` excludes every exec/secret/exfil/decode rule, so a real key hidden as a "word" still fires. Two-sided at unit + dispatch + E2E (`tests/test_issue_171_cspell_json_words_fp.py`, 22): the `.cspell.json` clears while a non-cSpell `config.json` with identical content and a real `.js` payload both still fire. **#169 (pre-push forbade ALL branch sharing):** the `publish.py`-generated `git-hooks/pre-push` refused every push not descended from `publish.py`, and `publish.py` refuses off the default branch — so a feature-branch push was impossible, silently stalling multi-agent fleets. Both the generated template (`generate_plugin_repo.py::gen_pre_push_hook`) and CPV's own dogfooded hook now classify refs: a push to the default branch (main/master/origin-HEAD) or a tag STILL requires `publish.py` ancestry (release gate unchanged → `publish.py --minor` still works), while a feature-branch-only push is allowed AFTER a passing trufflehog secret scan (fail-closed if trufflehog is absent; a mixed push containing a release ref falls back to the ancestry gate, so you cannot smuggle a release ref past it). Two-sided (`tests/test_issue_169_prepush_branch_aware.py`, 17, incl. real-trufflehog E2E). **#167 / #168** (resolver-tag migration skip / RC-DEP-TAG-PIPELINE FP) were ALREADY FIXED in v2.159.0 (`0b175d6`) — the reporters were on the pre-fix v2.158.0 → verified and closed, no code. **#170 (defensive anti-injection guardrails FP as injection — PROMPT_INJECT / INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT / MCP_SCHEMA_POISON / A2A_CROSS_AGENT_INJECT):** ruled **NOT-AUTO-CLEARABLE, by-design** (same class as #67 / #70-B) after an opus adversarial analysis. On an instruction-loaded surface the quoted payload reaches the model byte-for-byte; every candidate discriminator (negation-proximity, quotes, defensive header, fenced code, or all combined) is attacker-forgeable — and CPV's own `RC-130` detector IS a negation-form injection, so "negation ⇒ suppress" would silence a real attack. CPV already made and held this decision: the audit-consent sentinel deliberately excludes the INTENT/injection family, and the only thing CPV auto-clears (v2.120 provably-inert) keys on a data-flow SHAPE a guardrail lacks. Closed with devitalize guidance for the author (describe the attack CLASS not the verbatim payload / break the literal signature / relocate examples to a doc-only file / never in an MCP description). NOT a security / `--strict`-weakening change anywhere in the batch — three FP reductions + a pre-push feature; #170 explicitly NOT suppressed. version 3.1.0, scripts 124, test files 409. **v3.0.1 — silence the google-re2 `E0000 ... Error parsing` stderr wall the v3.0.0 publish surfaced (user, mid-publish: "fix those anyway")**
— during the v3.0.0 publish the test gate printed ~8 red `E0000 ... invalid perl operator: (?!` / `invalid escape sequence: \R,\u` lines. Investigation confirmed they were NOT a false-negative: the `HybridMatcher` (`cpv_re2_matcher.py`) routes every RE2-rejected pattern to a Python-`re` fallback where it matches identically, so detection was already correct. The lines are google-re2's C++ absl logging, emitted the instant `RE2::Set::Add()` is handed one of the 8 catalog patterns using a construct re2 can't compile (lookahead, the `\R` line-break escape, real unicode escapes). The prior code caught the rejection AFTER the C++ layer had already logged — the module comment even noted the log "cannot be suppressed from Python." **FIX:** don't hand a known-incompatible pattern to `Add()` at all. A new `_pattern_is_re2_unsafe()` pre-check (mirroring the audit tool's `_PERL_ONLY` set) routes those 8 straight to the fallback, so re2 never parses them → zero stderr noise, one fewer doomed `Add()` each. **The pre-check is PRECISE, not blunt:** a naive `\\u` test over-flagged 12 re2-SAFE patterns (the INVISIBLE_TEXT / LOG_INJECTION rules whose regex matches the *literal two-char* `\u` escape text via a doubled backslash) — routing a re2-safe pattern to the backtracking engine would weaken re2's linear-time ReDoS guarantee, so the check only fires when the backslash is itself UNESCAPED (odd-length backslash run). Verified against live `re2.compile`: it now flags EXACTLY the 8 re2 rejects (0 over-, 0 under-match), `re2_compiled` rose 509→521 to match the compatibility audit's own `compatible: 521`, and a direct scan emits 0 `E0000` lines while the lookahead patterns (yaml/jwt/torch) still fire via fallback. FAIL-SAFE both directions: a misjudgement can only change WHICH engine serves a pattern, never WHAT is detected. +5 two-sided tests (`test_cpv_re2_matcher.py::TestRe2UnsafePreFilter`). NOT a security / `--strict` / detection change — pure stderr-noise elimination. version 3.0.1, scripts 124, test files 406. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-regen-hashes-last-markdown-poison]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v3.0.0 — BREAKING project-wide component rename: every agent is now `cpv-…-agent`, every command `cpv-…`, every skill `cpv-…` (user: "all commands, skills and agents must start with `cpv-`. and all agents must end with `-agent`" + "do as you think is best")**
— the CPV agents lacked a `cpv-` prefix, so a user (or a routing Claude) scanning the agent registry could not find them among the fleet's dozens of agents. The fix is a namespace sweep of CPV's OWN components, MAJOR-bumped because the public dispatch names change (`Agent(subagent_type: "plugin-fixer")` → `"cpv-plugin-fixer-agent"`, `Skill(claude-plugins-validation:fix-validation)` → `:cpv-fix-validation`). **Scope:** 50 renames — 13 agents (→ `cpv-<stem>-agent`), 1 command (`the-skills-menu-create` → `cpv-the-skills-menu-create`), 36 skills (→ `cpv-<stem>`); `cpv-doctor-agent`, `cpv-main-menu`, `cpv-main-menu-skill`, `cpv-pre-install-scan` and every `cpv-batch-*` were already compliant and untouched, and SCRIPTS keep their names (they are not user-dispatched components). **Mechanism:** a reviewed, shape-scoped deterministic perl codemod, NOT `fastedit rename-all` — the component names are hyphenated STRING identifiers (`plugin-fixer`), not AST symbols (`class`/`def`), so the AST renamer finds 0 matches (verified). The codemod matched whole tokens with hyphen-aware boundaries + a `(?<!cpv-)` guard (never double-prefixes an already-fixed `name:` field) + longest-old-first (a short name never matches inside a longer one), and is idempotent. Phase 1 `git mv`'d all 50 dirs/files; Phase 2 set each frontmatter `name:` = filename/dirname (portable awk); Phase 3 ran 48 pairs over `agents commands skills scripts tests README .claude-plugin .mcp.json` (~1018 subs / 205 files) PLUS the top-level `references/` runbook dir the first pass missed (79 subs / 5 files); config files (`.github/`, `.gitignore`, `.markdownlint*`) verified clean. The bare 3-letter `cpv` agent → `cpv-agent` was done by SHAPE only (never a bare-token replace — 88 % of bare `cpv` hits are the manifest-config key / `.cache/cpv/` / plugin slug), fixing the two real dispatch sites (`cpv-the-skills-menu/SKILL.md`, `README.md`). **Verified by the validator-as-oracle loop** (`validate_plugin --strict`): 20 CRITICAL / 61 MAJOR / 49 MINOR at the start (all rename breakage — `RC-GHOST-DISPATCH-001` ghost `subagent_type:`, "Reference to non-existent skill", broken paths/links) → **VALID 0/0/0/0**. Two residual MAJORs were content-trim consequences of the longer names, not ref bugs, and were trimmed WITHOUT capability loss: `cpv-harden-and-redact` frontmatter `description` (203 → <200 tokens) and `cpv-the-skills-menu` body (~5295 → <5000 tokens — condensed the duplicated Scripts-à-la-carte section + the longest table parentheticals; the routing `via cpv-the-skills-menu` phrase the `RE_USED_VIA_THE_SKILLS_MENU` check needs is preserved). The batch-status slug is DERIVED from the agent name (`_cms_slug_for` → `f"batch-{agent_type}-status"`), so it correctly became `batch-cpv-plugin-fixer-agent-status` — reconciled in the orchestrator test + the 3 batch-command docs that document it. NOT a security / `--strict` change — pure rename; the historical version paragraphs below KEEP the old names as they were per-version. version 3.0.0, agents 14, commands 13, skills 47. **v2.162.0 — CC spec-drift sync v2.1.212 → v2.1.216: eight allowlist-widening / FP-reduction adds, none touching a security gate or `--strict` severity (user: "update the plugin to the latest changes of claude code")**
— the changelog spanned v2.1.209→.216; CPV was last synced through v2.1.212 (v2.160.0, commit `32331f5`), so the genuinely-new deltas are .214/.215/.216 (.213 is absent from the public changelog — it survives only as the `min-version: 2.1.213` marker on EndConversation). A detection agent fetched the RAW docs (not a WebFetch summary — the method [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] records as unreliable) and mechanically set-diffed each CPV constant table; every file:line was spot-verified before editing (the `CANONICAL_TOOLS: frozenset[str] =` form had defeated a naive `\s*=` grep). **The adds:** `EndConversation` (v2.1.214 — the abusive-session/jailbreak-exit tool) into BOTH `VALID_TOOLS` (strict validity) AND `CANONICAL_TOOLS` (detection breadth — the file comment keeps the two apart, but a genuinely-current tool belongs in both); SessionStart source `fork` (v2.1.212/.214) into `SESSION_START_SOURCES` (a session begun via `/fork` reports source `fork`); and ten env vars into `VALID_PLUGIN_ENV_VARS`, each a live "unknown env var" false positive on any plugin hook/skill/mcp/lsp that references it — `CLAUDE_CODE_FORWARD_SUBAGENT_TEXT` (.211), `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION` / `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENTS_PER_SESSION` / `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS` (.212), `CLAUDE_CODE_OTEL_CONTENT_MAX_LENGTH` plus the three OTel-SDK `..._ATTRIBUTE_VALUE_LENGTH_LIMIT` variants (.214), and two gaps the prior sweep missed (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROCESS_WRAPPER` .208, `CLAUDE_AX_SCREEN_READER` .181 — its settings twin `axScreenReader` had been added, the env-var form skipped). Also synced `BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS` (the plugin-command name-collision WARNING) with the current-builtin slash commands the changelog names — `fork` / `subtask` / `background` / `btw` (.212), `usage-credits` (.211), `verify` / `code-review` (.215) — an FN-direction fix (a missed collision warning, never a false block), added only from named evidence so no plugin draws a false collision. **VERIFIED CURRENT (diffed, no change):** `sandbox.filesystem.disabled` (.216 — CPV tolerates the boolean subkey, no FP/FN), the Task-tool `mode` deprecation (.212 — CPV never validates it), memory-file frontmatter `modified` (.214 — a CC-auto-written field, not a plugin-authored surface), all 30 hook events, and .215 (runtime-only). +15 two-sided tests (`tests/test_cc_spec_sync_2_1_213_216.py`); 357 constant-touching regression tests still green; cache-cold strict self-validate VALID 0/0/0/0. NOT a security / `--strict` change — allowlist-widening only. version 2.162.0, scripts 124, test files 406. **v2.161.0 — de-fork `/cpv-main-menu`: it runs INLINE in the main session (no subagent fork), the vestigial `cpv-main-menu-agent` is deleted, and every derived reference is reconciled (user: "it should not fork anything … spawning an agent to write a json file is a waste"; "remove everything from the frontmatter except the name and description")**
— `/cpv-main-menu` "took minutes and printed no menu." TWO independent root causes, both fixed. **(1) CPV side — the wasteful fork.** The command carried `context: fork` + `agent: cpv-main-menu-agent`, forking a subagent to do ~0.04 s of mechanical work (queue a menu spec, parse a digit) that the main session does inline — and the external `claude-menu-system` Stop hook renders the menu regardless of who queued it, so the fork bought nothing but the cold-cache re-prime CPV's own **CA-07** rule flags. Frontmatter stripped to `name` + `description`; body trimmed 189→~70 lines (dropped the triplicated menu + the key→action routing table now SSOT in `menu-tree.md`; kept a concise 8-category overview for `test_menu_unification_v290`). The agent was then vestigial — its whole body just restated the inline flow, and nothing dispatches it (the `A — Ask` leaf hands off to `cpv-spark`) → **deleted; agents 15→14.** All derived refs reconciled two-sided: 14 skills + `cpv-main-menu-skill` + `skills-catalog.md` + `marketplace-fixer.md` moved off "Loaded by `cpv-main-menu-agent`" to "Loaded dynamically via the-skills-menu, reached via …" (validator-safe: `RE_USED_VIA_THE_SKILLS_MENU` needs `the-skills-menu` IMMEDIATELY after `via `, no backtick, or the MINOR fires); `test_consolidation_v211` `AGENT_COMMANDS = {}` + docstring; README agent list 15→14; this file's inventory + menu note + a LATENT-FALSE "pins opus" parenthetical in the v2.150.0 note corrected — the agent never pinned a model in FRONTMATTER (line 145's `model: opus` is a body example dispatching cpv-spark; `test_no_agent_pins_a_model` globs every agent with no exemption, so a frontmatter pin would have failed the suite since v2.102.0). No CA-04 exemption to unwind; CA-04 findings are WARNING-only, so nothing blocked `--strict`. **(2) claude-menu-system side (separate repo I maintain; landed as PR #4, UNMERGED pending review)** — the actual "5-minute silence": `menu_queue.py::session_id()` read `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` / `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID_HOOK`, NEITHER of which Claude Code exports; CC exports **`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`**, so under concurrent fleet sessions the menu was queued into a DIFFERENT session's dir and never rendered (the emit hook, which correctly reads `session_id` from its Stop-hook stdin payload, found nothing). Fixed the env tuple + hardened the transcript fallback to scope to the cwd project first; +3 two-sided tests, plugin suite 259 green. NO security / `--strict` change on either side. version 2.161.0, agents 15→14, scripts 124, test files 405. **v2.160.0 — CPV's 6th scanner: the OPT-IN, token-gated Snyk Agent Scan (Check 28) + a CC v2.1.205–212 spec-drift sweep (E1–E6 plus two reserved-name adds) — every code change here is allowlist-widening or a stale-warning removal; NOT ONE touches a security gate or `--strict` severity**
— **Snyk Agent Scan (Check 28, opt-in).** New `scripts/cpv_snyk_agent_scanner.py` (776 LOC) wired as Check 28 in `validate_security.py`: a CLOUD-BACKED LLM second opinion on a plugin's INSTRUCTION SURFACE — skills, plus agents/commands/rules/hooks STAGED as synthetic skills with findings remapped back to the real path. It is OPT-IN and token-gated on `SNYK_TOKEN` (a free Snyk account, from `https://app.snyk.io/account`) BECAUSE it sends instruction-surface content to Snyk's cloud and CPV must stay usable offline and must never ship a user's private pre-install plugin to a third party by default — absent a token it is a visible SKIPPED WARNING naming the variable and the page, NEVER folded into the pass count (a scan that never ran must never read as a scan that passed). FOUR safety invariants are the whole reason a wrapper exists rather than a raw `snyk scan` (Snyk's own `scan` can START MCP servers, which on an untrusted plugin would turn CPV's scan into the exploit): (1) it stages ONLY from a fixed script allowlist and NEVER an `.mcp.json`; (2) `--dangerously-run-mcp-servers` is never passed and `--ci` is BANNED (upstream `--ci` REQUIRES `--dangerously-run-mcp-servers`); (3) cannot-check ≠ clean — a real failure (exec-fail, staging-fail, empty/unparseable output, timeout) reports FAILED, never a benign SKIPPED; (4) ephemeral name-only staging, torn down after. The RAN/SKIPPED/FAILED status is derived from the result object's own `invoked` / `exit_code` fields, NOT sniffed from the emitted WARNING prose — the code-review "F2" status-precision fix, so a later reword can never silently mislabel a broken scan as a benign skip. It reuses CPV's full self-scan skip chain (self-scan, vendored, dev-scratch, test, gitignored). Excluded from the baseline "SET" scanner bar in `cpv_setup_auth.py` (token-gated → requiring its binary would wrongly gate 6-of-6 full-coverage on a scanner a tokenless user can never run); `manage_doctor.py` now counts scanners off the live status dict (`len(statuses)`), not a magic 6. **CC v2.1.205–212 spec-drift — 6 FP-reduction items (E1–E6) plus 2 reserved-name adds (C1/E2), all found by the raw-doc-fetch + mechanical set-diff method, NONE a security or `--strict` change:** E1 the `best` model alias → `_SHORT_MODEL_RE` in `cpv_validation_common.py` (an alias, not a base model, so deliberately NOT added to `VALID_MODELS`); E2 `first-party-plugins` + `healthcare` → `RESERVED_MARKETPLACE_NAMES`; E3 `Setup` is a CURRENT hook event → removed the stale "legacy/deprecated" WARNING in `validate_hook.py` + `validate_hook_output.py` (Setup stays in `VALID_HOOK_EVENTS`, its command/mcp_tool restriction untouched); E4 `MessageDisplay` → `EVENTS_WITHOUT_MATCHERS`; E5 `elicitation_complete` + `elicitation_response` → `COMMON_NOTIFICATION_TYPES`; E6 `overloaded` + `oauth_org_not_allowed` + `model_not_found` → `STOPFAILURE_ERRORS`; C1 `Claude Preview` + `Claude Browser` → `RESERVED_MCP_SERVER_NAMES` (exact-string match, MAJOR — a reserved server name loads as installed but produces no tools). NOT built (recommend-only, a NEW capability rather than stale-enum drift): a FileChanged/StopFailure exact-match-vs-regex matcher-charset classifier. README gained an "Opt-in scanner — Snyk Agent Scan" section documenting the token flow. Two-sided coverage: `tests/test_cc_spec_sync_2_1_205_212.py` (22 tests — each new value accepted-or-no-longer-warned PLUS a positive control that a bogus sibling still rejects), `tests/test_cpv_snyk_agent_scanner.py`, `tests/test_validate_mcp.py` (C1), `tests/test_cpv_install_scanners.py` + `tests/test_install_scanners_re2.py`. No security-catalog pattern changed → no re2-audit regen owed. Full parallel suite 10981 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed; cache-cold strict self-validate VALID 0/0/0/0. version 2.160.0, scripts 124, test files 405. **v2.159.0 — closes #167 + #168 (the `{name}--v{version}` resolver-tag, DOUBLE hyphen) + a CC v2.1.196–v2.1.209 spec sync — BACKFILLED into this snapshot (the v2.159.0 release shipped without its paragraph or an inventory bump; both are caught up in the v2.160.0 commit)**
— **#168 was a false positive keyed on the wrong thing.** The dependency-tag detector matched on the VARIABLE NAME (`dep_tag` / `dependency_tag`), but the reporter's generator named it `resolver_tag`, so a correct construct went unflagged / a renamed one mis-flagged. Replaced with an SSOT SHAPE regex `DEP_TAG_CONSTRUCT_RE` in `cpv_validation_common.py` (`--v` immediately followed by `{` / a quote / `%` / `+` — the tag-construction shape, name-independent) plus a ground-truth override: a real `{name}--v*` tag present ⇒ no warning. **#167 was a silent 4-of-13 migration.** The old release-tag-push detector was a brittle regex anchor recognising only some push shapes; replaced with an AST walk `_is_release_tag_push` covering all four (including the two-call `f"v{...}"` form), and when ≥2 anchors are present it REFUSES with an explicit CANNOT-migrate line rather than half-migrating — coverage went 4/13 → 11/13 + 2 legitimate no-ops. DECLINED #167 item-2 ("make the resolver-tag WARNING blocking"): an offline pre-publish scan cannot know whether a plugin has dependents, so blocking would fail every standalone plugin — it stays a WARNING. Also synced CC v2.1.196–v2.1.209 (commit `3e4aa2b`: `autoMode` is dead in `settings.local.json`). version 2.159.0. [[lesson-verify-issue-is-not-already-shipped]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v2.158.1 — the v2.158.0 rules get a FIX RECIPE, and the cache audit stops advertising six rules while checking seven (docs-only)**
— two doc defects, both of the same shape: the code was right and the documentation was lying, so an agent reading the docs would do the wrong thing. **(1) The v2.158.0 rules had no entry anywhere the fixer reads.** `plugin-fixer` resolves a finding by looking it up in `skills/fix-validation/references/plugin-error-index.md` and opening the fix guide it points at — so a blocking CRITICAL with no index row is a DEAD END: the agent sees the finding and has nothing to apply. Both new rules shipped that way. Added `hook-fixes.md §14` (exec form via the `args` array, or read `$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_<KEY>` in the script), `mcp-fixes.md §14` (`headersHelper` has NO exec form — the value must be read inside the helper, from the server's `env` block), `plugin-structure-fixes.md §18` (monitors — also no exec form; inline `plugin.json` hooks; and the non-blocking `[RC-USERCFG-PROJECT-SETTINGS]` advisory), plus the four routing rows. Each guide states TWO things explicitly because they are exactly the mistakes a fixer would otherwise make: **quoting/escaping is NOT a fix** (the rejection is on the SHAPE, not the value — a quoted interpolation is still rejected, and still wrong the day the value contains a quote), and **exec form is LEGAL and must never be "fixed"** (flagging it would condemn the very construct the spec prescribes as the remedy). Also documented the `{name}--v{version}` resolver-tag stage and the merge-don't-clobber semantics in BOTH copies of `pipeline-rules.md` (canonical-pipeline + standardize-plugin), stating the two merge DIRECTIONS separately — canon JSON merges canon-as-base (canon wins on a shared key, the plugin's extra keys are preserved), `.mega-linter.yml` merges plugin-as-base (the plugin's value wins, so a load-bearing suppression AND its rationale comment survive). One sentence covering both would have been false for one of them. **(2) The cache audit is CA-01..CA-07, not CA-06.** `validate_cache` has emitted CA-07 (a component declaring `context: fork` re-primes its prompt prefix from cold) for releases, but EVERY user-facing string still advertised the range as six: the CLI help, the one-line pointer `validate_plugin` prints after each scan, the menu labels, the batch-command descriptions, the skills catalog. A user reading any of them concludes CPV does not check the rule it IS checking, and never goes looking for the CA-07 finding in the report. 32 occurrences across 16 files; only the RANGE label moved (every reference to CA-06 as an INDIVIDUAL rule is untouched), and ONE occurrence was deliberately NOT flipped — `ca-rules.md` calls CA-01..CA-06 "the first six", which is CORRECT: those six come from `ussumant/cache-audit` and CA-07 is CPV's own addition, so renaming that range would have contradicted the next sentence. **CPV's own gate caught a defect in this very changeset:** the test suite failed `SUPPLY_CHAIN` on the new README, because the shell-injection example spelled out a literal pipe-to-shell string. The prose was reworded to DESCRIBE the attack instead of spelling it — the rule was not touched, not muted, not relaxed. README also re-verified against the code rather than memory: skills 46→47, tests 5700+→10,800+, marketplace layouts two→three (Layout C was already supported), linters "6 languages"→15, and a paragraph still claiming the menus use `AskUserQuestion` when they have been Stop-hook rendered since v2.90.0. Full suite 10865 passed / 0 failed; cache-cold strict self-validate VALID 0/0/0/0. version 2.158.1. **v2.158.0 — CC v2.1.207 `${user_config.*}` shell-injection rules (#166) + standardize gains a resolver-tag upgrade path and stops clobbering canon configs (#165)**
— shipped together because the fleet was blocked on them: eleven role-plugins had PAUSED their pipeline migration (`ai-maestro#44`) waiting for exactly these. **#166 (CC v2.1.207 spec-drift, 2 rules):** CC now **REJECTS `${user_config.*}` interpolated into a SHELL-FORM command** on plugin **hooks**, **monitors**, and **MCP `headersHelper`** — a shell-injection fix, because an option VALUE carrying shell metacharacters escapes its argument slot and is executed as code. A plugin shipping that shape is broken at runtime AND unsafe, hence a blocking **CRITICAL `[RC-USERCFG-SHELL-INJECT]`**, wired into all three surfaces from ONE SSOT in `validate_hook.py` (token grammar + per-surface remediation + `hook_is_exec_form()`); `validate_mcp` and `validate_plugin` IMPORT it rather than re-derive the regex — a second copy of a security grammar drifts, and a drifted copy is a false negative. Coverage gap closed on the way: plugin.json **inline** hooks had no `${user_config.*}` checking at all. **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS SHELL-FORM vs EXEC-FORM, and getting it backwards would be catastrophic:** exec form (`{"command": "node", "args": [..., "${user_config.x}"]}`) spawns the program with an argv vector that NO shell parses, so the value can never become code — it is the fix the spec PRESCRIBES. Flagging it would condemn the remedy, so it is a hard-fail condition, regression-locked by two-sided tests (`$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_<KEY>` likewise never fires). Plus a non-blocking **WARNING `[RC-USERCFG-PROJECT-SETTINGS]`**: plugin option values (`pluginConfigs`) are no longer read from project-level `.claude/settings.json` (user / `--settings` / managed only). **The docs half is the part that actually prevents recurrence** (the v2.137.1 lesson: canon references PRESCRIBED an inverted env var, so every upgrade RE-INTRODUCED the bug the generator had just been fixed for) — 5 skill references were still TEACHING the rejected shape, incl. a literal monitor `"command": ".../poll-deploy.sh ${user_config.api_endpoint}"` shown as the recommended form; all corrected WITHOUT over-correcting (the still-legal uses — MCP/LSP configs, exec-form `args`, an MCP server's `env` block — stay standing, because telling authors to abandon `${user_config.*}` everywhere would be its own misinformation). Also fixed a self-contradictory hook example that was wrong TWICE: it interpolated `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_API_TOKEN}` into a shell string, which (a) does not even work — sensitive keys are omitted from env-var expansion, so the header expanded to a bearer prefix with nothing after it — and (b) puts the secret in the process argv where any `ps` reads it, which is precisely what CPV's own `TOKEN_STEAL` rule fires on. The rule was right; the doc was wrong. **#165 — B3 (the fleet-blocking one):** since CC 2.1.110 a version-constrained dependency resolves ONLY against a `{name}--v{version}` tag (DOUBLE hyphen). The GENERATOR already minted it (v2.156.0) — but `standardize` is profile-aware (#145/#140) and REFUSES to overwrite an EXISTING `publish.py`, so every plugin that already had one — **all 11 migrating plugins** — was being standardized WITHOUT ever gaining the stage, shipping releases nothing could depend on. There was no upgrade path at all. New surgical, **idempotent** `migrate_publish_py_dependency_tag()` (modelled on `repin_stale_cpv_ref`: detect → targeted edit → report) injects ONLY that stage under a **plain `--fix`** (NOT `--force-templates`, which is precisely the thing these plugins cannot safely run), derives the name from the manifest, and lands the tag in the SAME atomic push as the release tag so a release can never ship with one ref and not the other; an unrecognisable `publish.py` is left byte-identical and REPORTED (never half-migrated). Verified two-sided through the REAL validator on a genuine 83KB pre-v2.156 `publish.py` (+71/−1) and end-to-end in a real git repo (both tags created). **#165 — B4 (the catch):** the delegated agent reported this DONE, but its fix did not fix the reported bug — it built a custom-**KEY** detector, while the actual divergence is a custom **VALUE inside a key canon ALSO declares** (the author extended canon's `REPOSITORY_CHECKOV_ARGUMENTS: "--skip-check CKV2_GHA_1"` to `"...,CKV_DOCKER_2"` — a HEALTHCHECK skip, load-bearing because every Dockerfile they ship is an ephemeral run-once container). The agent flagged its own uncertainty ("I could not confirm without the real file") and then guessed in the OPTIMISTIC direction; fetching the real `.mega-linter.yml` showed its detector returns `[]` on it, i.e. `--force-templates` would have kept clobbering the exact file the issue was filed about. Root-caused and rewritten: **the correct merge needs no `ruamel.yaml` at all — the trick is the DIRECTION.** Merging canon-as-base cannot preserve comments (the agent's dead end, and why it concluded a round-trip loader was required); merging **plugin-as-base** — append only the canon keys it LACKS — preserves values, key order, and the rationale comments for free, because no line the author owns is ever rewritten. Where canon and the plugin disagree on a shared key **the plugin's value WINS and is reported**: we cannot distinguish "the author customized this" from "this is stale canon", and the asymmetry decides it — deleting a load-bearing suppression breaks their build, while missing a canon refresh merely annoys. JSON (`.markdownlint.json`, the `MD010` case) merges losslessly (no comments to lose). Verified against the REAL file: `CKV_DOCKER_2` + its 8-line rationale survive, canon-vs-canon is a byte-identical no-op (no false churn), idempotent, output still parses as YAML. The superseded custom-KEY detector was DELETED — keeping a weaker second detector invites someone to reach for the one that cannot see the real case. NOT a `--strict` relaxation anywhere: #166 strictly ADDS a blocking rule; #165 is pipeline-correctness. Full serial suite 10865 passed / 0 failed; cache-cold self-validate VALID 0/0/0/0. version 2.158.0, scripts 123, test files 401, ~10868 tests. **v2.157.2 — ROOT FIX for #162: the REPO LINT CI hang that recurred despite the v2.145.0 per-linter timeout (a recurrence of #148 with a DIFFERENT root cause)**
— last session flagged #162 as genuinely unsolved, root cause unknown. FOUND it by source-read (not reproduced — it is ~50% on cold CI, never local): the #148/v2.145.0 fix added a HARD PER-LINTER ceiling (`PLUGIN_REPO_LINT_TIMEOUT`, 60-180s) that bounds each `_run_linter` spawn but **NEVER their SUM**. The 17 call-site ceilings sum to **≈2040s ≈ 34 min** > the generated CI validate job's own **30-min `timeout-minutes`**, and the template never set `PLUGIN_REPO_LINT_TIMEOUT`, so each linter used its full default. On a cold runner uv/npm **serialize the concurrent `uvx`/`npx` first-run fetches on a global cache lock**, so the `max_workers=8` parallel fan-out in `lint_repo` degrades toward serial and the phase marches to ~27-34 min, past the job timeout → GitHub SIGKILLs the job → the orphaned `uv`/`python` children #162 saw. This is NEITHER "the per-linter timeout not covering the hang path" (it DOES — verified every one of the 17 `_run_linter` calls passes `timeout=`, resolution uses `shutil.which`, `smart_exec.get_version` is `timeout=10`, and the only unbounded `subprocess.run` is `smart_exec:616` in `main()`, off the lint path) NOR "upstream of the linter spawn" — it is the **MISSING AGGREGATE bound**, provable from the arithmetic (34 min > 30 min) independent of the uv-lock mechanism (which only explains the intermittency). FIX (`cpv_lint_engine.py`): a new `_phase_timeout()` (env `PLUGIN_REPO_LINT_PHASE_TIMEOUT`, default **600s** — ~17x the ~35s warm run and ~4x a plausibly-slow-but-healthy cold run, yet well under the 25-30 min job ceiling, so it never false-skips a healthy run but always fires before the job timeout on a cold linter storm; non-positive/blank/garbage → default, mirroring `_effective_timeout` so a typo cannot disable the guard). The parallel path uses `executor.map(timeout=phase_budget)` on a MANUAL executor lifecycle (NOT a `with` block — its `__exit__` calls `shutdown(wait=True)`, which would re-block on the very in-flight linters we are escaping) + `shutdown(wait=False, cancel_futures=True)` on `FuturesTimeoutError`; the serial path checks `time.monotonic()` before each language (`idx > 0` guarantees ≥1 language always runs). On exhaustion the remaining languages are a **non-blocking WARNING** naming them (the same degrade as a per-linter timeout / `PLUGIN_SKIP_REPO_LINT`; Mega-Linter stays the authoritative CI lint) — `all_passed` reflects only completed languages, so a budget-forced skip never flips `--strict` to fail. The generated CI template (`generate_plugin_repo.py`, both the ci.yml Validate step AND the release.yml validate step) now sets `PLUGIN_REPO_LINT_PHASE_TIMEOUT: "600"` so downstream CI is bounded out of the box (belt-and-braces with the job `timeout-minutes`); actionlint exit 0 on both. +12 two-sided tests (`tests/test_issue_162_repo_lint_phase_budget.py`): budget TRIPS (parallel partial-completion, parallel all-skipped, serial partial, serial tiny-budget-still-runs-≥1) AND does NOT trip (parallel fast, serial fast, default-600 fast); env parsing (default/positive/0/neg/garbage); message content; a static AST guard that the parallel `executor.map` keeps its `timeout=`. NOT a security/`--strict` change — pure reliability + a non-blocking degrade; every skip is visible. NO catalog change → no re2-audit regen. version 2.157.2, scripts 123, test files 399, ~10799 tests. [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v2.157.0 — ROOT FIX for "the publishing agents always fail CI" (user: "not a single time … was the result a success … you must improve their skills")**
— **the premise was FALSE and measuring it first is what saved the session.** Run-ID forensics over **21 repos / 235 runs** (`reports/ci-failure-forensics/`): **89.4 % green overall, 93 % on the `push` path the agents actually build, 19/20 repos green on their LATEST default-branch run, 21/21 with ≥1 green canonical-pipeline run — and ZERO failures attributable to an agent skipping a step.** Rewriting the agent prompts would have fixed **nothing**. The *perception* was real, though: failures cluster on `pull_request` events, so every Actions tab shows red at the top while the release path is green. **The defect was in CPV's own TEMPLATE, and 5 CPV-owned bugs accounted for 15 of the 18 failures:** **(RC-1)** the emitted `commitlint` job had **no Dependabot exemption** and no repo ships a `.commitlintrc.json`, so `@commitlint/config-conventional`'s `body-max-line-length: 100` fails Dependabot's machine-generated body — **every bot PR on every pipeline plugin, forever, with no plugin change** (the single largest red signal). Now ships a canonical `.commitlintrc.json`; a **human's** badly-typed commit still fails (RC-5 proved that gate correct — not weakened). **(RC-3)** Mega-Linter ships SPELL enabled but the template shipped **no `.cspell.json`**, so CI hard-errors on the plugin's own proper nouns — and `_gate_cspell` **SKIPPED** exactly when no dictionary existed, i.e. precisely when CI was guaranteed to fail: **local GREEN, CI RED**, a textbook parity hole. Both ends closed: `standardize_plugin.py` + `generate_plugin_repo.py` now emit `.cspell.json` from ONE SSOT (`_CSPELL_BASE_WORDS` — the generator imports it; a second word list would drift by construction), and the gate FAILs instead of skipping. Proven two-sided **with the real cspell**: pristine scaffold → exit 0 (`Issues found: 0`), inject `sentance`/`mispeled`/`wrod` → exit 1 on all three ⇒ a **dictionary, not a mute button**. **(RC-6)** the absolute-path detector flagged `/usr/bin` **inside a YAML comment** as a blocking MINOR (exit 3) — a code *comment* blocked a publish. **(RC-8)** the template's error handler printed "CRITICAL/MAJOR/MINOR/NIT found" on ANY non-zero exit (a network flake read as a validation failure) **and was FAIL-OPEN: exit ≥5 silently GREENED CI**. **(RC-9)** a sharded `pytest --splits/--group` matrix without `pytest-split` in the dev extra. **THE STRUCTURAL FIX — `publish.py` never invoked the CI-parity preflight at all** (`grep ci_preflight scripts/publish.py` was **empty**): `cpv_ci_preflight.py` existed, mirrored CI's Lint job, and was "enforced" only by PROSE in `agents/plugin-fixer.md` + `plugin-creator.md` — which an agent can simply skip, sail through all 14 gates, push, tag, cut a release, and only THEN go red. It is now **Gate 3b** in BOTH CPV's `publish.py` (direct import) and the emitted template (via `uvx cpv-remote-validate ci-preflight .`, so a scaffold needs no vendored module), running BEFORE the bump/commit/tag/push so a failure can never strand a half-published repo. A **missing tool still only degrades to WARNING** — it cannot false-block a box without actionlint. New **CIP-7** (commitlint w/o bot exemption) + **CIP-8** (sharded matrix w/o `pytest-split`), both **WARNING** — migration nudges, never a gate CC does not have. **CIP-1 now MIGRATES** (strips the inverted `CLAUDE_PRIVATE_USERNAMES` that made CPV flag every public `github.com/Emasoft/…` URL as a private-path leak → 22 false CRITICALs) instead of only warning. **Review + hermeticity fixes found by NOT trusting the agents' self-reports:** `_SYSTEM_BINARY_PREFIXES` entries all end in `/`, so a **bare `/usr/bin` was a MINOR FP in EVERY context** (value line, `.py` comment) and RC-6 only *masked* it in config comments — fixed one level down with a **derived** `_SYSTEM_BINARY_DIRS` (kept narrow: `/etc/passwd` still fires); **4 non-hermetic tests** silently depended on the developer's `~/.gitignore_global` (green in CI, red only on a dev box) → fixtures now pin `core.excludesFile=os.devnull`; and `test_bump_version`'s **hand-listed** copy set broke the moment `publish.py` gained an import → now copies the whole `scripts/` tree, so it cannot drift again. **A vacuous-test near-miss, caught by its own positive control:** my new test helper read `severity` when the field is `level`, so `_blocking()` returned `[]` and every FP-clear assertion passed **while proving nothing** — the controls failed loudly; a guard-the-guard test now pins the helper. version 2.157.0, scripts 123, test files 398, ~10787 tests. [[lesson-cannot-check-is-not-clean]] [[lesson-suppression-test-needs-positive-control]] [[lesson-fix-the-generator-not-the-instance]] [[claim-verification]]

**v2.156.0 — ROOT FIX for the un-dependable-plugin defect (closes #163; user: "make sure those issues won't happen again. fix the problems at the root.")**
— since CC **2.1.110** a version-constrained dependency (`{"name": "x", "version": ">=1.2"}`) resolves ONLY against `{name}--v{version}` git tags; the plain `vX.Y.Z` tag is **IGNORED**. A plugin publishing only `vX.Y.Z` therefore **cannot be depended upon** — every dependent fails to install with `no-matching-tag` and is **DISABLED**. It is invisible from the depending side (an already-installed dependent keeps working), which is how it broke `claude-plugins-validation` ← `claude-menu-system` for months with every local check green. NOTE the separator is a **DOUBLE** hyphen; the single-hyphen `{name}-v1.2.3` form (seen on real ecosystem tags) matches nothing. **THREE root causes, all fixed — the symptom was one missing tag, the root was that nothing created it and nothing noticed:** **(1) CPV's CANONICAL TEMPLATE never emitted it** (`generate_plugin_repo.py::gen_publish_py`) → every plugin CPV scaffolds/migrates inherited the bug. Now derives `{name}--v{ver}` from the manifest (never hardcoded, so a rename cannot desync it) and pushes BOTH refs in the SAME `--atomic` push, so a release can never ship with one and not the other; an unreadable name WARNS loudly instead of silently skipping (a silent skip is exactly how this hid). Verified by EXECUTING the emitted code, not grepping it. **(2) CPV COULDN'T SEE IT** — a plugin validator that could not tell you your plugin was un-dependable. New `check_dependency_resolution_tags` (`validate_plugin.py`, **WARNING / non-blocking**) with two offline signals: `RC-DEP-TAG-PIPELINE` (ships a canonical `publish.py` that tags `v{ver}` but never `{name}--v{ver}` — works on an UNINSTALLED, tag-less source per [[feedback-cpv-scans-uninstalled-plugins]]) and `RC-DEP-TAG-MISSING` (repo has release tags but zero `{name}--v*`; names the single-hyphen near-miss explicitly). WARN-only **on purpose**: the docs do not require the tag for a plugin nobody depends on, and CPV must not invent a publish gate CC does not have ([[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] cuts both ways — see v2.154.1) — but silence was not an option either. **(3) CPV ITSELF was un-dependable** — the detector's first act was to flag CPV: **324 release tags, ZERO `claude-plugins-validation--v*`**. Fixed CPV's own `scripts/publish.py` the same way (dogfooded, not exempted). **Also fixed upstream in `claude-menu-system` (I am its maintainer):** backfilled `claude-menu-system--v0.2.0` via `claude plugin tag --push` (which independently CONFIRMED the exact tag name — I did not guess it), fixed its `publish.py` identically, and shipped **v0.2.1**, which auto-tagged itself — proving the fix end-to-end. Also fixed a live blocker there: its `uv.lock` recorded 0.1.6 while shipping 0.2.0, so the next `uv run` re-locked it, dirtied the tree, and aborted the publish at the clean-tree gate (the same class as #149) — the bump stage now re-locks. **A latent crash caught by the tests, not by luck:** the new WARNINGs passed a `suggestion=` kwarg that `report.warning()` does not accept — it would have `TypeError`d the first time a finding actually fired; the firing-path tests caught it. +12 two-sided tests (`tests/test_dependency_resolution_tags.py`), incl. a loop-closing test that CPV's OWN generated pipeline passes CPV's OWN detector. NO catalog change → no re2-audit regen. version 2.156.0, scripts 123, test files 392, ~10591 tests. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-suppression-test-needs-positive-control]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v2.155.0 — CC spec-sync round 2 (user: "there are other important changes in claude code that you missed! please be more accurate!"): 2 REAL publish-blocking FPs my first `relevance`-only audit missed**
— the v2.154.0 audit found only `relevance` and declared the rest clean. That was WRONG, and the miss was METHODOLOGICAL: I leaned on WebFetch SUMMARIES of the docs, which is precisely the method [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] already records as unreliable (it previously returned a false-negative "NO PLUGIN-SPEC CHANGES" while the raw changelog held ~8 deltas). Redone the RELIABLE way — `curl` the RAW `.md` docs, grep every `min-version` marker, and mechanically DIFF each authoritative field/enum table against CPV's constants — which surfaced two genuine FPs, both of which BLOCK a publish: **(A) `VALID_TOOLS` was missing `ReportFindings` (tools-reference, v2.1.196) and `SendUserFile`** — both documented tools, both entirely absent from CPV, so any agent declaring one in `tools:` was flagged as referencing an unknown tool. **(B) `validate_plugin.known_dirs` was missing `themes`** — a DEFAULT component directory in the plugins-reference "Directory | Purpose" table ("Color theme definitions"), a peer of `skills/` `agents/` `commands/` `output-styles/` `bin/` — so a plugin shipping `themes/` drew a publish-blocking **MAJOR `RC-NONSTD-DIR-001`**. Both fixes are pure allowlist-widening (FP-reduction, FN-safe; **no rule suppressed, `--strict` not relaxed**). Also resolved a STALE comment that claimed `PushNotification` was "not enumerated in tools-reference … remains unverified" — re-checked against the live table: it IS there. **VERIFIED-CLEAN (diffed, no change needed):** agent frontmatter (all 16 documented fields incl. `isolation`/`effort` already present), skill/command frontmatter (CPV is a superset), the 30 hook events, `VALID_EFFORT_VALUES` (`max` already present), `VALID_MODELS`, and the marketplace top-level `renames`. `channels` was checked and correctly NOT added — it is a manifest ARRAY, not a directory (verified rather than assumed). +8 two-sided tests (`tests/test_cc_spec_sync_tools_and_themes.py`), each "now accepted" assertion paired with a POSITIVE CONTROL proving the same code path still rejects a bogus tool / a genuinely unknown dir ([[lesson-suppression-test-needs-positive-control]]). NO catalog change → no re2-audit regen. Full serial suite 10579 passed / 0 failed; cache-cold self-validate 0/0/0/0. version 2.155.0, scripts 123, test files 391, ~10579 tests. [[claim-verification]] [[cc-spec-drift-check-method]] [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v2.154.1 — self-review of the v2.154.0 `relevance` validator: stop BLOCKING a publish for something Claude Code accepts (a self-inflicted FP), + 3 correctness/consistency fixes**
— an `xhigh` code review of my own v2.154.0 diff caught a real over-block I had shipped one release earlier. **(A) THE BUG: `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-LIMIT` was emitted at MINOR, and a MINOR BLOCKS `--strict`.** But the CC docs ENUMERATE what `claude plugin validate` rejects — unknown keys (warning), a non-object `relevance`, and a `hosts` entry with a scheme/port/path — and a documented per-signal MAXIMUM is **NOT** among them, so Claude Code loads a marketplace that overruns one. CPV was therefore inventing a publish gate the spec does not have: a marketplace with 11 `cli` entries would be hard-blocked by CPV while CC accepts it — **exactly the false-positive class CPV exists to eliminate** ([[feedback-cpv-scans-uninstalled-plugins]]-adjacent: never block legitimate content). FIX: all 4 LIMIT sites → **WARNING** (still visible, never blocking; same call as the WARN-only `RC-TEST-COVERAGE`), with the rationale written into the module header AND pinned by tests so nobody "tightens" it back into an over-block. This is NOT relaxing a security gate — `relevance` is a non-security spec-conformance rule, and [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] governs security rules; here the over-strict severity WAS the defect. **(B)** the missing/empty-`signals` advisories reused the `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-UNKNOWN` code although they are not about an unknown key — split into their own `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-NO-SIGNALS` (conflated codes cannot be filtered or triaged apart), via one `_relevance_not_suggestible()` helper that also collapses two copy-pasted WARNING blocks. **(C)** a `signals` object carrying ONLY unknown keys was silently inert — every unknown key warned individually, but nothing said the block as a whole can never suggest anything; now a `known_signal_count == 0` check covers empty AND only-unknown alike. (Deliberately kept `"signals" not in relevance` rather than `.get()`: an explicit `"signals": null` must still reach the isinstance check and raise MAJOR, which `.get()` would silently downgrade to a WARNING.) **(D)** unknown keys INSIDE a `manifestDeps` entry now warn, consistent with every other level. **(E) test-harness fix:** `_codes()` matched messages against a HARDCODED list of known RC codes, so any new code silently yielded `[]` — which would make a `_codes(...) == []` assertion pass VACUOUSLY ([[lesson-suppression-test-needs-positive-control]], the same class of harness bug, found twice in one day); replaced with a regex so the harness cannot go stale relative to the code it tests. +2 tests (32 total in `test_relevance_block.py`). NO catalog change → no re2-audit regen. Full serial suite 10571 passed / 0 failed; cache-cold self-validate 0/0/0/0. version 2.154.1, scripts 123, test files 390, ~10573 tests. [[claim-verification]] [[lesson-suppression-test-needs-positive-control]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v2.154.0 — clear the SHELL_EXEC prose FP on the English word "function" (closes #161) + spec-sync the marketplace `relevance` block (CC v2.1.152), which CPV was hard-blocking with a MAJOR**
— TWO independent FP fixes, both two-sided, shipped as one release. **(A) #161 skillaudit SHELL_EXEC prose FP.** The catalog SHELL_EXEC pattern `(?:\bnew\s+)?\bFunction\s*\(\s*[`'"]` fired on ordinary lowercase prose (`the helper function (\`run_all\`) is defined below`), because EVERY catalog pattern is compiled with `re.IGNORECASE` (`cpv_skillaudit_native._compiled_rules` — there is NO per-pattern flag field in the JSON), so `Function` matched the English word, `\s*\(\s*` matched " (", and the character class matched the markdown BACKTICK. In a doc the finding demotes to NIT — and a NIT blocks `--strict` — so it hard-blocked the reporter's publish. **The reporter's stated root cause was WRONG** (they believed the pattern was a bare `Function\s*\(`; it already required a quote, and their simplified repro "The real function (see below)" does NOT fire — verified through the REAL scanner); their *fix recommendation* (case-sensitivity) was nonetheless correct. FIX (detection-accuracy, FP-reduction, FN-safe, **no rule suppressed / `--strict` not relaxed**): pin the identifier CASE-SENSITIVELY with a scoped inline flag — `(?:\bnew\s+)?\b(?-i:Function)\s*\(\s*[`'"]` — which is **re2-compatible** (verified; the audit regen leaves the pattern's verdict and the 529/8 totals unchanged). The JS constructor is capitalised by definition, so no real threat is lost. Deliberately does NOT clear the AMBIGUOUS capitalised form: a sentence-initial `Function ("dispatch")` is byte-identical to the real constructor, so it KEEPS firing (fail-safe) and that boundary is now regression-locked so a future "fix" cannot widen the clear into an FN. **(B) marketplace `relevance` spec-drift.** `relevance` (a documented CC **v2.1.152+** marketplace plugin-entry field — the org plugin-suggestion signals) was absent from `OPTIONAL_PLUGIN_FIELDS`, so the strict allowlist emitted a publish-blocking **MAJOR `RC-MKPL-UNKNOWN-FIELD`** on ANY marketplace adopting it (reproduced at baseline through the real validator on the spec's own example). Now accepted AND shape-validated by a new `validate_relevance_block()` mirroring `claude plugin validate`: unknown keys under `relevance`/`relevance.signals` → **WARNING** `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-UNKNOWN` (non-blocking — the docs state they are ignored at load time so older clients keep loading the marketplace); non-object `relevance`/`signals`, a non-array signal, a bad `manifestDeps` element, a non-string `topic` → **MAJOR** `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-TYPE`; a `hosts` entry carrying a scheme/port/path → **MAJOR** `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-HOST` (the docs say CC rejects it); a documented limit overrun → **MINOR** `RC-MKPL-RELEVANCE-LIMIT`. Every limit was CENTRAL-VERIFIED against the spec, not taken from the agent's self-report (topic 64 chars; `cwd` 10×256; `cli` 10×64; `hosts` 20×128; `filesRead` 10×256; `manifestDeps` 10 entries × 256-char regex sources) — all correct. Delegated to an opus agent, then central-verified two-sided E2E through the REAL validator (spec-exact example → 0 relevance findings; stashed baseline → the MAJOR reproduces; malformed block → TYPE + HOST + 2×UNKNOWN all fire). The rest of the CC spec audit came back CLEAN (all 30 hook events present; `renames` / `defaultEnabled` / `displayName` / `allowCrossMarketplaceDependenciesOn` already known; `plugin-hints` needs no CPV change — no plugin-file schema). +40 two-sided tests (`tests/test_issue_161_function_prose_fp.py` 12, `tests/test_relevance_block.py` 28). Catalog change → re2-audit regen. version 2.154.0, scripts 123, test files 390, ~10571 tests. **v2.153.4 — clear the INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT CRITICAL FP on an HTML-comment provenance banner (closes #160, a v2.153.2 regression that blocked the ai-maestro-janitor v0.35.6 publish fleet-wide)**
— a REGRESSION from my own v2.153.2 "your" placeholder fix, root-caused to a catalog pattern, NOT a path-classification gap. SYMPTOM (reporter's A/B): v2.153.1 validated the janitor tree clean; v2.153.2 raised 8 CRITICAL `INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT` — one per shipped `rules/*.md`, all at line 1, the janitor's installed-rule banner `<!-- ai-maestro-janitor:installed-rule — copied into your rules dir … -->`. ROOT CAUSE (reproduced through the REAL scanner on the janitor's v0.35.5 rule files): catalog `INDIRECT_PROMPT_INJECT` pattern index-6 was `<!--\s*(?:INSTRUCTION|PROMPT|SYSTEM|OVERRIDE|AI|AGENT|COMMAND)` — a bare, case-insensitive alternation with NO token boundary, so `<!-- ai` matched because `ai` is the PREFIX of the plugin name `ai-maestro-janitor`. v2.153.2's change (making the word "your" case-sensitive) merely REMOVED the accidental `_has_placeholder("… your …")` suppression that had hidden this match forever — it did not create the over-match. VERIFIED the reporter's premise was slightly off: the finding fires on EVERY instruction surface (SKILL.md / agents / commands / rules — their SKILL.md just lacks this banner), so exempting `rules/**.md` would be inconsistent; the correct fix is at the pattern and fixes every surface uniformly. FIX (detection-accuracy, FP-reduction, FN-safe, **no rule suppressed / `--strict` not relaxed**, and it leaves the v2.153.2 "your" FN-fix fully intact — a separate mechanism): require a non-identifier terminator after the keyword — `<!--\s*(?:INSTRUCTION|PROMPT|SYSTEM|OVERRIDE|AI|AGENT|COMMAND)(?:[^-\w]|$)` — so a hyphenated/glued compound identifier (`ai-maestro`, `agent-config`, `system-check`) cannot match, while a genuine hidden-directive comment (a keyword followed by `:` / whitespace / `=` / `.` / `(` / any non-identifier char) still fires CRITICAL. Two-sided verified through the REAL scanner: all 8 janitor rule files now clean (0 unsuppressed), a real directive comment still fires CRITICAL on rules/SKILL.md/agents/commands, and the `<!--SYSTEM-->` empty-directive glued form (no payload = not a threat) is correctly not flagged. +28 two-sided tests (`tests/test_issue_160_html_comment_provenance_fp.py`). CATALOG change → re2-audit regen (`scripts_dev/regen_re2_compat.py`; total 529 unchanged since a pattern was MODIFIED not added, `_source_sha256` refreshed, pattern index-6 stays re2-compatible). Full serial suite green; cache-cold self-validate VALID 0/0/0/0. version 2.153.4, scripts 123, test files 388, ~10531 tests. [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] [[lesson-greedy-match-truncates-right-context]] [[lesson-review-competing-fix-with-real-scanner]] [[claim-verification]] [[maintain-project-claude-md]]

**v2.153.3 — clear the absolute-path MAJOR FP on a colon-joined system `PATH` run (closes #158, filed by the ai-maestro-janitor Claude relaying a v0.35.6 publish blocker) + harden the CI lint-parallelization timing flake**
— TWO pieces, both FN-safe / non-security, shipped as one release. **(A) #158 absolute-path FP.** `validate_no_absolute_paths` (`scripts/cpv_validation_common.py` → `scan_file_for_absolute_paths`) fired a publish-blocking MAJOR on a literal `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` quoted in a design-doc — a forensic record of the `PATH` macOS launchd handed a daemon, which has NO plugin-relative form so the rule's `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` remediation is inexpressible. ROOT CAUSE (verified through the scanner): the two existing allowlists — `ALLOWED_DOC_PATH_PREFIXES` (doc-only, line 7531) and `_SYSTEM_BINARY_PREFIXES` (all files, line 7559) — both match a `/`-terminated prefix (`/usr/bin/`), but a `PATH` element is followed by `:` not `/`, so a colon-joined system-dir run misses both and falls to MAJOR (doc file). FIX (detection-accuracy, FP-reduction, **no rule suppressed / `--strict` not relaxed**): new `_SYSTEM_PATH_ROOTS` (the FHS roots `/usr /bin /sbin /etc /tmp /var /opt /lib /lib64 /dev /proc /sys /run /snap /boot`, deliberately EXCLUDING `/root` `/home` `/Users`) + `_segment_is_system_path` + `_is_system_path_run`, wired into the existing INFO-downgrade branch: a colon-joined run of ≥2 non-empty segments where EVERY segment is a system dir downgrades to INFO (visible, non-counting), exactly as a single `/usr/bin/...` path already did. FN-safe by construction — a run mixing in a `/Users`, `/home` or `/root` segment (the real dev/home leak the rule guards) has a non-system segment → not cleared → still fires (and that segment also fires the separate home-directory-path pattern); an empty segment (implicit `.` CWD element) is never cleared. **Rejected** the reporter's option-4 (an inline `<!-- cpv:allow -->` suppression comment) — a self-declared rule-mute is exactly what [[feedback-never-suppress-never-relax-gate]] forbids; the intrinsic system-prefix recognition needs no suppression. +20 two-sided tests (`tests/test_issue_158_system_path_run.py` — PATH run clears in .md/.sh; /Users, /home, mixed-run-with-/Users, /root-mixed runs still fire). Verified the two failing "leak-still-fires" fixtures were my own dot-containing test bugs (the validator's pre-existing line-7519 regex-char guard skips any match with `.`), not FN holes ([[lesson-greedy-match-truncates-right-context]]). **(B) CI timing flake.** `tests/test_lint_parallelization.py::test_lint_repo_wall_time_is_slowest_linter_not_sum` asserted `elapsed < serial_lower_bound` (1.2s, ZERO slack — contradicting its own "5x slack" docstring), which flaked to 2.73s on the 4-shard CI matrix where oversubscription pushed the parallel path's overhead above the serial floor and the two distributions overlapped (the SOLE red on the v2.153.2 CI). Reworked to a RELATIVE same-run baseline: time a forced-serial run (via the engine's `CPV_LINT_PARALLEL=0` escape hatch) AND a parallel run (each with its own isolated cache dir + a dedicated `repo/` subtree so neither is a cache-hit and the caches don't pollute detection), then assert parallel is at least one `sleep_s` faster than serial. Contention-independent — both runs pay identical cache/detection/merge overhead (inflated equally under load), only the ≥2×sleep critical-path overlap differs, and `time.sleep` needs no CPU so the overlap survives a starved scheduler; a serial regression makes the two runs ~equal and trips it. Verified 6/6 clean + 3/3 under real xdist 4-worker contention. NOT a security/`--strict` change — FP-reduction + test-robustness only. NO catalog change → no re2-audit regen. version 2.153.3, scripts 123, test files 387, ~10503 tests.
